L58: ## 1 Executive Summary
L59: ## 1.1. Product Introduction
L60: Inavolisib (Itovebi) is a small molecule kinase inhibitor of the phosphatidylinositol 3-kinase (PI3K) with inhibitory activity predominantly against PI3K\alpha. Inavolisib is a new molecular entity. It does not have an approval for any indication worldwide. Itovebi film-coated tablets are supplied for oral administration with two strengths that contain 3 mg and 9 mg of inavolisib.
L61: The Applicant proposed the following indication for NDA 219249:
L62: (b) (4)
L63: The recommended indication for regular approval is:
L64: *Inavolisib, in combination with palbociclib and fulvestrant, is indicated for the treatment of adults with endocrine-resistant, PIK3CA-mutated, hormone receptor (HR)-positive, human epidermal growth factor receptor 2 (HER2)-negative, locally advanced or metastatic breast cancer, as detected by an FDA-approved test, following recurrence on or after completing adjuvant endocrine therapy.*
L65: The recommended dose for inavolisib is 9 mg taken orally once daily in combination with palbociclib and fulvestrant until disease progression or unacceptable toxicity. The recommended dosage of palbociclib is 125 mg taken orally once daily for 21 consecutive days followed by 7 days off treatment to comprise a cycle of 28 days. The recommended dosage of fulvestrant is 500 mg administered intramuscularly on Days 1, 15, and 29, and once monthly thereafter.
L66: For premenopausal and perimenopausal women, a luteinizing hormone releasing hormone (LHRH) agonist should be administered in accordance with local clinical practice.
L67: For men, administration of an LHRH agonist should be considered in accordance with local clinical practice.
L68: ## 1.2. Conclusions on the Substantial Evidence of Effectiveness
L69: The review team recommends granting regular approval for inavolisib, in combination with palbociclib and fulvestrant, for the treatment of adults with endocrine-resistant, PIK3CA-mutated, HR-positive, HER2-negative, locally advanced or metastatic breast cancer, as detected by an FDA-approved test, following recurrence on or after completing adjuvant endocrine therapy in accordance with 21 Code of Federal Regulations (CFR) 314.126(a)(b).
L70: The basis for recommendation for regular approval of inavolisib is the efficacy and safety results obtained from INAVO120, an ongoing, randomized, placebo-controlled, double-blind, multicenter trial in 325 patients with endocrine resistant, PIK3CA-mutated, HR-positive, HER2-negative (defined as IHC 0 or 1+, or IHC 2+/ISH-), advanced or metastatic breast cancer, following recurrence on or within 12 months of adjuvant endocrine therapy and who have not received prior systemic therapy for locally advanced or metastatic disease. Patients were randomized 1:1 to receive either inavolisib 9 mg or placebo orally once daily, in combination with palbociclib 125 mg orally once daily for 21 consecutive days followed by 7 days off treatment, and fulvestrant 500 mg administered intramuscularly on Cycle 1, Days 1 and 15, and then on Day 1 of every 28-day cycle. The primary efficacy endpoint was PFS as assessed by investigator. Key secondary endpoints included OS and ORR. DOR was a secondary endpoint.
L71: Presence of a PIK3CA mutation was determined through testing of blood plasma-derived ctDNA with a next-generation sequencing (NGS) assay performed at a designated central laboratory, or through local testing of ctDNA or tumor tissue with an appropriately validated NGS or PCR assay. Randomization was stratified by presence of visceral disease (yes or no), endocrine resistance (primary or secondary), and geographic region (North America/Western Europe, Asia, other). Primary endocrine resistance in the trial was defined as relapse while on the first 2 years of adjuvant endocrine therapy (ET) and secondary endocrine resistance was defined as relapse while on adjuvant ET after at least 2 years or relapse within 12 months of completing adjuvant ET.
L72: INAVO120 demonstrated a statistically significant and clinically meaningful improvement in the primary endpoint of PFS based on investigator assessment. The median PFS was 15 months (95% CI 11.3, 20.5) vs. 7.3 months (95% CI: 5.6, 9.3) favoring inavolisib + palbociclib + fulvestrant (HR 0.43, 95% CI: 0.32, 0.59; p-value &lt;0.0001). PFS by blinded independent central review (BICR) was prospectively planned and was supportive of the primary endpoint. At the planned interim analysis for OS, a total of 97 events had occurred (63% of planned OS events) and did not reach statistical significance based on the interim analysis stopping boundary. Interim OS results showed a HR of 0.64 (95% CI: 0.43, 0.97) with median OS not estimable (95% CI: 27.3, NE) on the inavolisib + palbociclib + fulvestrant arm and 31.1 months (95% CI: 22.3, NE) on the placebo + palbociclib + fulvestrant arm. Thus, there appears to be no detriment in OS at this time. The final analysis of OS is expected in the second quarter of 2025 and a postmarketing commitment (PMC) has been issued for the final OS.
L73: Results from secondary endpoints of ORR, and DOR appeared to be supportive of the primary endpoint results. However, since the interim analysis of OS at the time of PFS analysis was not statistically significant, none of the remaining secondary endpoints in the pre-specified testing hierarchy were formally tested at this time. The ORR was 58% (95% CI: 50-66%) for the inavolisib + palbociclib + fulvestrant arm vs. 25% (95% CI: 19-32%) for the placebo + palbociclib + fulvestrant arm and median DOR was 18.4 months (95% CI: 10.4-22.2) vs. 9.6 months (7.4, 16.6), respectively.
L74: (b) (4)
L75: Data from a phase 1 trial, GO39374, was submitted by the Applicant
L76: GO39374 is an ongoing, open-label, multicenter, trial designed to evaluate the safety, tolerability, and PK of inavolisib administered orally as a single agent in patients with locally advanced or metastatic PIK3CA-mutated solid tumors, including breast cancer, and in combination with standard-of-care endocrine and/or targeted therapies in patients with PIK3CA-mutated locally advanced and metastatic breast cancer. The Applicant submitted data from 3 cohorts. Arm B (N=33; inavolisib in combination with palbociclib and letrozole), Arm E (N=20; inavolisib in combination with palbociclib and fulvestrant), and Arm F (N=21; inavolisib in combination with palbociclib, fulvestrant, and metformin). Arms B, E and F did not require that patients have endocrine resistant breast cancer. The confirmed ORR results in patients with measurable disease in Arm B (N=25), Arm E (N=15), and Arm F (N=18) were 52% (95% CI: 31%, 72%), 40% (95% CI: 16%, 68%), and 17% (95% CI: 0%, 41%), respectively. While activity was observed across the three treatment arms based on the confirmed ORR and DOR, given the small sample sizes and wide CI of ORR, the contribution of inavolisib to efficacy was unclear in these single arm cohorts.
L77: (b) (4)
L78: Patients treated with inavolisib + palbociclib + fulvestrant experienced more toxicities than those treated with palbociclib + fulvestrant. The most common (\ge 20\%) adverse reactions, including laboratory abnormalities, were decreased neutrophils, decreased hemoglobin, increased fasting glucose, decreased platelets, decreased lymphocytes, stomatitis, diarrhea, decreased calcium, fatigue, decreased potassium, increased creatinine, increased ALT, nausea, decreased sodium, decreased magnesium, rash, decreased appetite, COVID-19 infection, and headache. A higher proportion of patients experienced serious adverse events (SAEs) in the inavolisib arm (24.1% vs. 10.5%), Grade \ge 3 adverse events (86% vs. 82%), and dose discontinuation (6% vs. 1%). Fatal adverse reactions occurred in 3.7% of patients receiving inavolisib. Hyperglycemia, stomatitis, and diarrhea are included as Warnings and Precautions, as these were considered severe, and occurred in a significant proportion of patients as noted in labeling.
L79: In addition, the Applicant further explored safety and tolerability through assessment of patient-generated data. A list of expected symptomatic adverse events from the combination of inavolisib, palbociclib, and fulvestrant (diarrhea, nausea, vomiting, fatigue, mouth sores, decreased appetite, and rash), was selected from the patient reported outcome (PRO)-CTCAE item library, along with an additional single item assessing bother due to side-effects of treatment. These PRO data were assessed appropriately with acceptable data quality. Analysis of patient-reported symptoms that worsened from baseline were generally observed to be worse in the inavolisib arm, with the greatest difference observed for diarrhea, mouth sores, decreased appetite, and rash. The patient-reported data provide additional insight into the safety and tolerability of the approved regimen and were included in section 6 of the USPI to further inform patients and providers.
L80: Overall, results from INAVO120 showed a statistically significant and clinically meaningful improvement for the primary endpoint of PFS when inavolisib was added to palbociclib and fulvestrant for the population enrolled. Results for secondary endpoints of ORR and OS were supportive of the primary endpoint. The addition of inavolisib to palbociclib and fulvestrant resulted in increased toxicity that was appropriately characterized by both clinician- and patient-reported assessment. Overall, the safety profile was acceptable for a patient population with a serious and life-threatening condition and is manageable with appropriate monitoring, dose modifications, and/or supportive care as described in the labeling.
L81: Therefore, the review team recommends granting regular approval to inavolisib + palbociclib + fulvestrant for endocrine-resistant, PIK3CA-mutated, HR-positive, HER2-negative, locally advanced or metastatic breast cancer. This represents a new therapeutic option in combination with palbociclib + fulvestrant for an endocrine resistant population. The indication for approval is:
L82: *Inavolisib (ITOVEBI), in combination with palbociclib and fulvestrant, is indicated for the treatment of adults with endocrine-resistant, PIK3CA-mutated, hormone receptor (HR)-positive, human epidermal growth factor receptor 2 (HER2)-negative, locally advanced or metastatic breast cancer, as detected by an FDA-approved test, following recurrence on or after completing adjuvant endocrine therapy.*
L83: A supplemental Pre-Market Approval (PMA) Application (sPMA P190032-S023) was submitted to CDRH for the FoundationOne Liquid CDx (F1LCDx). The sPMA will receive contemporaneous approval with this NDA as a companion diagnostic device for inavolisib. A PMC has been issued for tissue testing, as the PMA is planned to be submitted at the end of 2024.
L84: ## 1.3. Benefit-Risk Assessment (BRA)
L85: ## Benefit-Risk Summary and Assessment
L86: Breast cancer is the second most common cancer in women worldwide after skin cancer. In the U.S., 310,720 new cases of breast cancer and 42,780 deaths from breast cancer are estimated for 2024. Histopathological subtypes of breast cancer are defined by expression of the estrogen receptor (ER), progesterone receptor (PR), and/or human epidermal growth factor receptor 2 (HER2). Approximately 70% of patients with breast cancer will have hormone receptor (HR)-positive, HER2-negative disease. Although associated with a better prognosis than the other breast cancer subtypes, if HR-positive, HER2-negative breast cancer is advanced or metastatic, it is incurable and associated with a limited life expectancy.
L87: Endocrine therapy is the mainstay for HR-positive breast cancer. However, resistance may occur to endocrine therapy and a major challenge to treating HR-positive breast cancer is overcoming endocrine resistance. Activation of the PI3K/AKT pathway may lead to endocrine resistance. Endocrine resistant breast cancer is not uniformly defined but generally refers to cancer that grows in the presence of endocrine therapy (ET) or soon after completing ET. Endocrine resistant breast cancer is considered to be a negative prognostic factor as these patients tend to have worse survival outcomes compared to patients with endocrine sensitive breast cancer.
L88: While there are no specific therapies approved by FDA for treatment of patients with ET resistant HR-positive advanced or metastatic breast cancer, generally the treatment options after first line therapy can be considered for this population.
L89: The preferred first-line treatment for patients with HR-positive, HER2-negative advanced or metastatic breast cancer in the U.S. is a CDK4/6 inhibitor in combination with endocrine therapy (an aromatase inhibitor [AI] or fulvestrant). At disease progression, FDA-approved options for second and later-line treatment include endocrine monotherapy with fulvestrant, an AI, or tamoxifen (if not received as first-line treatment); everolimus in combination with exemestane; alpelisib in combination with fulvestrant in patients with a PIK3CA mutated tumor; capivasertib in combination with fulvestrant for patients with tumors that have a PIK3CA/AKT1/PTEN alteration, and chemotherapy such as capecitabine. Sequential endocrine based therapy is preferred because it is typically associated with less toxicity than chemotherapy. Endocrine monotherapy is also associated with less toxicity than combinations with targeted agents. In addition to these therapies, the antibody-drug-conjugate, fam-trastuzumab deruxtecan, is now available for patients with unresectable or metastatic HER2-low (IHC 1+ or IHC 2+/ISH-) breast cancer who have received prior chemotherapy in the metastatic setting or developed disease recurrence during or within 6 months of completing adjuvant therapy. At the time of design of INAV0120 (2019), palbociclib plus fulvestrant appeared to be an acceptable control arm
L90: for patients with HR-positive, HER2-negative metastatic breast cancer (with or without a PIK3CA mutation). This combination is still an option for first-line treatment; however, based on results from the MONALEESA-2 and MONARCH 3 trials which showed overall survival improvement, ribociclib and abemaciclib are now used more commonly than palbociclib in the first line setting in the US.
L91: Approximately 35-40% of patients with HR-positive breast cancer have tumors that harbor mutations in PIK3CA, the gene encoding the catalytic subunit of the alpha isoform of PI3K. PIK3CA mutations result in upregulation of PI3K pathway and are considered a negative prognostic factor. Alpelisib (PI3K\alpha inhibitor) plus fulvestrant and capivasertib (AKT inhibitor) plus fulvestrant are two treatment regimens approved by the FDA for use in patients with PIK3CA-mutated advanced breast cancer. However, both are for second line and later treatment and are not specifically for patients with endocrine resistant breast cancer.
L92: The efficacy and safety assessment for inavolisib in combination with palbociclib and fulvestrant is primarily based on data from INAVO120, a randomized (1:1), placebo-controlled, double-blind, multicenter trial in 325 patients with PIK3CA mutated, HR-positive, HER2-negative (defined as IHC 0 or 1+, or IHC 2+/ISH-), advanced or metastatic breast cancer, following recurrence on or within 12 months of adjuvant endocrine therapy and who have not received prior systemic therapy for locally advanced or metastatic disease. PIK3CA mutation was determined through testing of blood plasma-derived ctDNA with a next-generation sequencing (NGS) assay performed at the Sponsor-designated central laboratory, or through local testing of ctDNA or tumor tissue with an appropriately validated NGS or PCR assay. Randomization was stratified by presence of visceral disease (yes or no), endocrine resistance (primary or secondary), and geographic region (North America/Western Europe, Asia, other). Primary endocrine resistance was defined as relapse while on the first 2 years of adjuvant endocrine therapy and secondary endocrine resistance was defined as relapse while on adjuvant ET after at least 2 years or relapse within 12 months of completing adjuvant ET. Patients were required to have a HbA1c&lt;6% and fasting blood glucose&lt;126 mg/dL. The study excluded patients with Type 1 diabetes mellitus or Type 2 diabetes mellitus requiring ongoing systemic therapy at the start of study treatment.
L93: Patients received either inavolisib 9 mg (n=161) or placebo (n=164) orally once daily, in combination with palbociclib 125 mg orally once daily for 21 consecutive days followed by 7 days off treatment to comprise a cycle of 28 days and fulvestrant (500 mg) administered intramuscularly on Cycle 1, Days 1 and 15, and then on Day 1 of every 28-day cycle. Patients received treatment until disease progression or unacceptable toxicity. The primary efficacy endpoint for INAVO120 was PFS as assessed by investigator. A prospectively planned sensitivity analysis of PFS by BICR assessment was also performed on all patients. Key secondary endpoints included OS and ORR. DOR was a secondary endpoint.
L94: INAVO120 met its primary endpoint with a median PFS of 15 months (95% CI 11.3, 20.5) vs 7.3 months (95% CI: 5.6, 9.3) favoring inavolisib arm
L95: (HR 0.43, 95% CI: 0.32, 0.59; p-value &lt;0.0001). PFS by BICR results were supportive of the primary endpoint. At the planned interim analysis for OS, a total of 97 events had occurred (63% of planned OS events) and did not reach statistical significance based on the interim analysis stopping boundary [HR of 0.64 (95% CI: 0.43, 0.97)]. The final analysis of OS is expected in the second quarter of 2025. Results for the secondary endpoints of ORR and DOR appear to support the primary endpoint results. However, since the interim analysis of OS at the time of PFS analysis was not statistically significant, none of the remaining secondary endpoints in the pre-specified testing hierarchy were formally tested at this time.
L96: Racial and ethnic minorities reflective of the U.S. population of patients with breast cancer were significantly underrepresented in INAVO120. INAVO120 was a multinational trial with just 16 patients (4.9%) enrolled from the US. Almost all patients were female (98%) and the median age of enrolled patients was 54 years. The majority of patients on study were White, with only two Black patients and 20 Hispanic/Latino patients enrolled on study. Due to the lack of diversity in the enrolled population, a Post Marketing Commitment (PMC) is being issued to further characterize the efficacy and safety of inavolisib for the treatment of breast cancer in study populations whose demographics are more representative of the population of U.S. patients for the proposed indication.
L97: (b) (4)
L98: Data from a phase 1 trial GO39374 was submitted by the Applicant
L99: GO39374 is an ongoing, open-label, multicenter, trial designed to evaluate the safety, tolerability, and PK of inavolisib administered orally as a single agent in patients with locally advanced or metastatic PIK3CA-mutated solid tumors, including breast cancer, and in combination with standard-of-care endocrine and/or targeted therapies in patients with PIK3CA-mutated locally advanced and metastatic breast cancer. The applicant submitted data from 3 cohorts. Arm B (N=33; inavolisib in combination with palbociclib and letrozole), Arm E (N=20; inavolisib in combination with palbociclib and fulvestrant), and Arm F (N=21; inavolisib in combination with palbociclib, fulvestrant, and metformin). Arms B, E and F did not require that patients have endocrine resistant breast cancer. The confirmed ORR results in patients with measurable disease in Arm B (N=25), Arm E (N=15), and Arm F (N=18) were 52% (95% CI: 31%, 72%), 40% (95% CI: 16%, 68%), and 17% (95% CI: 0%, 41%), respectively. While activity was observed across the three treatment arms based on the confirmed ORR and DOR, given the small sample sizes and wide CI of ORR, the contribution of inavolisib to efficacy was unclear in these single arm cohorts.
L100: (b) (4)
L101: The overall safety profile for the triplet combination was consistent with the anticipated safety profile for inavolisib and the known safety
L102: profile for palbociclib and fulvestrant. Adverse events known to occur with PI3K inhibitors include hyperglycemia, stomatitis, diarrhea, and rash. Hyperglycemia occurred at much higher rates on the inavolisib treatment arm (85%) compared to the placebo arm (43%). There were no grade 4-5 cases of hyperglycemia or cases of DKA and HHNK. However, patients that enrolled on the trial were required to fasting blood glucose (FBG) &lt;126 and HBA1c&lt;6%. Stomatitis occurred at higher rates in the inavolisib treatment arm (51%) compared to the placebo arm (27%), including 6% of patients on the inavolisib arm having grade 3 events. There were no grade 4-5 events or SAEs. Diarrhea occurred at higher rates in the inavolisib treatment arm (48%) compared to the placebo arm (16%), including 4% of patients on the inavolisib arm having grade 3 events. There were two SAEs and no grade 4-5 events or colitis. Rash occurred in 27% of patients treated with inavolisib compared to 19% of patients on the placebo arm. There were no grade 3-5 events or SAEs. Ocular toxicity occurred in 22% of patients treated with inavolisib compared to 13% on the placebo arm. The most common toxicity was dry eye (9%). There were no grade 3-5 events, no SAEs and ocular toxicity did not lead to dose modifications (discontinuation, reduction, or interruption).
L103: Hematologic toxicities were seen in both treatment arms and are known toxicities with palbociclib. Neutropenia and anemia were seen in a similar proportion of patients regarding all grade toxicity; however, grade 3-4 toxicity was slightly higher in the inavolisib treatment arm. Thrombocytopenia (including platelet count decreased) was higher in the inavolisib treatment arm compared to the placebo treatment arm. Other adverse events (including laboratory abnormalities) noted in &gt;20% of patients treated with inavolisib + palbociclib + fulvestrant include decreased calcium, fatigue, decreased potassium, increased creatinine, increased ALT, nausea, decreased sodium, decreased magnesium, decreased albumin, decreased appetite, Covid-19, and headache.
L104: In addition, the Applicant evaluated expected symptomatic adverse events using patient-reported outcomes. Symptomatic adverse events related to likely toxicity from the combination of inavolisib, palbociclib, and fulvestrant (diarrhea, nausea, vomiting, fatigue, mouth sores, decreased appetite, and rash), were selected and assessed from the patient reported outcome (PRO)-CTCAE item library, along with an additional single item assessing bother due to side-effects of treatment. In general, these patient-reported symptoms were observed to be worse in the inavolisib arm, with the greatest difference observed for diarrhea, mouth sores, decreased appetite, and rash. Although the PRO data collection was considered exploratory, these symptoms were considered supportive of the clinician reported safety information and informative for labeling and were included in Section 6 of the USPI.
L105: The addition of inavolisib to standard of care treatment with palbociclib and fulvestrant resulted in a statistically significant and clinically meaningful improvement in PFS for the patient population enrolled on INAV0120. Results for secondary endpoints were supportive of the primary endpoint. The addition of inavolisib to palbociclib and fulvestrant resulted in increased toxicity. However, the median relative dose
L106: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219249}
## ITOVEBI {inavolisib}
L107: intensity of palbociclib and fulvestrant was similar between the two treatment arms of the INAVO120; therefore, the triplet combination therapy including inavolisib did not have a significant impact on the ability to administer full dose palbociclib + fulvestrant. The most notable increase in adverse events were observed in hyperglycemia, stomatitis, and diarrhea, including severe cases. Therefore, hyperglycemia, stomatitis and diarrhea will be labeled under warnings and precautions in the USPI to better inform providing physicians and to provide guidance for dose modifications. These adverse events could be managed with dose interruptions and dose reductions in most cases.
L108: Overall, the benefit-risk assessment is favorable for the triplet combination treatment with inavolisib + palbociclib + fulvestrant in adult patients with PIK3CA mutated, HR-positive, HER2-negative, locally advanced, or metastatic breast cancer, following recurrence on or within 12 months of adjuvant endocrine therapy. There is not adequate data to support a benefit for use of the triplet combination in patients that do not have PIK3CA mutated advanced breast cancer that is not considered endocrine resistant or for use of a different endocrine therapy other than fulvestrant.
L109: Based on the overall benefit-risk assessment, the review team recommends regular approval for the following indication:
L110: *Inavolisib (ITOVEBI), in combination with palbociclib and fulvestrant, is indicated for the treatment of adults with endocrine-resistant, PIK3CA-mutated, hormone receptor (HR)-positive, human epidermal growth factor receptor 2 (HER2)-negative, locally advanced or metastatic breast cancer, as detected by an FDA-approved test, following recurrence on or after completing adjuvant endocrine therapy.*
L111: Inavolisib will be the first FDA-approved treatment to be used in a triplet combination, specifically for the first line treatment of patients with PIK3CA mutated locally advanced or metastatic HR positive, HER2 negative breast cancer considered to have endocrine resistant disease. Patients should be selected for treatment using the FoundationOne Liquid CDx (F1LDx). The sPMA P190032-S023 will be contemporaneously approved with this NDA for inavolisib. A PMC has been issued for tissue testing, as the PMA is planned to be submitted at the end of 2024.
L112: Dimension	Evidence and Uncertainties	Conclusions and Reasons
Analysis of Condition	Breast cancer is the most common cancer in women, with 310,720 new cases estimated and 42,780 deaths estimated from breast cancer in the U.S. in 2024. Although rare breast cancer does occur in male patients.
Endocrine resistant, PIK3CA mutated, HR-positive, HER2-negative advanced or metastatic breast cancer is a serious and life-threatening condition.

L115: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219249}
## ITOVEBI {inavolisib}
L116: Dimension	Evidence and Uncertainties	Conclusions and Reasons
About 70% of breast cancers are hormone receptor (HR) positive.
Approximately 35-40% of patients with HR-positive breast cancer have tumors that harbor mutations in PIK3CA.
Endocrine resistant breast cancer is considered to be a negative prognostic factor as these patients tend to have worse survival outcomes compared to patients with endocrine sensitive breast cancer.
Advanced or metastatic HR-positive, HER-negative breast cancer remains incurable.

Current Treatment Options	Treatment for advanced or metastatic breast cancer is palliative in intent with the goals to prolong survival, delay progression, and reduce symptoms from cancer.
Treatment with a CDK 4/6 inhibitor plus endocrine therapy (fulvestrant or aromatase inhibitor) is considered standard of care first line treatment for patients with advanced or metastatic HR-positive, HER-negative breast cancer.
For patients that have progression of disease with/after CDK4/6 inhibitor + endocrine therapy, treatment options include endocrine monotherapy or combination endocrine therapy + targeted agents (alpelisib, capivasertib, elacestrant, everolimus) depending on the presence or absence of specific genetic alterations. Second line treatment with CDK 4/6 inhibitor + endocrine therapy may be considered for patients that did not receive it in the first line. Other options include chemotherapy (capecitabine, eribulin, ixabepilone, paclitaxel protein-bound, gemcitabine), and/or an antibody-drug conjugate fam-trastuzumab-deruxtecan, for patients with HER2 low (IHC1+ or IHC2+/ISH-) advanced or metastatic breast cancer
There are no FDA approvals specifically for the first line treatment of patients with endocrine resistant, PIK3CA mutated, HR-positive, HER-negative advanced or metastatic breast cancer. These patients are treated with currently available options for HR-positive, HER2 negative breast cancer. All treatment options are palliative and there is an unmet medical need to improve outcomes in these patients.

Benefit	INAVO120 enrolled 325 patients with PIK3CA-mutated, HR-positive, HER2-negative (defined as IHC 0 or 1+, or IHC 2+/ISH-), advanced or metastatic breast cancer, following recurrence on or within 12 months of adjuvant endocrine therapy and who have not received prior systemic therapy for locally advanced or metastatic disease.
Patients were randomized 1:1 to either inavolisib + palbociclib + fulvestrant or placebo + palbociclib + fulvestrant.
The median PFS by investigator was 15 months in the inavolisib arm and 7.3 months in the placebo arm (HR 0.43, 95% CI: 0.32, 0.59; p-value <0.0001).
PFS by BICR results were supportive of the primary endpoint.
At the planned interim analysis for OS, a total of 97 events had occurred (63% of planned OS events) and did not reach statistical significance based on the interim analysis stopping boundary.
Inavolisib + palbociclib + fulvestrant demonstrated a statistically significant and clinically meaningful improvement in PFS for endocrine resistant, PIK3CA mutated, HR-positive, HER2-negative advanced or metastatic breast cancer, which was supported by a favorable trend in OS and secondary endpoints such as ORR and DOR.
Risk and Risk Management	Serious adverse reactions occurred in 24% of patients who received inavolisib with palbociclib and fulvestrant. Serious adverse reactions in ≥ 1% of patients included anemia (1.9%), diarrhea (1.2%), and urinary tract infection (1.2%).
Fatal adverse reactions occurred in 3.7% of patients who received inavolisib with palbociclib and fulvestrant, including (0.6% each) acute coronary syndrome, cerebral hemorrhage, cerebrovascular accident, COVID-19 infection, and gastrointestinal hemorrhage.
Safe use of inavolisib with palbociclib plus fulvestrant can be managed with labeling.
Hyperglycemia, stomatitis, diarrhea, and embryo-fetal toxicity are included under Warnings and Precautions.
There is no indication for a REMS.

L128: ## 2 Therapeutic Context
L129: ## 2.1. Analysis of Condition
L130: **The Applicant's Position:** Breast cancer is the most commonly diagnosed cancer in women, with an estimated global incidence of 2.2 million new cases and almost 700,000 deaths reported in 2020 (Sung et al. 2021). Breast cancer in men is rare; accounting for less than 1% of all breast cancer cases in the US. Hormone receptor positive (HR+), human epidermal growth factor receptor 2-negative (HER2-) breast cancer is the most common subtype accounting for approximately 70% of all breast cancers (Breast Cancer, Facts and Figures 2022-2024).
L131: In the U.S., the median age at diagnosis for HR+, HER2- breast cancer patients is 64.0 years. For metastatic HR+, HER2- breast cancer, the annual incidence was observed to be highest for non-Hispanic Blacks (5.1 per 100,000 women), followed by non-Hispanic Whites (4.0 per 100,000), Hispanics (3.0 per 100,000), and Asian/Pacific Islanders (2.8 per 100,000 women) (SEER 2023).
L132: Locally advanced and metastatic breast cancer (LA/mBC) remains a virtually incurable disease, with 29.2% of these patients likely to survive at least 5 years from the time of diagnosis. The 5-year survival of distant disease also remains low for HR+, HER2- LA/mBC at 34% (SEER 2023).
L133: Approximately 35-40% of patients with HR+ breast cancer have tumors that harbor mutations in *PIK3CA*, the gene encoding the catalytic subunit of the alpha isoform of PI3K (i.e., p110\alpha) (Fillbrunn et al. 2022). There are mixed reports on whether there are differences in *PIK3CA* mutation prevalence in HR+ breast cancer by race. In studies of Asian and Hispanic patients, the mutation prevalence range (i.e., 35-40%) was similar to non-Hispanic Whites, while for African American women and those with African ancestry, some data suggest *PIK3CA* mutation prevalence may be up to 10% lower (Li et al. 2021; Blesinger et al. 2018; Chen et al. 2022; Pitt et al. 2018; Baselga et al. 2017).
L134: *PIK3CA* mutations result in upregulation of PI3K pathway activity (Saal et al. 2005; Stemke-Hale et al. 2008) and are a negative prognostic factor (Fillbrunn et al. 2022). Specifically, it has been estimated that patients with HR+, HER2- mBC whose tumors harbor one or more *PIK3CA* mutations exhibit approximately 2 months shorter progression-free survival (PFS) and 8 months shorter overall survival (OS), when treated with standard of care options compared with their wildtype counterparts (Fillbrunn et al. 2022).
L135: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L136: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment. FDA considers advanced and metastatic HR-positive, HER2-negative breast cancer that is endocrine resistant to be incurable, and the goal of therapy is palliative.
L137: Endocrine therapy is the mainstay for HR-positive breast cancer. However, resistance may occur to endocrine therapy and a major challenge to treating HR-positive breast cancer is overcoming endocrine resistance. Activation of the PI3K/AKT pathway may lead to endocrine resistance. Endocrine resistant breast cancer is not uniformly defined but generally refers to cancer that grows in the presence of endocrine therapy (ET) or soon after completing ET. Endocrine resistant breast cancer is considered to be a negative prognostic factor as these patients tend to have worse survival outcomes compared to patients with endocrine sensitive breast cancer.
L138: ## 2.2. Analysis of Current Treatment Options
L139: ## The Applicant's Position:
L140: ## Treatment Options in HR-positive, HER2-negative LA/mBC
L141: Endocrine therapy (ET) remains the cornerstone of treatment for patients with HR+ breast cancer. The current treatment paradigm for advanced HR+, HER2– breast cancer is sequential endocrine-based therapies to control the disease and delay chemotherapy in the majority of cases. For patients considered endocrine-resistant/refractory or with visceral crisis (imminent organ failure), chemotherapy is recommended (NCCN 2024).
L142: The combination of a cyclin dependent kinase 4/6 (CDK4/6) inhibitor with either fulvestrant or an aromatase inhibitor (AI) is considered the standard of care first- and/or second-line treatment for LA/mBC; endocrine monotherapy may be considered appropriate for frail patients with comorbidities (ESMO 2023; NCCN 2024). Endocrine-based therapy is recommended for both post- and pre-/peri-menopausal patients, though pre-/peri-menopausal women are recommended to have ovarian function suppression or ablation. In men, the treatment approach is similar to that of women (ESMO 2023; NCCN 2024; Vasconcelos de Matos et al. 2023).
L143: In patients who relapse after ET plus a CDK4/6 inhibitor, several treatment options are available, which are tailored depending on several factors including clinical (e.g. disease burden), molecular (e.g. presence of somatic *PIK3CA*, *AKT1*, *PTEN*, or *ESR1* mutations), response to prior agents (treatment resistance), patient preference, and treatment availability. As such, the treatment options range from endocrine monotherapy (e.g. elacestrant for *ESR1* mutated tumors) to combinations with targeted agents, like alpelisib plus fulvestrant (for *PIK3CA*-mutated tumors), everolimus plus ET, and capivasertib plus fulvestrant (for *PIK3CA/AKT/PTEN* altered tumors), and in some cases, second-line CDK4/6 inhibitor plus an endocrine agent (ESMO 2023; NCCN 2024).
L144: In addition to these therapies, particularly in cases of endocrine resistance and/or rapid disease progression and significant visceral disease, chemotherapy-based may be used at physician's clinical discretion (Guiliano et al. 2019). Current guidelines recommend the use of sequential single-agent chemotherapy. However, in patients with visceral crisis, combination chemotherapy regimens are recommended on the basis of improved response rates and longer time to progression compared to single agents, but at the expense of increased toxicity and suboptimal quality of life (ESMO 2023; NCCN 2024). There are several chemotherapy agents considered appropriate as monotherapy or in combination, including taxanes (most commonly weekly paclitaxel), capecitabine and eribulin. (Seidman et al. 2008; Jäger et al. 2010; Kaufmann et al. 2010; Cortes et al. 2011; Stockler et al. 2011; O'Shaughnessy et al. 2012; Rugo et al. 2015; Harbeck et al. 2017). There is no clear recommendation for one over the other, and the treatment decision is individualized after taking into consideration factors such as previous therapies, co-morbidities, and patient preference (ESMO 2023; NCCN 2024). Recently, the antibody-drug-conjugate (ADC), fam-trastuzumab deruxtecan has been approved for patients with HER2-low (IHC 1+ or IHC 2+/ISH-) LA/mBC, who have received prior chemotherapy in the metastatic setting or developed disease recurrence during or within 6 months of completing adjuvant therapy.
L145: ## Treatment Options in PIK3CA-mutated, HR-positive, HER2-negative LA/mBC
L146: Currently, patients with HR+, HER2- LA/mBC whose tumors harbor a *PIK3CA* mutation can be treated with any standard-of-care therapy for HR+, HER2- LA/mBC since these therapies have been approved without a specific biomarker requirement. However, as none of these treatment options target the PI3K pathway directly, patients with *PIK3CA*-mutated tumors may derive less benefit compared to patients whose tumors do not harbor a *PIK3CA* mutation (Fillbrunn et al. 2022). This has been observed with both endocrine-based (Fillbrunn et al. 2022) and chemotherapy-based options (Mosele et al. 2020).
L147: Because *PIK3CA* mutations are a known predictive biomarker of response to PI3K\alpha AKT inhibitors (André et al. 2019; Turner et al. 2023), there are additional treatment options for patients with *PIK3CA*-mutated tumors (André et al. 2019; Turner et al. 2023). Alpelisib (PI3K\alpha inhibitor) plus fulvestrant is a preferred second/later line option for patients with *PIK3CA*-mutated, HR+, HER2- LA/mBC (André et al. 2019; Rugo et al. 2020). The challenging safety and tolerability of alpelisib has affected its adoption in clinical practice and is a key reason it is not recommended as a first-line treatment option (ESMO 2023; NCCN 2024). In addition, everolimus (an mTOR inhibitor) in combination with exemestane is recommended as second/later line option, regardless of *PI3KCA* mutation status.
L148: Recently, capivasertib (AKT inhibitor) plus fulvestrant was approved for patients with HR+, HER2- LA/mBC that harbors a *PIK3CA/AKT1/PTEN* alteration following recurrence or progression on an endocrine-based regimen and is a preferred second/later -line option (NCCN 2024).
L149: Version date: June 2022 (ALL NDA/BLA reviews)
L150: **Disclaimer:** In this document, the sections labeled as "Data" and "The Applicant's Position" are completed by the Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.
L151: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L152: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment.
L153: Currently, there are no FDA approved treatments specifically for the first line treatment of patients with PIK3CA mutated locally advanced or metastatic HR-positive, HER2-negative breast cancer considered to have endocrine resistant disease.

L179: FoundationOne Liquid CDx is a qualitative next generation sequencing based in vitro diagnostic test that uses targeted high throughput hybridization-based capture technology to detect and report substitutions, insertions and deletions (indels) in 311 genes, including rearrangements in eight (8) genes and copy number alterations in three (3) genes. FoundationOneLiquid CDx utilizes circulating cell-free DNA (cfDNA) isolated from plasma derived from anti-coagulated peripheral whole blood of cancer patients collected in FoundationOneLiquid CDx cfDNA blood collection tubes included in the FoundationOneLiquid CDx Blood Sample Collection Kit. The test is intended to be used as a companion diagnostic to identify patients who may benefit from treatment with targeted therapies in accordance with the approved therapeutic product labeling.
L180: The FoundationOne Liquid CDx assay was initially approved in November 2017 under P190032. Foundation Medicine, Inc. has since submitted multiple supplements expanding the indications of the device, including expansions for CDx indications, as well as updates to the assay.
L181: This PMA supplement for F1CDx was primarily comprised of results from the INAVO120 clinical study. Analytical study data were leveraged with data from P190032 (this was agreed to in CDRH's written response to Q232680 provided to FMI on December 18, 2023).

L184: ## 5 Nonclinical Pharmacology/Toxicology
L185: ## 5.1. Executive Summary
L186: Inavolisib (ITOVEBI), also known as GDC-0077, RO7113755, and G02967907, is an inhibitor of phosphatidylinositol 3-kinase (PI3K) with inhibitory activity predominantly against PI3K\alpha, encoded by the PIK3CA gene, and belongs to the pharmacological class of kinase inhibitors. PI3K is a signal transducer downstream of cell surface receptors and regulator of cell metabolism and survival. The PI3K signaling pathway has been reported to be dysregulated in hormone receptor (HR)-positive breast cancer, often due to activating PIK3CA mutations. In this NDA submission, the Applicant submitted nonclinical pharmacology (primary, secondary, safety), ADME/PK, and toxicology (general, developmental, genetic) data to support the approval of inavolisib in combination with palbociclib and fulvestrant for the treatment of adults with endocrine-resistant, PIK3CA-mutated, HR-positive, human epidermal growth-factor receptor 2 (HER2)-negative, locally advanced or metastatic breast cancer, as detected by an FDA-approved test, following recurrence on or after completing adjuvant endocrine therapy. The proposed recommended dosage and administration of inavolisib is 9 mg taken orally once daily.
L187: In an in vitro biochemical assay, inavolisib inhibited PI3K\alpha with higher potency compared to the other PI3K isoforms including PI3K\beta, PI3K\delta, and PI3K\gamma with inhibitory constant (Ki) values of 0.038 nM, 101.7 nM, 12.8 nM, and 21.8 nM, respectively. The steady-state C_{max} for inavolisib is 69 ng/mL (169 nM or \sim107 nM unbound) at the recommended dose of 9 mg/day. In a kinase panel of 224 kinases, inavolisib only inhibited 3 kinases (defined as &gt; 80% inhibition at 1 \muM) – PI3K\alpha, PI3K\gamma, and PI3KC2\beta, (96.4%, 96.2%, and 81.7% inhibition at 1 \muM, respectively). Inavolisib treatment of a human breast cancer cell line (HCC1954) reported to contain the hotspot H1047R PIK3CA alteration, led to proteasome-dependent depletion of the PI3K catalytic subunit alpha isoform (p110\alpha) protein but not in a human breast cancer cell line (HDQ-P1) reported to be PIK3CA wild type. Similarly, inavolisib treatment resulted in greater increases in apoptosis, as measured by cleaved PARP protein, and decreases in downstream signaling, as measured by pAKTS473, in the PIK3CA-mutated breast cancer cell line (HCC1954) than in the PIK3CA-WT breast cancer cell line (HDQ-P1). Inavolisib treatment of the MCF-7 breast cancer cell line (estrogen receptor [ER]-positive, HER2-negative, and PIK3CAE545K-mutated) resulted in reduced in vitro cell viability alone and a synergistic effect in combination with fulvestrant or palbociclib. Inavolisib showed anti-tumor effects in xenograft mouse models of human breast cancer using KPL-4, HCC1954X1, and MCF7 cell lines. KPL-4 and HCC1954X1 are reported to be ER-negative/HER2-positive and to harbor a hotspot alteration in the p110\alpha catalytic domain (H1047R), resulting in elevated levels of pAKT and constitutive PI3K pathway activation. The antitumor effect of inavolisib (% tumor growth inhibition [TGI] = 61) was increased when administered in combination with palbociclib (%TGI = 102), fulvestrant (%TGI =
L188: 79), or palbociclib plus fulvestrant (%TGI = 119) in mice bearing MCF-7 tumors.
L189: Inavolisib did not show significant inhibition of cardiac human ether-à-go-go-related gene (hERG) channel, NaV1.5 voltage gated sodium channel, or CaV1.2 L type calcium channel, in vitro. In a GLP-compliant single dose safety pharmacology study in telemetry instrumented Beagle dogs, inavolisib-related cardiovascular effects included generally dose-dependent, reversible decreases in heart rate, increases in arterial pressure and contractility, lengthening of PR and QT intervals (likely driven by the decreased heart rates), and minimally lengthened corrected QT (QTc) interval (+11 msec/+5%) at doses \ge 0.3 mg/kg. Overall, the findings up to 3 mg/kg (10 times the human exposure at the recommended dose of 9 mg/day based on C_{max}) were not considered adverse based on the changes being minimal in magnitude and transient. No cardiovascular, respiratory, or neurobehavioral abnormalities were detected in the rat or dog repeat-dose toxicity studies.
L190: Nonclinical ADME/PK studies showed that exposure to inavolisib in rats and dogs increased dose proportionally and showed no significant accumulation following multiple administrations. In rats, exposure was higher (approximately 2-fold) in females than in males; however, overall, there were no significant differences in toxicities in female compared to male rats. No sex-related differences in exposure parameters were noted in dogs. Tissue distribution studies in pigmented and non-pigmented rats showed that inavolisib-related radioactivity was extensively distributed in rat tissues and organs, including the endocrine tissues (adrenal gland), excretory tissues (kidney, liver, urinary bladder wall), the gastrointestinal tract, uveal tract, and the skin. By 672 hours post-dose, levels of radioactivity in all tissues were below quantifiable levels except for the adrenal gland, medulla and cortex, and uveal tract, where concentrations remained quantifiable at 1344 hours post-dose. Following a single oral administration, inavolisib was the most abundant drug-related component in rat plasma, urine, feces, and bile. No human-specific metabolites (&gt; 10\% of total circulating radioactivity exposure) were observed. The primary route of elimination of inavolisib and drug-related radioactivity in rats was the feces (57% - 64% of the dose), followed by elimination in urine (23% - 35% of the dose) and bile (9% - 15% of the dose).
L191: Inavolisib was evaluated in GLP-compliant, repeat-dose toxicology studies in Sprague-Dawley rats and Beagle dogs following once daily oral administration, consistent with the clinical route of administration and intended dosing schedule, for up to 3 months in duration. Rats were dosed up to 10 mg/kg and 5 mg/kg in the 4- and 13-week studies, respectively, corresponding to 6.3 times and 1.8 times the human exposure at the recommended dose of 9 mg/day based on AUC, respectively. Dogs were dosed up to 5/3 mg/kg and 1.5/1 mg/kg in the 4- and 13-week studies, respectively, corresponding to up to 6.1 times and 1.5 times the human exposure at the recommended dose of 9 mg/day based on AUC. There were no early mortalities in rats; however, dogs were dose reduced in both 4- and 13-week studies due to moribund condition associated with hypoactivity, body weight loss, hyperglycemia, reddish conjunctiva, and/or
L192: clinical observations indicative of persistent ocular inflammation correlated with slight mixed cell endophthalmitis. Overall, the major target organs of toxicity in rats or dogs were the lymphoid tissues (thymus, gut-associated lymphoid tissue/Peyer's patch, mesenteric and mandibular lymph nodes, spleen, bone marrow), eye, pancreas, and male and female reproductive organs. Findings in the lymphoid organs consisted of decreases in erythroid or lymphoid cellularity which correlated with decreases in red or white blood cell parameters at \ge 1.5 mg/kg/day in rats (\ge 0.3 times the human exposure at the recommended dose of 9 mg/day based on AUC). Hyperglycemia was observed in both rats and dogs at \ge 1 mg/kg/day (\ge 0.1 times the human exposure at the recommended dose of 9 mg/day based on AUC) and \ge 0.5 mg/kg/day (\ge 0.8 times the human exposure at the recommended dose of 9 mg/day based on AUC), respectively. Hyperglycemia was reversible and was correlated with histopathology findings in the pancreas which included islet hypertrophy with vascular ectasia (angiectasia) in rats and diffuse hypertrophy/vacuolation of islet cells in dogs. Ocular findings were noted in rats and dogs. In rats, minimal to mild lens degeneration of the eye was observed at 10 mg/kg/day (6.3 times the human exposure at the recommended dose of 9 mg/day based on AUC) at terminal and recovery necropsy in the 1-month study only. In dogs, bilateral, reversible, very slight swelling of the fibers at the equatorial region of the lens and lens cortex vacuolation were observed at \ge 0.3 mg/kg/day (\ge 0.5 times the human exposure at the recommended dose of 9 mg/day based on combined male and female AUC value of 485 ng\cdoth/mL) and \ge 1 mg/kg/day (\ge 1.2 times the human exposure at the recommended dose based on combined male and female AUC value of 1250 ng\cdoth/mL), respectively, in the 3-month study. Hyperglycemia of any grade occurred in 60% of patients treated with ITOVEBI and is included in the Warnings and Precautions of the label. Ocular toxicities were limited to grades 1-2 adverse reactions in patients, with dry eye, blurred vision, and increased lacrimation as the most frequently reported adverse reactions. Findings of ocular toxicity were included in section 13.2 of the Prescribing Information (PI) based on distribution of inavolisib in the eye and dose-related findings in rats and dogs, suggesting that the ocular toxicities may be a direct effect of inavolisib administration.
L193: In GLP-compliant genetic toxicology studies, inavolisib was not mutagenic in the bacterial reverse mutation assay. Inavolisib was clastogenic in an in vitro human lymphocyte micronucleus assay. In an in vivo rat bone marrow micronucleus assay, female rats were administered vehicle control or 5, 15, or 40 mg/kg inavolisib orally, once daily for three days. Inavolisib treatment did not cause a significant increase in micronucleus frequency in polychromatic erythrocytes of the bone marrow and did not induce DNA strand breaks in the liver above the historical control range when evaluated by the alkaline Comet assay. Carcinogenicity studies were not conducted, nor warranted, with inavolisib to support the proposed indication in patients with advanced cancer in accordance with ICH S9 guidance.
L194: In a GLP-compliant preliminary embryo-fetal development study, pregnant rats received oral doses of inavolisib up to 6 mg/kg/day during the period of organogenesis from gestational day
L195: (GD)7 to GD17. Administration of inavolisib resulted in dose dependent effects on embryo-fetal development at \ge 2 mg/kg (\ge 0.9 times the human exposure at the recommended dose of 9 mg/day based on AUC) that included decreases in fetal body weight and placental weight, post-implantation loss, lower fetal viability, fetal malformations (including kyphosis of vertebral column, fused thoracic arch, microphthalmia) and variations (including dilated renal pelvis, short supernumerary rib, and wavy rib).
L196: Fertility and early embryonic development studies were not conducted nor are warranted to support the proposed indication in patients with advanced cancer in accordance with ICH S9 guidance. In the 4-week and 13-week repeat-dose toxicology studies with inavolisib in rats and dogs, histopathology changes observed in reproductive organs suggest that inavolisib has the potential to impair male and female fertility. Adverse findings in animals included dose-dependent and reversible atrophy of the prostate and seminal vesicle and decreased organ weights, without microscopic correlates, in the epididymis and testis at oral inavolisib doses \ge 1.5 mg/kg/day in male rats (\ge 0.4 times the human exposure at the recommended dose of 9 mg/day based on AUC), and focal inspissation of seminiferous tubule contents and reversible multinucleated spermatids in the testis and epithelial degeneration/necrosis in the epididymis at oral inavolisib doses \ge 1.5 mg/kg/day in male dogs (\ge 2 times the human exposure at the recommended dose of 9 mg/day based on AUC). Findings in dogs were not observed following a recovery period. Atrophy in the uterus and vagina, decreased ovarian follicles and findings suggestive of an interruption/alteration of the estrus cycle were observed in female rats following up to 3 months of dosing at oral inavolisib doses \ge 3 mg/kg/day (\ge 1.2 times the human exposure at the recommended dose of 9 mg/day based on AUC). Findings in the uterus, vagina, and estrous cycle observed in the 4-week toxicity study were not observed following a 4-week recovery period. Recovery was not assessed in the 3-month study in rats.
L197: Based on animal data and its mechanism of action, ITOVEBI can cause fetal harm when administered to a pregnant woman. Inavolisib was genotoxic in vitro (clastogenic) but was not genotoxic in an in vivo rat bone marrow micronucleus test and did not induce DNA break in a liver comet assay. Therefore, overall, inavolisib is not considered genotoxic. Inavolisib has an estimated elimination half-life of 15 hours following a single 9 mg dose. Consistent with the recommendations outlined in the FDA guidance "Oncology Pharmaceuticals: Reproductive Toxicity Testing and Labeling Recommendations Guidance for Industry", females of reproductive potential should use effective non-hormonal contraception during treatment with ITOVEBI and for 1 week after the last dose. Male patients with female partners of reproductive potential should use effective contraception during treatment with ITOVEBI and for 1 week after the last dose. FDA recommended the use of non-hormonal contraception for females due to the potential for drug-drug interaction between inavolisib and combined oral contraceptives, which are known substrates of CYP3A. These contraceptives could reduce the effectiveness of the combined oral contraceptives and lead to unintended pregnancies. Until the drug-drug interaction potential between inavolisib and CYP3A substrates is evaluated, the concomitant
L198: medication should be limited to non-hormonal contraceptives. There are no data on the presence of inavolisib or its metabolites in human milk, its effects on milk production, or the breastfed child. Because of the potential for serious adverse reactions in a breastfed child, lactating women should not breastfeed during treatment with ITOVEBI and for 1 week after the last dose. Because ITOVEBI is indicated in combination with palbociclib and fulvestrant, the prescribing information also includes a reference to the Full Prescribing Information of palbociclib and fulvestrant for pregnancy, contraception, infertility, and lactation information.
L199: ## **Recommendation:**
L200: The nonclinical data submitted to this NDA are adequate to support the approval of ITOVEBI for the granted indication.
L201: ## 5.2. Referenced NDAs, BLAs, DMFs
L202: The Applicant's Position: Not applicable.
L203: ## 5.3. Pharmacology
L204: ## Primary pharmacology
L205: The Applicant's Position: Inavolisib is a highly selective oral small molecule inhibitor of the catalytic subunit alpha isoform protein of PI3K (p110\alpha; encoded by the *PIK3CA* gene), with sub-nanomolar potency. In addition, inavolisib promotes the degradation of mutated p110\alpha. With its dual mechanism of action, inavolisib inhibits the activity of downstream PI3K pathway targets, including AKT, resulting in reduced cellular proliferation and induction of apoptosis in *PIK3CA*-mutated breast cancer cell lines.
L206: In *PIK3CA*-mutated breast cancer cell line-derived xenograft models, inavolisib reduced tumor growth. This anti-tumor activity was more pronounced when inavolisib was used in combination with standard-of-care therapies for breast cancer, including fulvestrant or palbociclib (CDK4/6 inhibitor). Together, these nonclinical data for inavolisib support combination therapy use as a treatment for patients with *PIK3CA*-mutated, HR+, HER2–LA/mBC.
L207: ## Key In Vitro Primary Pharmacology Findings:
L208: - Inavolisib has an *in vitro* biochemical potency (K_i) of 0.038 nM for PI3K\alpha, which is 300-fold more selective over the other Class I PI3K isoforms (Hanan et al. 2022, Study 16-1792/4.2.1.1).
- Inavolisib inhibited 3 of 224 kinases in the Invitrogen kinase panel at &gt;80% at a concentration of 1 \muM. Two of the kinases, PI3K\alpha and PI3K\gamma, are targets of inavolisib. Additionally, inavolisib inhibited PI3KC2\beta, a PI3K family member, at 82%. In a subsequent
L209: study, inavolisib was 2000-fold more selective for PI3K\alpha relative to all other kinases tested (Study 16-1794/4.2.1.1).
L210: - Inavolisib promotes the degradation of mutated p110\alpha. Treatment of cells with inavolisib leads to depletion of p110\alpha protein in the HCC1954 breast cancer cell line that contains mutated *PIK3CA* but not in the HDQ-P1 cell line that is wild type (WT) for *PIK3CA*. Inavolisib shows greater reduction in levels of pathway markers such as pAKT and a greater increase in apoptosis when assayed by cleaved poly (ADP-ribose) polymerase (cleaved PARP) in *PIK3CA*-mutant cell lines than in *PIK3CA* WT cancer cells (Study 16-1793/4.2.1.1, Song et al. 2022).
- Inavolisib inhibits cellular proliferation of *PIK3CA*-mutated breast cancer cells *in vitro*, with mean half-maximal effective anti-proliferative concentrations (EC_{50}) in the nanomolar range (Hanan et al. 2022, Song et al. 2022).
L211: ## **Key *In Vivo* Primary Pharmacology Findings:**
L212: - PI3K pathway markers pAKT, pPRAS40, and pS6 were reduced for up to 8 hours in response to a single dose of 5 or 50 mg/kg inavolisib, and reduction of pathway markers was both dose- and time-dependent in the HCC1954X1 breast cancer xenograft model (Study 15-0797-B/4.2.1.1).
- Inavolisib inhibited tumor growth of KPL-4 *PIK3CA*-mutated human breast cancer xenograft tumors grown orthotopically in severe combined immunodeficient (SCID) beige mice. Treatment at the highest dose of inavolisib at the maximum tolerated dose (MTD) of 50 mg/kg resulted in a maximal response of 153% tumor growth inhibition (TGI) and 100% objective response rate (ORR) (9 partial responses [PR] and 1 complete response [CR] out of 10 animals) (Study 13-3399-L/4.2.1.1).
- Inavolisib inhibited tumor growth in a nude mouse HCC1954X1 *PIK3CA*-mutated human breast cancer xenograft model. Treatment at the highest dose of inavolisib (50 mg/kg) resulted in 149% TGI and 60% ORR (6 PRs out of 10 animals) (Study 13-3399-O/4.2.1.1).
- The combination of inavolisib with palbociclib and fulvestrant resulted in greater TGI than inavolisib, palbociclib, or fulvestrant administered as single agents or doublet combinations in a nude mouse MCF7 *PIK3CA*-mutated breast cancer xenograft model. The triple combination of inavolisib, palbociclib, and fulvestrant resulted in greater TGI on Day 20 (119%) when compared with each single agent as well as the doublet combinations (Study 16-2204/4.2.1.1, **Figure 1**).
L213: **Figure 1: Applicant - Response of MCF7 *PIK3CA*-Mutated Breast Cancer Xenografts to Combination Therapy with Inavolisib, Palbociclib, and Fulvestrant (Study 16-2204 / 4.2.1.1 Primary Pharmacodynamics)**

L216: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L217: In general, FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. Authors in the publications discussed above and used to support labeling are affiliated with Genentech (the Applicant). Select data discussed by the Applicant are shown below.

L229: ## Secondary Pharmacology
L230: **The Applicant's Position:** No off-target responses (&gt;50% inhibition or stimulation of control-specific binding) were identified when inavolisib was evaluated in radioligand binding and enzyme assays at a concentration of 10 \muM against 88 targets (including those associated with abuse liability), including major classes of biogenic amine receptors, neuropeptide receptors, ion channels, neurotransmitter transporters, and enzymes (Studies 15-0292 and 15-1941/4.2.1.2).
L231: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L232: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.
L233: ## Safety Pharmacology
L234: Inavolisib does not show significant inhibition of cardiac human ether-à-go-go-related gene (hERG) channel (Study 15-3355/4.2.1.3), Nav1.5 voltage-gated sodium channel (Study 15-0486/4.2.1.3), or Cav1.2 L-type calcium channel (Study 15-0487/4.2.1.3) in *in vitro* assays. The 50%
L235: inhibitory concentration (IC_{50}) for inhibition of hERG current was &gt; 1700 \times higher than the human plasma maximum concentration observed (C_{max}) [unbound] (0.116 \mu M) at the 9 mg dose.
L236: In the single-dose telemetry-instrumented dog safety pharmacology study (Study 15-3293/4.2.1.3), inavolisib-related cardiovascular effects included generally dose-dependent, reversible and non-adverse decreases in heart rate, increases in arterial pressure and contractility, lengthening of PR and QT intervals (likely driven by the decreased heart rates), and minimally lengthened corrected QT (QT_c) interval (+ 11 msec/+ 5\%) at doses \ge 0.3 mg/kg (Study 15-3293). The C_{max} [total] at the no-observed-adverse-effect level (NOAEL) of 3 mg/kg was 679 ng/mL (9.0 \times the C_{max} of 75.1 ng/mL at 9 mg in the clinic).
L237: No cardiovascular, respiratory, or neurobehavioral abnormalities were detected in rat or dog repeat-dose toxicity studies.
L238: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L239: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. To calculate the exposure multiples, FDA used the C_{max} of 69 ng/mL at the recommended dose of 9 mg/day human dose described in the PI for ITOVEBI.
L240: ## 5.4. ADME/PK
L241: **The Applicant's Position:** The nonclinical PK and toxicokinetic (TK) studies demonstrate that inavolisib has a favorable absorption, distribution, metabolism, excretion (ADME) profile and characteristics consistent with a compound that can be delivered orally to achieve the targeted clinical exposure. Inavolisib was well absorbed across preclinical species, with bioavailability ranging from 57.5\% to 100\%. Metabolism of inavolisib was minimal and no human-specific metabolites were observed in *in vitro* studies and in plasma. *In vitro* studies suggested a low potential for drug-drug interaction (DDI) involving cytochrome P450s (CYPs) or drug transporters at clinically relevant concentrations.
L242: See Table 3 and Table 4 for integrated summaries of area under the concentration-time curve (AUC) and C_{max} parameters of toxicology studies. See Table 5 for exposure multiples from toxicology studies.
L243: Absorption
Determination of Mass Balance and Pharmacokinetics Following a Single Oral Administration of [^{14}C]Inavolisib in Male and Female Sprague Dawley Rats. Study 17-2251/4.2.2.5 Excretion.
Inavolisib was absorbed relatively slowly following PO dosing of 3 mg/kg (200 \mu Ci/kg), with a T_{max} of 4h for total radioactivity. Absorption was estimated to be 45.3\% and 50.7\% in male and female rats, respectively. The systemic half-life of total radioactivity was 17.8 and 26.6h for male and female rats, respectively.
Distribution
Tissue Distribution Following a Single Oral Dose of [^{14}C]Inavolisib in Male and Female Long-Evans and Male Sprague Dawley Rats Using Quantitative Whole Body Autoradiography (QWBA). Study 18-2298/4.2.2.3 Distribution.
Determination of In Vitro Protein binding of Inavolisib in CD-1 Mouse, Sprague Dawley Rat, New Zealand Rabbit, Beagle Dog, Cynomolgus Monkey, and Human Plasma, and Human \alpha 1-Acid Glycoprotein (AAG) and Human Serum Albumin (HSA) Solutions Using Rapid Equilibrium Dialysis (RED). Study 15-3820/4.2.2.3 Distribution.
L244: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219249}
L245: ## ITOVEBI {inavolisib}
L246: Following PO administration of 3 mg/kg (200 µCi/kg) ^{14}\text{C}-inavolisib to Sprague-Dawley (albino) rats, the absorption and distribution were rapid, with the majority of peak tissue radioactivity concentrations observed 1 hour post dosing. The endocrine tissues (adrenal gland), excretory tissues (kidney, liver, urinary bladder wall) and GI tract contained the highest distribution of ^{14}\text{C}inavolisib-derived radioactivity. In pigmented rats, distribution of inavolisib-derived radioactivity was comparable to that in albino rats, with the exception of melanin containing tissues, such as uveal tract and skin that presented higher levels of radioactivity. Radioactivity levels were below the quantitation limit at 1344 h post-dose in all tissues, except in the adrenal gland, eye and kidney.
No or minimal radioactivity could be detected in the brain and spinal cord throughout the study.
Plasma protein binding in rank order of highest overall average binding: mouse (25.2% unbound) > rabbit (53.1% unbound), rat (59.8% unbound), and human (63.3% unbound) > dog (69.2% unbound) and monkey (73.4% unbound). The binding to HSA was 74.0% unbound and binding to AAG was 98.2% unbound.

Metabolism
Metabolite Identification for Inavolisib.1.5 in Rat, Dog, and Human Liver Microsomes. Study 16-0871/4.2.2.4 Metabolism.
Metabolite Profiling and Identification of Inavolisib in Plasma, Bile, Urine, and Feces from Intact and Bile Duct Cannulated Male and Female Sprague Dawley Rats Following a Single Oral Dose of ^{14}\text{C}Inavolisib. Study 18-0814/4.2.2.4 Metabolism
After 1 hour incubation, more than 98.8% of inavolisib was recovered in all LMs tested. Three minor metabolites were detected: M1, M2, and M3 (1% of total UV response). Based on structurally informative fragments ions, the following 3 minor metabolites were tentatively identified in human, rat, and dog: oxidative metabolite (M1, + 16 amu), oxidative ring opened metabolite (M2, + 32 amu), and dealkylated and oxidative metabolite (M3, -55 amu). No human unique metabolites were detected.
In vivo, following PO administration of 3 mg/kg (200 µCi/kg) ^{14}\text{C}-inavolisib to rats, GDC-0077 was the most abundant drug-related material in plasma and accounted for an average of 80.6% of extractable radioactivity from plasma. Circulating metabolites were M5 (imidazole ring cleavage) and M27 (- 29 Da) which accounted for an average of 7.3% and 3.6% of extractable radioactivity from plasma, respectively.

Excretion
Determination of Mass Balance and Pharmacokinetics Following a Single Oral Administration of ^{14}\text{C}Inavolisib in Male and Female Sprague Dawley Rats. Study 17-2251/4.2.2.5 Excretion.
A PO dose of 3 mg/kg (200 µCi/kg) ^{14}\text{C}-inavolisib was administered to rats. In intact rats, the primary route of elimination of radioactivity was in the feces (61.9% and 60.5% of the administered dose in male and female rats, respectively). A mean of 25.5% and 32.0% of the administered dose was recovered in the urine of male and female rats, respectively. In bile-duct cannulated (BDC) rats, the primary route of elimination of radioactivity was fecal (43.6% of the administered dose in males and an average of 37.7% in females). An average of 13.1% and 10.8% of the administered dose was recovered in the bile of BDC male and female rats, respectively. An average of 32.2% and 39.9% of the administered dose was recovered in the urine of BDC male and female rats, respectively. The sum of radioactivity recovered in urine and bile from BDC rats indicated that at least 45.3% and 50.7% of the oral dose had been absorbed in male and female rats, respectively.

Pharmacokinetic Drug-Drug Interactions
In Vitro Evaluation of GDC-0077.1-5 as an Inhibitor of Cytochrome P450 (CYP) Enzymes in Human Liver Microsomes. Study 15-2967/4.2.1.4 PK Drug Interactions.
In Vitro Evaluation of GDC 0077.1-5 as an Inhibitor of Cytochrome P450 (CYP) Enzymes in Human Liver Microsomes and Human Hepatocytes. Study 16-0806/4.2.1.4 PK Drug Interactions
In Vitro Interaction Studies of GDC-0077 with human BCRP and MDR1 Efflux (ABC) Transporters and with human MATE1, MATE2-K, OAT1, OAT3, OATP1B1, OATP1B3, OCT1 and OCT2 Uptake Transporters Interaction Studies of inavolisib with human BCRP and MDR1 Efflux (ABC) Transporters and with human MATE1, MATE2-K, OAT1, OAT3, OATP1B1, OATP1B3, OCT1 and OCT2 Uptake Transporters. Study 18-2263/4.2.1.4 PK Drug Interactions.
Evaluation of inavolisib as a Substrate of human ABC Transporters MDR1 and BCRP and Human Solute Carriers (SLC) OAT1, OAT3, OATP1B1, OATP1B3, OCT1, OCT2, MATE1 and MATE2K / 19-1182 / 4.2.2.6 Pharmacokinetic Drug Interactions OATP1B3, OCT1, OCT2, MATE1 and MATE2K. Study 19-1182/4.2.1.4 PK Drug Interactions.
Inavolisib was not a reversible inhibitor of any of the CYPs tested. It displayed mild metabolism-dependent inhibition of CYP3A4 and weak induction of CYP3A4. These results suggested a low likelihood of CYP-related DDI at clinically relevant concentrations.
Inavolisib did not inhibit any of the transporters tested at clinically relevant concentrations. It was substrate of P-gp and BCRP but not a substrate of any of the other transporters tested.

TK data from general toxicology studies
4-week and 13-week repeat dose toxicology and toxicokinetic studies in rats (Studies 15-3303, 18-2456); and in dogs (Studies 15-3288, 18-2455)/4.2.3.2 Repeat-Dose Toxicity.
In general, exposures of inavolisib in dog and rat increased dose proportionally and were similar on Day 1 and after multiple administrations. In rats, exposure was approximately 2-fold higher in females than in males, whereas there were no sex-related differences in exposure in dogs.

L261: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L262: Overall, FDA agrees with the Applicant's interpretation of the ADME/PK data. To calculate the exposure multiples, FDA used the AUC of 1010 ng•h/mL at the recommended dose of 9 mg/day described in the PI for ITOVEBI. Exposure multiples used to support labeling are discussed in the relevant sections of this review.
L263: See the clinical pharmacology Section 6 for information regarding human data and drug interaction studies.
L264: ## 5.5. Toxicology
L265: ## 5.5.1. General Toxicology
L266: The nonclinical safety of inavolisib has been assessed in a comprehensive battery of toxicology studies, including single dose and repeat-dose general toxicology studies (once daily [QD] oral [PO] dosing) of up to 3 months duration in rats and dogs. The majority of findings identified in nonclinical toxicology studies were consistent with the anticipated pharmacologic effects of PI3K inhibition, including hyperglycemia and body weight loss in rats and dogs, inflammation in dogs, bone marrow hypocellularity, atrophy of glandular and reproductive tissues, and eye lens degeneration in rats; and lymphoid depletion and swelling of the lens fibers in the eye in dogs. Findings were generally dose-dependent and reversible, and considered to be clinically monitorable and/or manageable. Lens fiber degeneration observed in some rats (≥ 3.6 times clinical AUC exposure of 1060 ng • hr/mL at 9 mg [determined from PK samples from Study GO39374]) was considered irreversible.
L267: ## A 4-Week Good Laboratory Practice (GLP) Toxicity Study of Inavolisib via Oral Administration in Sprague Dawley Rats with a 4 Week Recovery Period (Study 15-3303 / 4.2.3.2 Repeat Dose Toxicity):
L268: ## Drug-related Findings:
L269: - ≥ 1 mg/kg: Dose-dependent ↓ in body weight gain; mild ↓ monocytes (females), transient ↑ glucose with minimal-mild ↑ cholesterol; ↓ organ weights in pituitary gland, salivary gland, spleen, and liver; microscopic atrophy of mammary gland (minimal-moderate), pituitary gland (pars distalis, minimal), and mandibular salivary gland (minimal)
- ≥ 3 mg/kg: mild ↓ reticulocytes; minimal-mild ↑ creatinine, total bilirubin, globulins, and ↓ phosphorus, ALP; ↓ organ weights in thymus, prostate gland, uterus, thyroid gland, and kidney; minimal-mild ↓ hematopoiesis and minimal ↑ pigment-laden macrophages in spleen; minimal atrophy of Harderian gland (females), uterus, and vagina
L270: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219249}
L271: ## {Inavolisib}
L272: 10 mg/kg: body weight loss and thin appearance; ↓ platelets and eosinophils, ↑ lymphocytes (males only); ↑ fibrinogen (males only); transient hyperglycemia and glucosuria; minimal-mild ↑ BUN, ALT, AST and ↓ potassium; ↓ organ weights in seminal vesicles, testes, epididymis, ovary, lung, and heart; microscopic atrophy in the exocrine pancreas (minimal-mild), prostate and seminal vesicles (minimal); minimal pancreatic islet hypertrophy and angiectasia; adverse bone marrow hypocellularity and eye lens fiber degeneration.
L273: - At recovery necropsy, all findings had fully or partially recovered, except eye lens degeneration at 10 mg/kg.
L274: ## 13-Week GLP Multiple Dose Oral Gavage Toxicity and Toxicokinetic Study of Inavolisib in Sprague Dawley Rats with No Recovery (Study 18-2456 / 4.2.3.2 Repeat Dose Toxicity):
L275: ## Drug-related Findings:
L276: - Well tolerated up to 5 mg/kg.
- ≥ 1.5 mg/kg: dose-dependent ↓ in body weight gain; mild ↓ decreased red cell mass, leukocytes, lymphocytes, neutrophils; slight ↑ APTT; ↓ organ weights in the pituitary, prostate, thyroid, mandibular gland, spleen, and epididymis; very slight atrophy of pituitary gland (pars distalis), prostate gland; very slight ↑ in pigmented macrophages in the spleen
- 5 mg/kg: hyperglycemia and glucosuria; ↓ basophils and eosinophils; ↓ organ weights in the adrenal gland, liver, kidney (males), testis; very slight bone marrow hypocellularity; very slight-slight atrophy of exocrine pancreas and vagina; very slight ↓ in ovarian follicles; very slight ↓ cellularity of splenic red pulp; adverse interruption/alteration of estrus cycle in females
L277: ## 4-Week GLP Multiple Dose Oral Gavage Toxicity and Toxicokinetic Study of Inavolisib in Dogs with a 4-Week Recovery Phase (Study 15-3288 / 4.2.3.2 Repeat Dose Toxicity):
L278: ## Drug-related Findings:
L279: - Tolerated up to 1.5 mg/kg; Intolerability at 5/3 mg/kg due to multi-organ, ocular inflammation.
- ≥ 0.5 mg/kg: Dose-dependent body weight loss; abnormal feces; transient hyperglycemia; minimal-mild ↓ BUN; ↓ thymus weight; minimal mixed cell inflammation in the esophagus and rectum and ↑ incidence of neutrophils in mandibular lymph node with correlating minimal-mild ↑ neutrophils, fibrinogen; slight-moderate lymphoid depletion.
- ≥ 1.5 mg/kg: vomitus, thin appearance; glucosuria; mild ↓ reticulocytes; minimal ↓ creatinine; minimal-mild ↑ cholesterol; ↓ phosphorus; ↑ liver weights (males); minimal-marked multi-organ inflammation (gastrointestinal tract, mandibular lymph node, eyes, blood vessels, gall bladder, urinary bladder), with correlating ↑ cellularity of the bone marrow and minimal-mild ↑ monocytes; slight ↑ in the liver; minimal-slight inspissation of seminiferous tubules in the testis.
- 5/3 mg/kg: ophthalmic anterior segment inflammation; ↑ body temperature; minimal ↑ AST, minimal-moderate ↑ ALT, ALP, GGT; mild ↑ bilirubin; minimal ↓ red cell mass, RDW; minimal-mild ↑ WBC, lymphocytes, platelets; ↓ albumin, albumin:globulin ratio, ↑ globulin; morbidity due to multi-organ inflammation (eyes, lung, heart, liver); multinucleated spermatids in testis and epididymal degeneration and necrosis.
- At recovery necropsy, all findings exhibited reversibility with the exception of inspissation of seminiferous tubules in the testis
L280: ## 13-week GLP Multiple Dose Oral Gavage Toxicity and Toxicokinetic Study of GDC-0077 in Dogs with 12-Week Recovery (18-2455 / 4.2.3.2 Repeat Dose Toxicity):
L281: ## Drug-related Findings:
L282: - Intolerability at 1.5/1.0 mg/kg due to body weight loss and marked hyperglycemia.
- 0.3 mg/kg: mild ↑ platelets (females); very slight lens fiber swelling in the eye.
- 1.5/1.0 mg/kg: reddish conjunctiva; body weight loss; ophthalmologic opacity at posterior capsule/cortex of the lens of the eye without microscopic correlates; hyperglycemia with associated glucosuria, ↑ urine volume, hypertrophy/vacuolation of pancreatic islet cells, and, in one animal, centrilobular vacuolation/degeneration in the liver, tubular vacuolation in the kidney, and vacuolation of the lens of the eye; mild ↑ ALP, mild ↓ BUN, creatinine; mild-moderate platelets; and very slight-slight focal inflammation in tongue, rectum, and eyes accompanied with clinical pathology changes consistent with inflammation (↑ globulins, WBC, neutrophils, fibrinogen and ↓ albumin/globulin ratio, albumin).
- At recovery necropsy (males only), all inavolisib-findings demonstrated full reversibility
L283: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L284: In general, FDA agrees with the Applicant's summary of the results from the toxicology studies. Additional data and discussions are provided below.
L285: ## 4-Week rat study (Study 15-3303):
L286: The histopathology finding in the eye included minimal lens degeneration in 2/10 female rats and retina rosette in 1/10 female rats at 10 mg/kg/day (6.3 times the human exposure at the recommended dose of 9 mg/day based on AUC). At the recovery necropsy, mild lens
L287: degeneration in the eye was observed in 1/4 male rats at 10 mg/kg/day (3.8 times the human exposure at the recommended dose of 9 mg/day based on AUC on Day 28).
L288: ## 13-Week rat study (Study 18-2456)
L289: Study initiation date:	January 29, 2019
Duration:	13 Weeks
GLP compliance:	Yes
Drug, lot#, and % purity:	GDC-0077, lot # 68878-287, 98.9% purity
L290: ## Methods
