L306: ## 5.5.2. Genetic Toxicology
L307: **The Applicant's Position:** Inavolisib was considered not mutagenic in *S. typhimurium* strains (TA98, TA100, TA1535, and TA1537) or *E. coli* (WP2 uvrA) in the absence or presence of an exogenous metabolic activation system (S-9) (GLP Studies 15-3356 and 16-1286/4.2.3.3.1 *In Vitro* Genotoxicity).
L308: Inavolisib induced micronuclei *in vitro* in human peripheral blood lymphocytes (via a clastogenic mechanism) at concentrations \ge 180 \mu\text{g/mL} in the absence of a rat liver metabolic activation system (S-9) when tested for 24 + 24 hours (incubation plus recovery), with the no-effect level between 120 and 180 \mu\text{g/mL}. Inavolisib did not induce micronuclei when tested up to the limit of solubility (350 \mu\text{g/mL}) for 3 + 21 hours in the absence or presence of S-9 (GLP Study 15-3357/4.2.3.3.1 *In Vitro* Genotoxicity).
L309: There was no evidence of inavolisib-induced clastogenicity or aneugenicity in polychromatic erythrocytes of the bone marrow, or DNA damage in the liver of female Sprague-Dawley rats (which showed higher exposure than males) up to the MTD of 40 mg/kg, with a corresponding Day 1 C_{\text{max}} of 1740 ng/mL and area under the concentration-time curve from 0 to 24 hours (\text{AUC}_{0-24}) of 17,100 ng hr/mL (\sim 16.1\times clinical exposure at 9 mg) (GLP Study 16-0989/4.2.3.3.1 *In Vivo* Genotoxicity).
L310: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L311: FDA agrees that inavolisib was not mutagenic in the Ames Assay. In Study 15-3356, inavolisib showed weak evidence of inducing mutation in strain TA98 of *Salmonella typhimurium* only in the presence of S-9. Treatment of strain TA98 in the presence of S-9 at 1600 \mu\text{g/plate} and
L312: above resulted in an increase in revertant numbers that was 2.1-fold the concurrent control in Experiment 1 and up to 1.9-fold the concurrent control in Experiment 2. The increase in revertant numbers in Experiment 2 did not reach 2.0-fold but was concentration-related and reproducible and is, therefore, considered evidence of weak mutagenic activity in this strain. As a follow-up, the Applicant conducted another Ames assay (study 16-1286) with inavolisib in strain TA98 in the presence of S-9 using lots G02967907.1-20 (same lot as in study 15-3356, purity 98.1%) and 60470-32 (purified lot, purity 100%) in both plate incorporation and pre-incubation methodologies. No increase in revertant numbers \ge 2\times the concurrent vehicle control was observed using either the plate incorporation or pre-incubation method for either lot of inavolisib.
L313: FDA agrees that inavolisib was clastogenic in an in vitro human lymphocyte micronucleus assay and that inavolisib was not genotoxic in an in vivo rat bone marrow micronucleus assay and did not induce DNA break in a liver comet assay.
L314: ## 5.5.3. Carcinogenicity
L315: The Applicant's Position: Inavolisib is intended for the treatment of patients with advanced cancer. In accordance with ICH S9 guidance on the Nonclinical Evaluation for Anticancer Pharmaceuticals (ICH 2009), no carcinogenicity studies were conducted.
L316: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L317: FDA agrees that carcinogenicity studies are not warranted to support the proposed indication in patients with advanced cancer in accordance with ICH S9 guidance.
L318: ## 5.5.4. Reproductive and Developmental Toxicology
L319: The Applicant's Position: The embryo-fetal development study in rats identified inavolisib-related dose dependent effects on embryo-fetal development (\ge 0.8\times) that included decreases in fetal body weight and placental weight, post-implantation loss, lower fetal viability, and teratogenicity (fetal external, visceral, and skeletal malformations) (Study 19-0593).
L320: The ICH S9 guidance (ICH 2009) states that studies on (1) fertility and early embryonic development and (2) pre- and postnatal toxicology are not warranted to support marketing authorization applications for pharmaceuticals intended for the treatment of patients with advanced cancer, such as inavolisib. Accordingly, such studies were not performed.
L321: Assessment of male and female reproductive organ was conducted as a part of the 4- and 13-week toxicity studies in rats and dogs (Studies 15-3303, 15-3288, 18-2456, and 18-2455/4.2.3.2 Repeat Dose Toxicity). In male rats, dose-dependent atrophy of the prostate and seminal vesicle and decreased organ weights without microscopic correlate in the epididymis and testis were observed (\ge 0.4\times, Study 15-3303). In the 1-month toxicity study in dogs, focal
L322: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219249}
L323: ## {Inavolisib}
L324: inspissation of seminiferous tubule contents and multinucleated spermatids in the testis and epithelial degeneration/necrosis in the epididymis were observed (\ge 2.0 \times, Study 15-3288). However, there were no inavolisib-related microscopic findings in the testes or epididymides or effects on sperm concentration, motility, or morphology in the 3-month dog toxicity study at similar exposures (Study 18-2455). In female rats, minimal to mild and reversible atrophy in the uterus and vagina and decreased ovarian follicles were observed (\ge 1.1 \times) in the 4-week rat toxicity study (Study 15-3303); and findings suggestive of an interruption/alteration of the estrus cycle were observed (\ge 1.5 \times) in the 3-month rat toxicity study (Study 18-2456).
L325: ## A GLP Preliminary Embryo-fetal Development Study of GDC-0077 by Oral Gavage in Sprague Dawley Rats (19-0593 / 4.2.3.5.2 Embryo-fetal Development):
L326: ## Drug-related Findings:
L327: - Maternal \ge 2 mg/kg: non-adverse lower body weight gain and transient glucose increases.
- Embryo-fetal development:\ge 2 mg/kg: adverse decreases in fetal body weight and placental weight
- 6 mg/kg: higher post-implantation loss, lower fetal viability, and teratogenicity (external, visceral, and skeletal malformations, increased skeletal and visceral variations, delayed skeletal ossifications).
L328: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L329: FDA agrees with the Applicant's summary of the study results described above.
L330: FDA also agrees that fertility and early embryonic development and pre- and postnatal toxicology studies are not warranted to support the proposed indication in patients with advanced cancer in accordance with ICH S9 guidance.

L338: ## 5.5.5. Other Toxicology Studies
L339: ## Phototoxicity
L340: **The Applicant's Position:** The phototoxic potential of inavolisib was assessed in a GLP neutral red uptake phototoxicity assay in BALB/c 3T3 mouse fibroblasts (16-1227 / 4.2.3.7.7). Inavolisib did not reduce the viability of 3T3 cells exposed to ultraviolet radiation as compared with that in the absence of ultraviolet radiation up to the highest concentration tested of 31.6 µg/mL. Therefore, it was concluded that inavolisib did not show phototoxicity potential in this assay.
L341: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L342: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.
L343: ## Evaluation of Degradant
L344: (b) (4) is a degradant
L345: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L346: (b) (4) mutagenic in the bacterial mutagenesis assay (Study 1107383). However, in the 28-day rat Pig-a gene mutation assay with inavolisib, there were no biologically relevant increases in Pig-a gene mutation in either mature erythrocytes or immature reticulocytes in the peripheral blood up to 20 mg/kg, MTD under the assay conditions employed (Study 21-1488 / 4.2.3.7.6 Impurities). The use of inavolisib in the
L347: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219249}
L348: {Inavolisib}
L349: rat *Pig-a* assay is justified, as the AUC ratio of [REDACTED] (b) (4) to inavolisib was approximately 2.5% in rat plasma (Study 21-1488). In contrast, in humans, circulating metabolites were minimal with the majority (99.5%) of drug related material being inavolisib (See 2.6.4 Pharmacokinetic Written Summary, Section 5, and 2.7.2 Summary of Clinical Pharmacology Studies, Section 2.3.1.2) (Study 22-0069 [5.3.2.2, Other Human Biomaterials]; GP42652/5.3.4.1 Healthy Subject PD and PK/PD). Thus, inavolisib and [REDACTED] (b) (4) (at clinically relevant circulating exposure) do not pose biologically relevant mutagenic risks in mammals *in vivo*.
L350: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L351: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.
L352: ## Impurities (Including Mutagenic Impurities)
L353: **The Applicant's Position:** Impurities in the drug substance (inavolisib) and drug product are controlled according to ICH Q3A and ICH Q3B, respectively. All specified and unspecified impurities in the drug substance are controlled with individual acceptance criteria lower than or equal to the ICH Q3A qualification threshold, except for impurities [REDACTED] (b) (4)
L354: [REDACTED] (b) (4) were qualified in a 28-day GLP toxicity study (15-3288/4.2.3.2 Repeat-Dose Toxicity) that justifies higher limits than the ICH Q3A qualification threshold. Degradation products are controlled in the drug product with individual acceptance criteria lower than or equal to the ICH Q3B qualification threshold. Refer to Module 3, 3.2.P.5.6 Justification of Specification(s).
L355: In silico mutagenicity assessments were conducted with inavolisib and impurities of inavolisib using standard in silico structure-activity relationship software programs Derek for Nexus® (Version 6.1.0) and Leadscope Statistical bacterial mutation model (Version 2) in combination with expert review. One impurity [REDACTED] (b) (4) was shown to be experimentally non-mutagenic in a 5-strain non-GLP compliant bacterial reverse mutation assay (Study 15-3392/4.2.3.7.6).
L356: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L357: FDA agrees with Applicant's position. Additional details are provided below.
L358: An internal CDER (Q)SAR consult showed that [REDACTED] (b) (4) is predicted to be negative for bacterial mutagenicity. [REDACTED] (b) (4) was predicted to be positive for bacterial mutagenicity by Derek Nexus 6.3.0. However, it was not mutagenic in a non-GLP compliant Ames assay, and it showed a negative prediction for mutagenicity by CASE Ultra 1.9.0.8 GT1.BMUT model 1.9.0.2 and equivocal prediction by Leadscope Model Applier 2023.0.0-12 Bacterial Mut v2 model (LMA).
L359: [REDACTED] (b) (4) was detected in higher amounts during process development and in development
L360: batches. The Applicant proposed the limit of (b)(4)% w/w based on qualification of this impurity in the GLP-compliant 4-week toxicology study in dogs. (b)(4) was not mutagenic in a non-GLP-compliant Ames assay. (b)(4) was present at (b)(4) area% in inavolisib Batch G02967907.1-20 used in the 28-day GLP repeat-dose toxicology studies in rats (15-3303) and dogs (15-3288). In these studies, (b)(4) was qualified up to (b)(4) mg/kg/day ((b)(4) mg/day HED based on a 60-kg patient) and (b)(4) mg/kg/day ((b)(4) mg/day HED based on a 60-kg patient), based on the highest dose levels of 3 mg/kg/day inavolisib and 1.5 mg/kg/day inavolisib administered to rats and dogs, respectively. All finding at these dose levels were reversible except for necropsy findings of inspissation of seminiferous tubules in the testis in dogs. The maximum proposed daily dose of inavolisib is 9 mg/day and the proposed specification limit of (b)(4)% w/w is predicted to result in a maximum daily intake of (b)(4) mg/day of the impurity (b)(4). The potential maximum intake of (b)(4) is below the levels qualified in the GLP-compliant 4-week studies in dogs.
L361: The Applicant proposed the limit of (b)(4)% w/w based on qualification of this impurity in the GLP-compliant repeat-dose toxicology study in dogs (15-3288). (b)(4) was present at (b)(4) area% in inavolisib Batch G02967901.1-20 used in the 28-day GLP repeat-dose toxicology studies in rats (15-3303) and dogs (15-3288). In these studies, (b)(4) was qualified up to (b)(4) mg/kg/day ((b)(4) mg/day HED based on a 60-kg patient) and (b)(4) mg/kg/day ((b)(4) mg/day HED based on a 60-kg patient), based on the highest dose levels of 3 mg/kg/day inavolisib and 1.5 mg/kg/day inavolisib administered to rats and dogs, respectively. L366: ## 6 Clinical Pharmacology
L367: ## 6.1. Executive Summary
L368: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L369: (b) (4)
L370: The Applicant is seeking approval of inavolisib
L371: The proposed recommended dosage is inavolisib 9 mg orally (PO) once daily (QD) in combination with palbociclib and fulvestrant at their approved recommended dosages.
L372: The clinical pharmacology review addressed the following three key review issues:
L373: **Dosage Acceptability:** Although the clinical pharmacology review concluded that the proposed dosage is approvable from the clinical pharmacology perspective, the proposed dosage of inavolisib 9 mg PO QD may not be optimized due to 1) high rates of adverse events (AEs) and dose modifications, 2) positive exposure-response (E-R) relationships for safety, 3) limited clinical data at lower dosages, and 4) lack of E-R relationships for efficacy in patients who received the proposed dosage in combination with palbociclib and fulvestrant.
L374: **Intrinsic Factors:** Dosage reduction is recommended for patients with moderate renal impairment. A postmarketing requirement (PMR) will be issued for the complete study report (CSR) and datasets for the ongoing renal impairment study to include evaluation of severe renal impairment. FDA cannot rule out &gt;20% contribution of hepatic elimination; therefore, a PMR will be issued for a dedicated hepatic impairment study. Lastly, there was underrepresentation of patients from diverse racial and ethnic groups including Black/African American or Hispanic/Latino patients in the inavolisib development program. A postmarketing commitment (PMC) to evaluate inavolisib in a diverse patient population representative of the US population will include collection of clinical pharmacology data to assess potential impacts of race and ethnicity on inavolisib pharmacokinetics (PK) and pharmacodynamics (PD).
L375: **DDI Potential:** There is low risk for CYP-based DDIs for inavolisib as an object given the lack of CYP enzyme metabolism. However, in vitro studies showed that inavolisib is a time-dependent inhibitor of CYP3A and inavolisib induces CYP3A and CYP2B6. PMCs will be issued to evaluate inavolisib clinical induction potential on CYP3A and CYP2B6. Depending on the results of the CYP3A study, additional studies may be warranted to evaluate the potential for inavolisib to induce CYP2C enzymes and interaction with combined oral contraceptives (COC).
L376: ## Recommendations
L377: The Office of Clinical Pharmacology has reviewed the information contained in NDA 219249. This NDA is approvable from a Clinical Pharmacology perspective. The key review issues with specific recommendations/comments are summarized in **Table 6**. Clinical Pharmacology PMRs and PMCs are summarized in **Table 7**.
L378: Table 6: Key Clinical Pharmacology Review Issues, Recommendations and Comments
L379: REVIEW ISSUE	RECOMMENDATIONS/COMMENTS
Pivotal evidence of effectiveness	The primary evidence of effectiveness comes from the Phase 3 randomized, placebo-controlled trial (WO41554) in patients with PIK3CA-mutant, HR+/HER2- locally advanced or metastatic breast cancer. The study met the primary endpoint for improvement in progression-free survival (PFS) with a 7.7 month improvement in median PFS (15.0 months for inavolisib + palbociclib + fulvestrant vs 7.3 months for placebo + palbociclib + fulvestrant). See Section 8.1.2 for details.
General dosing instructions	The recommended dosage is inavolisib 9 mg PO QD with or without food in combination with palbociclib and fulvestrant at their approved recommended dosages.
Dosing in patient subgroups (intrinsic and extrinsic factors)	Renal impairment: Reduce the inavolisib starting dosage to 6 mg PO QD in patients with moderate renal impairment (estimated glomerular filtration rate [eGFR] 30 to <60 mL/min). No dosage modification is recommended in patients with mild renal impairment (eGFR 60 to <90 mL/min). A dedicated renal impairment study is ongoing to evaluate the safety and PK of inavolisib in participants with severe renal impairment (eGFR <30 mL/min); a PMR will be issued for the CSR and datasets from this study.
Hepatic impairment: No dosage modification is recommended in patients with mild hepatic impairment (total bilirubin > upper limit of normal (ULN) to ≤ 1.5 x ULN or AST > ULN and total bilirubin ≤ ULN). The PK and safety of inavolisib in patients with moderate or severe hepatic impairment has not been characterized. A PMR will be issued for a hepatic impairment study to identify a safe dosage of inavolisib in patients with moderate or severe hepatic impairment.
Race and ethnicity: The majority of patients who received inavolisib and had PK data available were White (224/336; 66.7%), Asian (62/336; 18.5%), or Unknown (45/336; 13.4%) race. Few patients were Black/African American (4/336; 1.2%), Hispanic/Latino (14/336, 4.2%), or other underrepresented minority populations. A PMC will be issued to evaluate inavolisib in a diverse patient population more representative of the US population. Clinical pharmacology data will be collected in the PMC study (or
L380: studies) to evaluate the effects of race and ethnicity on the PK and PD of inavolisib.PIK3CA mutations: The results suggested that inavolisib plus palbociclib and fulvestrant treatment is effective for patients regardless of PIK3CA mutated exon status.

Drug-drug interactions (DDI)	Two PMCs will be issued to conduct clinical study/studies to address the induction potential of inavolisib on CYP3A and CYP2B6. If the induction potential of inavolisib on CYP3A is confirmed, the Applicant may need to address the induction potential of inavolisib on CYP2C enzymes and DDI potential between inavolisib and COCs.
Bridge between the to be marketed and clinical trial formulations	The Applicant utilized dissolution studies to bridge the to-be-marketed and clinical trial formulations. Refer to the Biopharmaceutics Review for details.
Labeling	FDA labeling revisions per regulations and FDA guidances were provided. Overall, the proposed labeling language in the Clinical Pharmacology pertinent sections are generally acceptable upon the Applicant's agreement to the FDA revisions.

L385: ## 6.2. Summary of Clinical Pharmacology Assessment
L386: ## 6.2.1. Pharmacology and Clinical Pharmacokinetics
L387: **Data and the Applicant's Position:** The clinical pharmacology evaluation of inavolisib is based on data from two clinical studies in patients: a Phase I Study GO39374 (Report 1125233) and a Phase III Study WO41554 (Report 1126696), as well as a Phase I clinical pharmacology Study in healthy subjects, GP42652 that evaluated human mass balance and absolute bioavailability (Report 1130181).
L388: The clinical pharmacology of inavolisib has been well characterized. The data included in the dossier encompasses the following:
L389: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219249}
## {Inavolisib}
L390: - Pharmacokinetic (PK) properties following single dose and multiple doses of inavolisib administered as a single agent and with combination agents in subjects with cancer.
- PK properties following a single dose administered as a single agent in healthy subjects.
- Population PK analyses of single agent and combination studies included in the dossier.
- The results from clinical pharmacology assessments conducted in healthy subjects (mass balance and absolute bioavailability) to describe the ADME properties of inavolisib in humans.
- The results from clinical pharmacology assessments conducted in subjects with cancer to assess intrinsic (mild hepatic impairment and mild renal impairment) and extrinsic factors (food effect and effect of acid reducing agents) that may affect the PK of inavolisib.
- In addition, the outcomes of the exposure-efficacy and exposure-safety analyses conducted using data from Phase III Study WO41554 and supportive Phase I Study GO39374, which were used for the Phase III dose selection and justification in cancer subjects, support the use of the recommended dose of 9 mg inavolisib, administered orally, QD, on a continuous schedule.
L391: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L392: FDA agrees with the Applicant's summary of submitted clinical pharmacology data.
L393: In addition, during review of NDA 219249, the Applicant submitted an interim CSR for Study GP44944, an ongoing single-dose study to evaluate the effect of renal impairment on inavolisib PK. The interim CSR included data from participants with moderate renal impairment and normal renal function.
L394: FDA agrees that the proposed dosage of inavolisib 9 mg PO QD in combination with palbociclib and fulvestrant is approvable for the granted indication; however, FDA does not agree that the proposed dosage is optimized. See Section 6.1.2 for details on the FDA's position and recommendation for further dosage optimization of inavolisib.
L395: ## 6.2.2. General Dosing and Therapeutic Individualization
L396: ## 6.2.2.1. General Dosing
L397: **Data:** A dose of 9 mg QD is the recommended dose for oral administration of inavolisib in combination with palbociclib and endocrine therapy in patients with *PIK3CA*-mutated, HR+, HER2– LA/mBC.
L398: - Data from Phase I Dose Escalation/Expansion Study GO39374:9 mg QD was the MTD of inavolisib as identified during dose escalation (3 - 12 mg QD) in the dose escalation and expansion Phase 1b Study GO39374 (Stage 1 Arm A).
L399: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219249}
## {Inavolisib}
L400: - 9 mg QD showed numerically higher objective response rate (ORR) and clinical benefit rate (CBR) compared to 6 mg as a monotherapy, in doublet combination (with an endocrine agent), and in triplet combination (with a CDK4/6 inhibitor and endocrine agent) in limited number of patients evaluated (GO39374 CSR).
- Data from Phase III Study WO41554:Inavolisib at a dose of 9 mg QD showed a statistically significant and clinically meaningful improvement in investigator-assessed PFS for Inavo+Palbo+Fulv versus Pbo+Palbo+Fulv in the randomized, placebo controlled Phase III Study WO41554 (Section 8.1.1).Stratified HR = 0.43; 95% CI: 0.32, 0.59; log-rank p – value &lt; 0.0001
- Median PFS was 15.0 months in the Inavo + Palbo + Fulv arm vs. 7.3 months in the Pbo + Palbo + Fulv arm.
- Exposure-efficacy analyses supported 9 mg QD inavolisib as an efficacious dose in the context of the positive topline efficacy results for treatment versus (vs.) placebo.Inavolisib exposure was not significantly associated with ORR, best overall objective response (BOR), duration of response (DOR), CBR, PFS, or time to first subsequent therapy (TFST) based on Study WO41554, after adjustment to covariates, indicating that patients benefited similarly across the range of observed exposures following 9 mg QD.
- High dose intensity (&gt;90%) seen across all exposure quartiles of inavolisib supports the 9 mg QD dose from a safety and tolerability perspective. The higher risk for hyperglycemia (GO39374) and anemia (WO41554) was observed with higher inavolisib exposure, but these AEs were manageable with standard clinical measures. The discontinuation rate of inavolisib due to AEs was low (6.2% in WO41554).
L401: **The Applicant's Position:** At the proposed 9 mg QD dose of inavolisib, Study WO41554 demonstrated a compelling clinical benefit of inavolisib in combination with palbociclib and fulvestrant, and the treatment regimen demonstrated good tolerability. These results were further supported by the anti-tumor activity and manageable safety and tolerability in Study GO39374. Based on the totality of this information, the recommended dosing regimen for inavolisib is 9 mg QD dose, administered orally, with or without food.
L402: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L403: FDA agrees that inavolisib 9 mg PO QD in combination with palbociclib and fulvestrant is approvable in adult patients with HR+/HER2-, locally advanced or metastatic breast cancer following recurrence on or after adjuvant therapy
L404: (b) (4)
L405: with palbociclib and fulvestrant).(b) (4) (i.e., inavolisib in combination
L406: FDA does not consider that the Applicant has provided sufficient justification that inavolisib 9 mg PO QD is the optimized dosage for the target patient population based on the following reasons:
L407: ## 1) High Rate of AEs and Dosage Modifications
L408: Multiple common AEs in the pivotal study (i.e., increase blood glucose, stomatitis/mucosal inflammation, and diarrhea) were observed at high rates in patients who received inavolisib + palbociclib + fulvestrant and at higher incidence in the treatment arm than those in the control arm (Table 8). Neutropenia, a known toxicity of palbociclib, was similar between the two arms. In addition, 69% of patients in the inavolisib + palbociclib + fulvestrant arm had at least one inavolisib interruption with 84% (595/711) of the inavolisib dose interruptions due to AEs (Table 9). These results suggest that the proposed dosing regimen for inavolisib in combination with palbociclib and fulvestrant is poorly tolerated.

L416: ## 2) Positive E-R Relationships for Safety
L417: E-R analyses for safety identified positive E-R relationships for probability of any Grade \ge 3 AEs (Figure 4), Grade \ge 2 increased blood glucose, and dosage modification due to AE based on data from the inavolisib monotherapy dose escalation in Study GO39374 at the dosage range of 6 mg PO QD to 12 mg PO QD and a positive E-R relationship for probability of Grade \ge 2 anemia (Figure 5) based on data from inavolisib 9 mg PO QD + palbociclib + fulvestrant in Study WO41554 (See Section 19.4.2.3 Exposure Response Analysis for details).

L423: ## 3) Limited Clinical Data at Lower Dosages
L424: In stage 1 of Arm A (inavolisib monotherapy) in Study GO39374, patients received 6, 9, and 12 mg PO QD dosage levels. There were 2 dose-limiting toxicities (DLTs) at the 12 mg PO QD dose level and therefore 9 mg PO QD was identified as the maximum tolerated dose (MTD) of inavolisib monotherapy. Only 7 patients received a starting inavolisib monotherapy dosage of 6 mg PO QD and no patients received a starting inavolisib monotherapy dosage &lt;6 mg PO QD (Table 10). Multiple doublet and triplet combination regimens were subsequently studied in stage 1 of Study GO39374. For inavolisib in combination with palbociclib and letrozole (Arm B), there were three patients each who received inavolisib at 3 mg PO QD and 6 mg PO QD. In total, there were only 20 patients across all regimens who received inavolisib starting dosages lower than the proposed 9 mg PO QD dosage. Of note, all patients who received inavolisib in combination with palbociclib and fulvestrant received the proposed 9 mg PO QD dosage.

L430: ## 4) Lack of E-R Relationships for Efficacy
L431: No apparent E-R relationships for efficacy endpoints (PFS, duration of response [DoR], time to first subsequent cancer therapy after treatment [TFST], clinical benefit rate [CBR], overall response rate [ORR], and best overall response [BOR]) were identified based on analysis of data from Study WO41554; however, the E-R efficacy results should be interpreted with caution given that the proposed triplet combination (inavolisib + palbociclib + fulvestrant) was only studied at one inavolisib dose level (9 mg PO QD). While the Applicant notes numerically higher ORR for inavolisib 9 mg PO QD compared to inavolisib 6 mg PO QD in Study GO39374, there were too few patients treated with starting dosages &lt;9 mg PO QD to evaluate dose-activity relationships.
L432: Taken together, the available data support approval of the proposed recommended dosage of inavolisib 9 mg PO QD but do not support the conclusion that the inavolisib dosage was optimized. A PMR will be issued to study a lower dosage of inavolisib in patients with PIK3CA-mutant, HR+/HER2-locally advanced or metastatic breast cancer to determine whether a lower dosage may improve the benefit-risk profile compared to inavolisib 9 mg PO QD.
L433: ## 6.2.2.2. Therapeutic Individualization
L434: **Data:** Following 9 mg QD dosing, body weight, age, race (Asian vs. non-Asian), mild hepatic impairment, and mild renal impairment were not associated with clinically relevant differences in inavolisib exposure parameters. Given the totality of data, dose adjustment of inavolisib in these specific populations is not warranted.
L435: No clinically relevant food effect (high-fat meal) was observed on inavolisib PK in Study GO39374. Hence, inavolisib can be administered with or without food. Coadministration of inavolisib with proton-pump inhibitors in Study GO39374 did not have a clinically relevant effect on the exposure of inavolisib. Hence, no dose adjustment of inavolisib is needed when it is co-administered with acid-reducing agents.
L436: No DDIs were observed between inavolisib and combination partners used in studies GO39374 and WO41554, namely palbociclib, fulvestrant, or letrozole. Therefore, 9 mg QD inavolisib can
L437: be co-administered with these agents at their respective indicated dose levels. Similarly, no dose adjustment of inavolisib is needed when it is co-administered with these agents.
L438: Nonclinical *in vitro* data, clinical endogenous biomarker data, and physiologically-based pharmacokinetic (PBPK) modeling suggest that clinically relevant DDI between inavolisib and CYP3A4 substrates is unlikely. Therefore, inavolisib can be co-administered with CYP3A4 substrates at their respective indicated dose levels.
L439: Based on the ADME properties of inavolisib, the role of P-gp and breast cancer resistance protein (BCRP) transporters in *in vivo* disposition of inavolisib is not expected to be clinically significant. Therefore, inavolisib can be co-administered with inhibitors/inducers of P-gp and/or BCRP at their respective indicated dose levels.
L440: Similarly, based on the ADME properties of inavolisib, hepatic impairment is considered not to have a clinically significant impact on the PK of inavolisib. Hence, dose adjustment of inavolisib in patients with hepatic impairment (including mild, moderate, and severe) is not warranted.
L441: Population PK modeling suggested that mild renal impairment does not have a clinically significant impact on inavolisib exposure. Therefore, no dose adjustment is needed in patients with mild renal impairment. To assess if the dose of inavolisib needs to be adjusted in patients with moderate or severe renal impairment, a dedicated clinical renal impairment study is being conducted in these patients.
L442: **The Applicant's Position:** No therapeutic individualization is needed in the proposed indication based on demographic factors (body weight, age, sex), lack of food effect, lack of potential DDIs, and lack of clinically relevant impact of hepatic impairment (mild, moderate, severe) or renal impairment (mild) on inavolisib PK. Moderate or severe renal impairment may affect inavolisib PK, hence a dedicated clinical renal impairment study is being conducted in these patients.
L443: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L444: FDA agrees with the Applicant that no dose adjustment is necessary in the proposed indication based on body weight (39 to 159kg), age (27 to 85 years), sex, mild renal impairment (eGFR 60 to &lt;90 mL/min), and mild hepatic impairment. In addition, FDA agrees that neither a high-fat meal (approximately 1,000 calories with 50% from fat) nor concomitant use of a proton pump inhibitor have clinically significant effects on the PK of inavolisib (see Section 6.3.2.4 for details). FDA also assessed therapeutic individualization in the following clinical pharmacology area of interest:
L445: ## **1) Moderate renal impairment**
L446: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219249}
## {Inavolisib}
L447: Per the population PK (popPK) analysis, moderate renal impairment (creatinine clearance [CrCL] 30 to &lt;60 mL/min) increased inavolisib exposure (AUC_{0-\tau,ss} by 42%). During review of the NDA, on August 7, 2024, the Applicant submitted the interim CSR for the ongoing clinical renal impairment study. Participants with moderate renal impairment (eGFR 30 to &lt;60 mL/min; n=7) or normal renal function (eGFR &gt;90 mL/min; n=7) received a single dose of inavolisib 6 mg. Inavolisib AUC_{0-\tau} was 60% higher in participants with moderate renal impairment than participants with normal renal function (Table 11). The predicted median steady state AUC_{0-\tau} for patients with moderate renal impairment at 6 mg PO QD dosage was similar to that for patients with normal renal function at 9 mg PO QD dosage (Table 12). Based on these results, the Applicant submitted revised labeling proposing to reduce the proposed starting dosage of inavolisib to 6 mg PO QD in patients with moderate renal impairment. FDA agrees with this recommendation.

L457: Given that inavolisib is renally excreted and no data are available to inform the dosage of inavolisib in patients with severe renal impairment (eGFR &lt;30 mL/min), a PMR will be issued for
L458: the final CSR and datasets for the ongoing dedicated clinical renal impairment study, which includes participants with severe renal impairment.
L459: ## 2) Moderate and severe hepatic Impairment
L460: The Applicant concluded that a dedicated clinical study of inavolisib in patients with moderate or severe hepatic impairment is not warranted due to the following:
L461: - In vitro studies showed that inavolisib is metabolically stable in human liver microsomes (HLM). Of the metabolites recovered in excreta, the majority seem to be formed due to hydrolysis of amide and only ~2% were CYP-mediated metabolites.
- For drugs like inavolisib with low extraction ratio and low protein binding, hepatic clearance is mainly dependent on intrinsic clearance due to hepatic enzyme activity in the liver but not on the hepatic blood flow.
L462: FDA does not agree with the Applicant's conclusion that a hepatic impairment study is not warranted based on the following reasons:
L463: - Per the Mass Balance and Absolute Bioavailability Study GP42652, the absolute oral bioavailability of inavolisib is 76%.
- Circulating metabolites were minimal with the majority (99.5%) of drug-related material in plasma being inavolisib in the human mass balance study. Urine and feces excreta accounts for 96% of total administered dose (Table 13). A mean of 48% of the dose (40% as the parent drug and 8% as metabolites) was recovered in urine, and 48% (10% as the parent drug and 38% as metabolites) was recovered in feces. The amount of drug recovered as metabolites in feces and urine account for approximately 46% of the administered dose. Given the bioavailability and the percentage of inavolisib recovered as metabolites, &gt;20% contribution of hepatic elimination cannot be ruled out. Therefore, a PMR will be issued to evaluate the PK and safety of inavolisib in patients with moderate and severe hepatic impairment.
L464: **Table 13: Inavolisib Excreta Data in Human in Mass Balance Study GP42652**
L465: hAME (%)
Urine	Feces	Total
Parent	40	10	50
Metabolite	8	38	46
Total	48	48	97
L466: Source: Reviewer compiled based on Mass Balance and Absolute Bioavailability Report No. GP42652
L467: ## 3) Race and ethnicity
L468: In the popPK analysis, the majority of patients were White (n=224 [66.7%]) or Asian (n=62 [18.5%]) and there was limited representation of other racial and ethnic groups including Black
L469: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219249}
## {Inavolisib}
L470: or African American (n=4 [1.2%]) and Hispanic or Latino (n=14 [4.2%]) patients (Table 14). Based on published literature, among patients with estrogen receptor-positive (ER+)/HER2-breast cancer, the prevalence of PIK3CA mutations was around 37 – 48% across all ancestry groups, and the distributions of mutation types was also similar across all ancestry groups (Chen et al. 2022); therefore, inavolisib may be utilized in patients from racial or ethnic groups with limited current data. Due to the limited data from patients from underrepresented races and ethnicities, there are insufficient data to assess any potential differences in PK or PD for patients who were not White or Asian. A PMC will be issued to study inavolisib in a diverse patient population representative of the US population (see Section 8.1.1). Collection of clinical pharmacology data to assess potential differences in PK and PD will be included in the PMC.

L474: ## 4) DDI – Inavolisib as an Object
L475: ## i. CYP enzyme inhibitors or inducers
L476: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219249}
## {Inavolisib}
L477: In the mass balance study, &gt;99% of the drug-related material in plasma was parent inavolisib and the only CYP-mediated metabolite identified in vitro (M2) was not observed. Therefore, DDI potential between inavolisib and CYP inhibitors or inducers is considered low given the lack of CYP metabolism observed in vivo.
L478: ## ii. Transporter inhibitors or inducers
L479: In vitro, inavolisib is a substrate of P-gp and BCRP but not a substrate of MATE1, MATE2-K, OAT1, OAT3, OATP1B1, OATP1B3, OCT1, and OCT2. FDA agrees with the Applicant that the DDI potential of inavolisib with P-gp or BCRP inhibitors is low given that the absolute oral bioavailability of inavolisib was 76%.
L480: ## iii. Proton pump inhibitors
L481: Inavolisib exhibits pH-dependent solubility with reduced solubility at higher pH conditions. Based on reviewer's repeat analysis, patients with self-reported concurrent proton pump inhibitor (PPI) use in Study GO339374 (n=19) showed similar inavolisib steady state C_{max} and AUC_{0-24} to patients who self-reported no PPI use (n=56 for C_{max}; n=54 for AUC_{0-24}) (Figure 6). This analysis supports the conclusion that concomitant administration of a PPI does not have clinically meaningful impact on inavolisib PK exposure.
L482: Figure 6: Impact of PPI on Steady State C_{max} or AUC_{0-24} of Inavolisib – FDA Analyzed
L483: AUC_{0-24}: area under the concentration-time curve from 0 to 24 hours; C_{max}: maximum concentration observed; PPI: proton pump inhibitor.
L484: Source: Summary of Biopharmaceutics Studies and Associated Analytical Methods; Figure 4
L485: {Inavolisib}
L486: ## 5) DDI – Inavolisib as a Precipitant
L487: Per in vitro Study 16-0806, inavolisib was not a direct inhibitor of CYP1A2, CYP2B6, CYP2C8, CYP2C9, CYP2C19, CYP2D6, and CYP3A, and was not a time-dependent inhibitor of CYP1A2, CYP2B6, CYP2C8, CYP2C9, CYP2C19, or CYP2D6; however, inavolisib showed potential for time-dependent inhibition of CYP3A. Per in vitro Study 16-0131, inavolisib increased CYP3A4 mRNA up to 40-fold, CYP2B6 mRNA up to 4-fold, and CYP1A2 mRNA up to 2.5-fold at 30 µM inavolisib in each case.
L488: (b) (4)
L489: The Applicant provided the following
L490: (b) (4)
L491: Based on FDA assessment, available data cannot rule out potential induction effect of inavolisib on sensitive CYP3A substrates. See Section 19.4.4 for details. PMCs will be issued to conduct a clinical study or studies to assess inavolisib induction potential on substrates of CYP3A and CYP2B6. If the clinical study with a CYP3A substrate identifies inavolisib as at least a weak inducer of CYP3A in vivo, additional studies may be warranted to evaluate inavolisib induction potential on CYP2C enzymes and the potential for inavolisib to interact with COCs.
L492: ## 6.2.2.3. Outstanding Issues
L493: The Applicant's Position: Not applicable.
L494: The FDA's Assessment:
L495: The following clinical pharmacology PMRs and PMCs will be issued.
L496: - Dosage Optimization PMR: Inadequate justification and supportive data were provided to show that inavolisib 9 mg PO QD is the optimized dosage for the target patient population in combination with palbociclib and fulvestrant due to: 1) high rates of adverse events (including severe cases of hyperglycemia, stomatitis and diarrhea) and dosage modifications in patients receiving inavolisib 9 mg PO QD + palbociclib + fulvestrant, and 2) multiple positive E-R relationships for safety endpoints. In addition, there are limited clinical data at lower inavolisib starting dosages (i.e., &lt;9 mg PO QD) and a lack of E-R relationships for efficacy using clinical data from one dose level. Additional dosage optimization is needed to identify whether an alternative inavolisib dosage would provide an improved benefit-risk profile.
L497: - Severe Renal Impairment PMR: A dedicated renal impairment study is currently ongoing. A PMR will be issued to submit the full CSR and datasets, including data from participants with severe renal impairment, after completion of the ongoing study.
- Moderate and Severe Hepatic Impairment PMR: The effects of moderate and severe hepatic impairment on inavolisib safety and PK have not been characterized (see **Section 6.1.2.2**). Given the potential involvement of liver in inavolisib excretion, a PMR will be issued to evaluate the effects of moderate and severe hepatic impairment on the PK and safety of inavolisib.
- Diversity PMC (see **Section 8.1.1** for additional information): Patients from different racial and ethnic groups relevant to the US patient population were not well represented in the inavolisib development program. A PMC to evaluate inavolisib in a diverse patient population will be issued. The PMC will include collection of clinical pharmacology data to evaluate any potential differences in PK and PD based on race or ethnicity.
- CYP3A Induction PMC: Inavolisib increased CYP3A mRNA up to 40-fold in vitro and available data cannot rule out potential induction effect of inavolisib on sensitive CYP3A substrates in vivo. A clinical pharmacokinetic trial is needed to evaluate the effect of repeat doses of inavolisib on the single dose pharmacokinetics of a sensitive CYP3A substrate and inform appropriate management strategies for clinically relevant drug interactions.
- CYP2B6 Induction PMC: Inavolisib increased CYP2B6 mRNA up to 4-fold in vitro and available data cannot rule out potential induction effect of inavolisib on sensitive CYP2B6 substrates. A clinical pharmacokinetic trial is needed to evaluate the effect of repeat doses of inavolisib on the single dose pharmacokinetics of a CYP2B6 substrate and inform appropriate management strategies for clinically relevant drug interactions.
L498: ## 6.3. Comprehensive Clinical Pharmacology Review
L499: ## 6.3.1. General Pharmacology and Pharmacokinetic Characteristics
L500: ## **Data:**
L501: **Absorption:** Following oral administration, inavolisib was rapidly absorbed with median time to maximum observed concentration (t_{max}) of 3 hours. Administration of inavolisib in the presence of food did not impact the rate or the extent of absorption of inavolisib following a single or multiple doses of inavolisib. The absolute bioavailability of inavolisib following single oral administration of 9 mg inavolisib was 76%, demonstrating that inavolisib has a moderate to
L502: high absolute bioavailability. With continuous QD dosing, there was approximately 2-fold accumulation, estimated based on individual exposures on Day 15 relative to Day 1 (AUC_{0-24hr}). This accumulation is consistent with the observed half-life (t_{1/2}) and the dosing frequency, indicating that inavolisib does not demonstrate time dependent pharmacokinetics.
L503: **Distribution:** The percentage of inavolisib bound to human plasma proteins *in vitro* was low (37%) and did not appear to be concentration-dependent over the concentration range tested (0.1-10 mM). Following a single intravenous (IV) dose of 100 µg [^{13}C_6]-inavolisib, the geometric mean volume of distribution was 204 L (29% CV), indicating extensive distribution into tissues.
L504: **Metabolism:** Minimal metabolism of inavolisib was detected *in vitro* in rat, dog, and human liver microsome incubations. The formation of a minor oxidative metabolite was principally mediated by CYP3A4/3A5. Circulating metabolites were minimal with the majority (99.5%) of drug-related material in plasma being inavolisib in the human mass balance study. Total metabolites in the excreta accounted for 42% (35% in feces and 7% in urine) of the dose. The major metabolic pathway was hydrolysis of the primary amide to carboxylic acid; CYP-mediated metabolites were only ~2%.
L505: **Excretion:** Inavolisib was eliminated to a similar extent via urinary and fecal pathways. Following oral administration of 9 mg [^{14}C]-inavolisib, a mean (SD) of 48.5% (8.18) of the dose was recovered in urine and 48.0% (7.22) of the dose was recovered in feces through the last collection interval. The overall mean recovery of total radioactivity in urine and feces samples was 96.5%, with recovery in individual volunteers that ranged from 92.6 to 99.2%. Urinary excretion represented 48.5% of the dose; unchanged inavolisib was the most prominent drug-related compound in the urine, accounting for 40.4% of the dose. Fecal excretion represented 48.0% of the administered dose. Inavolisib accounted for 10.8% of the dose in feces; metabolites M8, M14, and M15 accounted for 6.4%, 7.9%, and 6.8% of the dose, respectively. Total metabolites in the excreta accounted for 42% (35% in feces and 7% in urine) of the dose. Mean half-life (t_{1/2}) following a single 9 mg dose was approximately 16.4 hours (range: 14.1-21.4 hours) in patients with advanced breast cancer.
L506: **Dose Proportionality:** Single agent data suggest dose proportionality within the dose range tested (6-12 mg) for C_{max} and AUC_{0-24} following single dose and at AUC_{0-24} steady state; however, for steady state C_{max}, the data suggests higher than dose proportional exposure.
L507: **Renal Impairment:** Population pharmacokinetics (popPK) analysis suggested that mild renal impairment has no clinically significant impact on the exposure of inavolisib. The geometric mean ratio (GMR) (90% CI) of inavolisib PK in patients with mild renal impairment (n = 124) vs. normal renal function (n = 185) for AUC was 1.10 (1.05 - 1.16), C_{max} is 1.11 (1.06 - 1.17), and minimum plasma concentration observed (C_{min}) is 1.11 (1.03 - 1.18). A dedicated renal impairment study (GP44944) in subjects with moderate or severe renal impairment is ongoing.
L508: **Hepatic Impairment:** PopPK analysis suggested no impact of mild hepatic impairment on the exposure of inavolisib. The GMR (90% CI) of inavolisib PK in patients with mild hepatic impairment (n = 111) vs. normal hepatic function (n = 224) for area under the concentration-time curve from Time 0 to Time t (AUC_{0-t}) was 0.986 (0.934 - 1.040), C_{max} is 1.010 (0.955 - 1.060), and C_{min} is 0.970 (0.902 - 1.040). A dedicated study is not warranted in patients with
L509: moderate or severe hepatic impairment because, based on the ADME properties of inavolisib, hepatic impairment is unlikely to have a clinically significant impact on the PK of inavolisib.
L510: **Population PK:** PK of inavolisib following oral administration was well-described by a population PK model consisting of a three-compartment model with first-order rate of absorption and elimination. eGFR effect on clearance (CL) and body weight and race (Asian) effects on central volume of distribution (V_c) were identified as significant with higher CL for subjects with higher eGFR, higher V_c for subjects with higher body weight and 25% lower V_c in Asian subjects compared to non-Asian subjects. However, PopPK model based simulations indicated that body weight, age, race, mild hepatic impairment, and mild renal impairment were not associated with clinically relevant differences in inavolisib exposures following 9 mg QD dosing.
L511: **The Applicant's Position:** No DDIs were identified or predicted with inavolisib. Given the lack of clinically relevant impact of body weight, age, race (Asian vs. Non-Asian), hepatic impairment (mild, moderate, severe), or renal impairment (mild) on inavolisib PK, dose adjustment of inavolisib in these specific populations is not warranted.
L512: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L513: FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's summary of ADME (absorption, distribution, metabolism, and excretion) properties and dose proportionality of inavolisib. FDA disagrees with the Applicant's positions in the following areas:
L514: - Total metabolites in the excreta accounted for 48% (38% in feces and 10% in urine) of the dose, rather than the Applicant's summarized 42%.
- Per the interim CSR of the ongoing renal impairment study submitted on August 7, 2024, inavolisib AUC_{0-t} was 64% higher and AUC_{0-inf} was 73% higher in subjects with moderate renal impairment compared to subjects with normal renal function after a single 6 mg oral dose of inavolisib (Table 8). The C_{max} values were similar between subjects with normal renal function and subjects with moderate renal impairment.
- FDA disagrees with the Applicant's conclusion that a dedicated hepatic impairment study is not warranted (see Section 6.1.2.2). A PMR will be issued to request a dedicated clinical hepatic impairment study.
- The popPK analysis included sufficient White and Asian patients to assess for PK differences between these two racial groups only. There was limited representation of Black/African American, Hispanic/Latino, and other underrepresented minority patients in the inavolisib development program. Clinical pharmacology data will be requested to be included if a PMC on race and ethnicity to be issued by FDA.
L515: The inavolisib steady state AUC is 1,010 h^*ng/mL (25%) and C_{max} is 69 ng/mL (27%) at the recommended dosage (9 mg PO QD). Inavolisib steady state apparent (oral) volume of distribution is 155 L (3.5%). The estimated total clearance of inavolisib is 5.5 L/hr (29%).
L516: ## **QT Analysis:**
L517: Based on FDA's QT-IRT analysis of the clinical data from the Phase 1 Study GO39374, inavolisib is unlikely to cause \ge 20 msec mean increase in QTc interval at the proposed recommended dosage of 9 mg PO QD. Refer to the QT-IRT review (dated 8/1/2024) for details.
L518: ## **Pharmacogenomics:**
L519: FDA's pharmacogenomics analysis suggested inavolisib plus palbociclib and fulvestrant treatment is effective for patients regardless of PIK3CA mutation status. See Section 6.3.2.3 for details.
L520: ## **6.3.2. Clinical Pharmacology Questions**
L521: ## **6.3.2.1. Does the clinical pharmacology program provide supportive evidence of effectiveness?**
L522: Data and the Applicant's Position: Yes, please refer to Section 6.2.2.1.
L523: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L524: Yes. The pivotal evidence of effectiveness is the improvement in PFS for patients who received inavolisib + palbociclib + fulvestrant compared with patients who received placebo + palbociclib + fulvestrant in Study WO51554 (refer to Section 8.1.2).
L525: There were no apparent exposure-response (E-R) relationships for efficacy endpoints (PFS, DOR, TFST, CBR, ORR, and BOR) identified; however, this finding should be interpreted with caution because the proposed triplet combination (inavolisib + palbociclib + fulvestrant) was only studied at one dose level of inavolisib (9 mg PO QD). See Section 19.4.3.2 for details on the E-R efficacy analysis.
L526: ## **6.3.2.2. Is the proposed dosing regimen appropriate for the general patient population for which the indication is being sought?**
L527: Data and the Applicant's Position: Yes, please refer to Section 6.2.2.1.
L528: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L529: FDA agrees that inavolisib 9 mg PO QD is an effective dosage based on improved PFS for inavolisib 9 mg PO QD + palbociclib + fulvestrant compared with placebo + palbociclib + fulvestrant in patients with HR+, HER2- locally advanced or metastatic breast cancer in Study WO51554. However, it is not clear whether the proposed dosage is optimized due to:
L530: - High rates of AEs and dosage modification
- Positive E-R relationships for safety
- Limited clinical data at lower dosages
L531: ## 4. Lack of E-R relationship for efficacy
L532: Given the lack of dosage optimization and potential for improved benefit-risk profile with a lower dose of inavolisib, a PMR will be issued to study a lower starting dosage. See Section 6.1.2.1 for details.
L533: ## 6.3.2.3. Is an alternative dosing regimen or management strategy required for subpopulations based on intrinsic patient factors (e.g., race, ethnicity, age, performance status, genetic subpopulations, etc.)?
L534: Data and the Applicant's Position: No, please refer to Section 6.2.2.2.
L535: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L536: FDA disagrees with the Applicant's position.
L537: - **Renal impairment:** As detailed in Section 6.1.2.2, dose reduction (to 6 mg PO QD) is recommended in patients with moderate renal impairment. Dosing recommendations for patients with severe renal impairment is not currently available and will be dependent on results of the ongoing renal impairment study.
- **Hepatic impairment:** A &gt;20% contribution of hepatic elimination cannot be ruled out for inavolisib. Therefore, it is not known at this time whether an alternative dosing regimen or management strategy is required in patients with moderate or severe hepatic impairment. A PMR will be issued to evaluate the PK and safety of inavolisib in patients with moderate and severe hepatic impairment. Refer to Section 6.2.2.2 for details.
- **Race and ethnicity:** The available data were insufficient to determine any potential differences in PK or PD between patients of different races (other than White or Asian) and ethnicities. Clinical pharmacology data will be collected in a diversity PMC. Refer to Section 8.1.1 for details.
- **PIK3CA mutation status and treatment effect:** FDA explored the treatment efficacy by PIK3CA mutation status in study WO41554. PIK3CA mutations were detected by central laboratory using the FoundationOne® Liquid CDx assay on plasma-derived circulating tumor DNA (ctDNA), or local laboratories using various validated polymerase chain reaction (PCR) or next-generation sequencing (NGS) assays on tumor tissue or plasma. In study WO41554 (N=325), 270 patients were identified as having PIK3CA mutations via central testing. Consistent with literature (Martínez-Sáez et al, 2020), the majority of identified PIK3CA mutations were located in exon 9 (N=103, 38.1%), and exon 20 (N=119, 44.1%) (Table 15). There were 5, 8, and 10 patients with PIK3CA mutations that were located in exon 1, 4, and 7, respectively. Efficacy in terms of PFS was observed across patients with PIK3CA mutated exon 9 and exon 20 and, were consistent with the overall population (Table 16). The efficacy results for patients with "multiple exons"
L538: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219249}
## {Inavolisib}
L539: and “other exons” need to be interpreted with caution due to the small sample size in those subgroups, but still showed greater PFS in the inavolisib arm versus placebo. In summary, the above results suggested inavolisib plus palbociclib and fulvestrant treatment is effective for patients regardless of *PIK3CA* mutation status.

L549: ## 6.3.2.4. Are there clinically relevant food-drug or drug-drug interactions, and what is the appropriate management strategy?
L550: ## Data:
L551: **Food-drug interaction:** The GMR (90% CI) for plasma Cmax comparing the fed to the fasted state was 0.925 (0.748-1.14) and 0.910 (0.712-1.16) after a single dose and following QD dosing, respectively. The GMR (90% CI) for AUC0-24 comparing the fed to the fasted state was 0.895 (0.737-1.09) and 0.876 (0.701-1.09) after a single dose and following QD dosing, respectively.
L552: ## Drug-drug interaction:
L553: ## Effects of inavolisib on other drugs
L554: {Inavolisib}
L555: ***CYP Substrates:*** *In vitro* studies suggest a low likelihood of time-dependent inhibition and induction of CYP3A4, and no potential to inhibit or induce the other CYP enzymes tested (CYP1A2, CYP2B6, CYP2C8, CYP2C9, CYP2C19, or CYP2D6) at clinically relevant concentrations. Endogenous biomarker data, palbociclib PK, and PBPK simulations together suggest that inavolisib is unlikely to have a clinically relevant impact on the exposure of CYP3A4 substrates.
L556: ***Transporters:*** *In vitro* studies have shown that inavolisib does not appear to have the potential to inhibit any of the transporters tested (P-glycoprotein [P-gp], BCRP, OATP1B1, OATP1B3, OCT1, OCT2, MATE1, MATE2K, OAT1, or OAT3) at clinically relevant concentrations.
L557: ## Effects of other drugs on inavolisib
L558: ***CYP Inhibitors/Inducers:*** Clinical study results show that the predominant metabolites of inavolisib are not mediated by CYP enzymes, suggesting a low likelihood of interaction between inavolisib and CYP inhibitors or inducers.
L559: ***Transporters:*** *In vitro* studies have shown that inavolisib is not a substrate of OATP1B1, OATP1B3, OCT1, OCT2, OAT1, OAT2, MATE1, or MATE2K, but is a substrate of P-gp and BCRP. However, based on the pharmacokinetic profile of inavolisib, inhibitors or inducers of P-gp and/or BCRP are not expected to have a clinically relevant DDI with inavolisib.
L560: ***Acid-reducing Agents:*** In clinical studies, concomitant use of proton pump inhibitors did not have a clinically meaningful effect on inavolisib exposure.
L561: ## The Applicant's Position:
L562: ***Food-drug interaction:*** These data suggest that administration of inavolisib in the presence of food did not impact the rate or the extent of absorption of inavolisib following a single or multiple doses of inavolisib.
L563: ***Drug-drug interactions:*** No clinically relevant DDIs are identified or expected with inavolisib as a victim or perpetrator.
L564: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L565: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position on food-effect on inavolisib PK. FDA does not agree with the Applicant's positions on inavolisib DDI potential. In addition, FDA agrees with the Applicant's approach of bridging different formulations using dissolution studies; refer to the Biopharmaceutics review for details.
L566: ## 1) Food-effect:
L567: The effect of a high-fat meal (approximately 1,000 calories with 50% from fat) on the PK of inavolisib was evaluated after a single dose (Day 1) and following QD dosing (Day 8) in Arm D of Study GO39374 (Table 17). Study results showed that AUC0-24 and Cmax of inavolisib given with a high-fat meal decreased by 12% and 9%, respectively. The food effect study was conducted with the Phase 1 formulation. Subsequent trials (including Study WO51554 using the Phase 3 formulation) allowed patients to take inavolisib with or without food. Given the minor changes between formulations and the data collected in Study WO51554 in patients taking inavolisib
L568: without regard to food, FDA agrees that inavolisib may be administered with or without food.

L573: ## 2) PPI
L574: As described in section 6.1.2.2, based on popPK analysis of inavolisib PK in patient with self-reported PPI use or no PPI use, no clinically significant difference in steady state inavolisib pharmacokinetics were observed based upon concomitant use of a proton pump inhibitor (lansoprazole, omeprazole, esomeprazole, pantoprazole, or rabeprazole). Refer to Section 6.2.2.2 for details.
L575: ## 3) DDI as an Object and Precipitant
L576: Based on the mass balance study and in vitro evaluations, DDI potential for inavolisib as an object is low given the lack of CYP metabolism observed in vivo. Inavolisib increased CYP3A and CYP2B6 mRNA in vitro. Induction effect on CYP3A and CYP2B6 in vivo cannot be ruled out; therefore, PMCs will be issued to conduct a clinical study or studies to assess inavolisib induction potential on substrates of CYP3A and CYP2B6. If inavolisib is confirmed to be at least a weak inducer of CYP3A, the induction potential on CYP2C enzymes will need to be addressed. Refer to Section 6.1.2.2.
L577: ## 4) DDI Potential with Combined Oral Contraceptives
L578: In section 8.3 of the initial proposed labeling, the Applicant proposed to “advise females of reproductive potential to use effective non-hormonal contraception during treatment with TRADENAME”. In response to a non-clinical IR, the sponsor proposed to modify the language in section 8.3
L579: (b) (4) in their updated version of the proposed label. Co-administration of CYP3A inducers may decrease the effectiveness of oral contraceptives. Therefore, the Applicant will be informed that a clinical DDI study may be warranted if the clinical DDI study on CYP3A induction potential confirms the in vivo induction potential on CYP3A.
L580: ## 5) Formulation Bridging
L581: Multiple formulations were used during the course of development, including Phase 1, Phase 1 resupply, and Phase 3 formulations. The proposed to-be-marketed formulation is identical to the Phase 3 formulation with the exception of the coating. Minor changes were made between these formulations as well as the manufacturing process. The Applicant bridged these changes by in vitro dissolution studies. Human bioequivalence studies were not needed to support the bioequivalence of these formulations, particularly the Phase 3 formulation and proposed commercial formulation. Refer to the Biopharmaceutics review for details.

L592: ## 7 Sources of Clinical Data
L593: ## 7.1. Table of Clinical Studies
L594: **Data and the Applicant's Position:** All studies pertinent to the evaluation of efficacy and safety are summarized in Table 18. The evidence in support of the proposed indication is primarily derived from the pivotal Phase III Study WO41554 (INAVO120) (CCOD: 29 September 2023). Additional evidence is derived from the Phase I Study GO39374 (CCOD: 27 March 2023).
L595: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L596: FDA agrees that the primary efficacy and safety analyses for this application are based on results from the INAVO120 (WO41554) trial.
L597: Data from GO39374 was submitted as supportive evidence. GO39374 was a phase 1 study with 7 different treatment arms and no comparator arm. The patient population varied in each of the 7 arms due to different eligibility criteria and differed from the patient population enrolled in INAVO120. In addition, the treatment regimen in most of the 7 arms was different than the triplet regimen from INAVO120. The Applicant submitted data from 3 of the 7 treatment arms (b)(4) that included use of any endocrine therapy for the triplet combination and to allow treatment with the triplet combination in later lines of therapy (compared to the patient population in INAVO120). The three treatment arms of focus include: Arm B (inavolisib + palbociclib + letrozole), Arm E (inavolisib + palbociclib + fulvestrant), and Arm F (inavolisib + palbociclib + fulvestrant + metformin).
