L606: ## 8 Statistical and Clinical Evaluation
L607: ## 8.1. Review of Relevant Individual Trials Used to Support Efficacy
L608: ## Pivotal Study WO41554 (INAV0120) and Supportive Study G039374
L609: ## Trial Design
L610: ## 8.1.1.1. Pivotal Study: WO41554 (INAV0120), Phase III
L611: **The Applicant's Description:** Study WO41554 is a Phase III, randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, multi-center, global study designed to compare the efficacy, as measured by investigator-assessed PFS, and the safety of the triplet combination of inavolisib with palbociclib and fulvestrant (Inavo+Palbo+Fulv) vs. placebo with palbociclib and fulvestrant (Pbo+Palbo+Fulv) in patients with *PIK3CA*-mutated, HR+, HER2- LA/mBC whose disease progressed during treatment or within 12 months of completing adjuvant ET and who had not received prior systemic therapy for locally advanced or metastatic disease.
L612: Patient biomarker eligibility (i.e., positive for a study-eligible *PIK3CA* mutation) was determined through testing of blood plasma-derived circulating tumor DNA (ctDNA) with a next-generation sequencing (NGS) assay performed at the Sponsor-designated central laboratory, or through local testing of ctDNA or tumor tissue with an appropriately-validated NGS or polymerase chain reaction (PCR) assay.
L613: Eligible patients were randomly assigned in a 1:1 ratio to one of two treatment arms: Inavo+Palbo+Fulv or Pbo+Palbo+Fulv. Inavolisib/placebo was administered at 9 mg PO QD on Days 1-28 of each 28-day cycle; palbociclib was administered at 125 mg PO QD on Days 1-21 of each 28-day cycle; fulvestrant was administered at 500 mg by intramuscular injection on Days 1 and 15 of Cycle 1 and then on Day 1 of each subsequent 28-day cycle, or approximately every 4 weeks. Patients were stratified by: visceral disease (yes or no), endocrine resistance (primary or secondary according to European Society for Medical Oncology Advanced Breast Cancer 4 (ESMO ABC4) guidelines and geographic region (North America/Western Europe, Asia, Other).
L614: Patients received study treatment until unequivocal disease progression as determined by the Investigator, unacceptable toxicity, patient withdrawal of consent, loss to follow-up, death, or study termination.
L615: After randomization, tumor assessments were performed to assess for response every 8 weeks (\pm 7 days) during the first 2 years of study treatment and every 12 weeks (\pm 7 days) thereafter.
L616: Primary efficacy analyses were based on the tumor assessments performed by the local radiologists or Investigators. Response was assessed on the basis of physical examinations,
L617: computed tomography (CT) scans, and other imaging modalities as clinically indicated, which could include brain imaging (CT or magnetic resonance imaging [MRI]), MRI scans, and/or bone scan, using Response Evaluation Criteria in Solid Tumors (RECIST) v1.1 criteria. A retrospective blinded independent central review (BICR) of tumor assessment data was performed to support the primary endpoint of investigator-assessed PFS.
L618: To evaluate the PK properties of inavolisib, palbociclib, and fulvestrant administered in this triplet combination, blood samples were taken at various timepoints before and after dosing.
L619: Patient-reported outcome (PRO) instruments, including the Brief Pain Inventory-Short Form (BPI-SF) worst pain severity item, the European Organisation for Research and Treatment of Cancer Quality of Life Questionnaire-Core 30 (EORTC QLQ-C30) and its accompanying disease-specific module, the QLQ-BR23, were completed to evaluate the impact of treatment from the patient's perspective. Additionally, pre-selected symptom items from the Patient-Reported Outcomes Common Terminology Criteria for Adverse Events (PRO-CTCAE) and an overall bother item were used to assess the experience of treatment-related symptoms and their associated bother.
L620: Patients were monitored for adverse events (AEs) throughout the study treatment, following informed consent until 30 days after the final dose of study treatment or until initiation of another anti-cancer therapy, whichever occurred first. AEs were graded according to National Cancer Institute Common Terminology Criteria for Adverse Events (NCI CTCAE) v5.0. Patients who, at the 30-day safety follow-up visit, were still on anti-hyperglycemic agents for the treatment of hyperglycemia during the study treatment, and those patients with events of hyperglycemia ongoing at the 30-day safety follow-up visit, proceeded to the post-treatment hyperglycemia follow-up.
L621: ## Dose Modification
L622: The inavolisib/placebo dose reduction instructions for managing AEs are provided in the protocol. They are intended to serve as recommended guidelines to inform rather than supersede clinical judgment and the benefit-risk balance as assessed by the Investigator in managing individual cases. Dose modification for AEs was to be taken first with inavolisib rather than palbociclib unless clearly attributable to palbociclib alone based on known toxicity profile and the investigator's assessment. Interruption, but not reduction, was permitted for fulvestrant.
L623: ## Blinding
L624: Study WO41554 was a double-blind study: the Sponsor and its agents, study site personnel, and patients were blinded to treatment assignment during the study.
L625: ## Independent Data Monitoring Committee
L626: An independent Data Monitoring Committee (iDMC) was established to monitor safety, study conduct, and futility analysis. The iDMC reviewed unblinded safety summaries and summaries of key efficacy endpoints prepared by an independent data coordinating center (iDCC).
L627: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219249}
## {Inavolisib}
L628: **Trial Location:** The study was conducted at 123 centers across 28 globally distributed countries, with a total of 325 patients enrolled from across Asia, Europe, Australia, North America, and Central/South America. Among them, 16 patients were enrolled from 9 U.S. sites (see **Section 8.1.1** for further details).
L629: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L630: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment. The control arm treatment regimen of palbociclib + fulvestrant was an appropriate regimen for the first line treatment of patients with HR positive/HER2 negative MBC when the INAVO120 trial was initiated in 2019. This combination is still an appropriate option for first line treatment; however, based on results from the MONALEESA-2 and MONARCH 3 trials, ribociclib and abemaciclib are now used more commonly than palbociclib in the first line setting in the US.
L631: ## **Eligibility Criteria**
L632: ## **The Applicant's Description:**
L633: ## **Key Inclusion Criteria**
L634: - If female, patients had to meet at least one of the following definitions:Postmenopausal, as defined by criteria specified in the protocol
- Premenopausal or perimenopausal (i.e., not meeting the criteria for postmenopausal) and meeting the following criterion:Treatment with luteinizing hormone-releasing hormone (LHRH) agonist therapy (e.g., goserelin or leuprolide) beginning at least 2 weeks prior to Day 1 of Cycle 1 and continuing for the duration of study treatment
- If male, recommendation of treatment with LHRH agonist therapy beginning at least 2 weeks prior to Day 1 of Cycle 1 and continuing for the duration of study treatment
- Histologically or cytologically confirmed adenocarcinoma of the breast that was locally advanced or metastatic and was not amenable to surgical or radiation therapy with curative intent
- Documented estrogen receptor (ER)-positive and/or progesterone receptor (PR)-positive tumor according to American Society of Clinical Oncology (ASCO)/College of American Pathologists (CAP) guidelines
- Documented HER2-negative tumor according to ASCO/CAP guidelines
- Confirmation of biomarker eligibility: valid results from either central testing of blood or local testing of blood or tumor tissue documenting *PIK3CA*-mutated tumor status
- Patients had to have progressed during adjuvant endocrine treatment or within 12 months of completing adjuvant ET with an aromatase inhibitor or tamoxifen.If a CDK4/6 inhibitor was included as part of neoadjuvant or adjuvant therapy, progression event had to be &gt; 12 months since completion of CDK4/6 inhibitor portion of neoadjuvant or adjuvant therapy
- Measurable disease per Response Evaluation Criteria in Solid Tumors (RECIST) v1.1
L635: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219249}
L636: ## {Inavolisib}
L637: - Treatment with endocrine-based therapy (e.g., palbociclib and fulvestrant) was recommended at time of entry into the study, as per national or local treatment guidelines
- Adequate hematologic and organ function within 14 days prior to initiation of study treatment, including:Fasting glucose &lt; 126 mg/dL (&lt; 7.0 mmol/L) and \text{HbA}_{1\text{c}} &lt; 6.0\% (&lt; 42 mmol/mol)
L638: ## Key Exclusion Criteria
L639: - Any history of leptomeningeal disease or carcinomatous meningitis
- Any prior systemic therapy for metastatic breast cancer
- Prior treatment with fulvestrant or any selective estrogen-receptor degrader, with the exception of patients that had received fulvestrant or any selective estrogen-receptor degrader as part of neoadjuvant therapy only and with treatment duration of no longer than 6 months
- Prior treatment with any PI3K, AKT, or mTOR inhibitor, or any agent whose mechanism of action was to inhibit the PI3K-AKT-mTOR pathway
- Type 2 diabetes requiring ongoing systemic treatment at the time of study entry; or any history of Type 1 diabetes
- Known and untreated, or active central nervous system (CNS) metastases (progressing or requiring anticonvulsants or corticosteroids for symptomatic control)
- Any active bowel inflammation (including diverticulitis)
- Symptomatic active lung disease, including pneumonitis
- Clinically significant and active liver disease, including severe liver impairment (Child-Pugh Class B/C), viral or other hepatitis, current alcohol abuse, or cirrhosis
- History of or active clinically significant cardiovascular dysfunction
- Chronic corticosteroid therapy of \ge 10 mg of prednisone per day or an equivalent dose of other anti-inflammatory corticosteroids or immunosuppressants for a chronic disease
L640: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L641: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment. The criteria for fasting glucose and \text{HbA}_{1\text{c}} are stricter than the criteria in the trial leading to the approval of alpelisib and capivasertib. There were no eligibility criteria regarding body mass index (BMI).
L642: All patients were required to have endocrine resistant HR-positive HER2-negative advanced/metastatic breast cancer. Endocrine resistance was further categorized as primary endocrine resistance or secondary endocrine resistance. Primary endocrine resistance was defined as relapse while on the first 2 years of adjuvant endocrine therapy (ET) and secondary endocrine resistance was defined as relapse while on adjuvant ET after at least 2 years or relapse within 12 months of completing adjuvant ET.
L643: ## Study Endpoints
L644: **The Applicant's Description:** For the primary and secondary efficacy objectives, a comparison of the treatment arms was performed in the full analysis set (FAS). The primary efficacy endpoint was PFS as assessed by the investigator according to RECIST v1.1. Secondary efficacy endpoints include: OS; ORR; BOR rate; DOR for patients with objective response; CBR; time to confirmed deterioration (TTCD) in worst pain severity; TTCD in physical functioning (PF); TTCD in role functioning (RF); and TTCD in Global Health Status/Health-Related Quality of Life (GHS/HRQoL). ORR, BOR, DOR, and CBR were based on tumor assessment by the investigator. Exploratory efficacy endpoints include time to end of next-line treatment (proxy for PFS2), time to first skeletal related event (SRE), mean and mean change from baseline scores in function (physical, role, cognitive, emotional, and social), HRQoL, and disease and treatment-related symptoms.
L645: Safety objective of the study was to evaluate the safety of inavolisib plus palbociclib and fulvestrant compared with placebo plus palbociclib and fulvestrant (for further details see **Section 8.2.1**). PK objective of the study was to characterize the PK of inavolisib, palbociclib, and fulvestrant, when administered in combination in this population.
L646: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L647: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment. The primary efficacy endpoint for the INAVO120 trial was PFS as assessed by investigator. A prospectively planned sensitivity analysis of PFS by BICR assessment was also performed on all patients.
L648: ## Statistical Analysis Plan and Amendments
L649: **The Applicant's Description:** The Statistical Analysis Plan (SAP) was finalized prior to the conduct of any efficacy analysis and unblinding of the database. SAP Version 1 was finalized on 25 April 2023. A subsequent amendment was finalized on 18 September 2023, to clarify the statistical testing procedure of key secondary efficacy endpoints and the power of OS analyses.
L650: ## Analysis Populations
L651: All efficacy analyses were performed in the FAS (unless otherwise specified), defined as all randomized participants; participants were included in the analyses according to the treatment they were assigned. The safety set analysis (SAS) included all participants exposed to study treatment and were analyzed according to the treatment that they actually received. The PK-evaluable population included all randomized participants in the inavolisib arm who had at least one evaluable inavolisib plasma concentration.
L652: ## Hypothesis Testing
L653: The primary analysis was a comparison of the investigator-assessed PFS between the two treatment arms using a stratified log-rank test at an overall 0.05 significance level (two-sided).
L654: The statistical hypothesis of this study was as follows:
L655: - H0: PFS Inavo+Palbo+Fulv = PFS Pbo+Palbo+Fulv
- H1: PFS Inavo+Palbo+Fulv \neq PFS Pbo+Palbo+Fulv
L656: The null and alternative hypotheses were tested at a two-sided 0.05 significance level. The primary study objective was to demonstrate superiority of the experimental over the control treatment.
L657: ## Statistical Analysis of Efficacy Endpoints
L658: Sensitivity analyses to the primary estimand of the primary PFS were performed by BICR-assessed PFS, investigator-assessed PFS based on unstratified analysis, investigator-assessed PFS in patients with *PIK3CA* mutation-positive status by the central test, and based on handling of missing scheduled tumor assessments.
L659: Two supplementary analyses based on differing strategies of handling intercurrent event (ICE) were performed for investigator-assessed PFS based on hypothetical strategy and composite strategy for use of any non-protocol anti-cancer therapy (NPT) prior to disease progression.
L660: The Type I error-controlled secondary endpoints of this study were to be tested hierarchically according to the following pre-specified and fixed order of endpoints, only if the primary endpoint, PFS, was statistically significant at the primary PFS analysis:
L661: 1) OS; 2) ORR; 3) BOR; 4) CBR; 5) TTCD in pain; 6) TTCD in PF; 7) TTCD in RF; 8) TTCD in GHS/HRQoL.
L662: The primary efficacy analysis was conducted when approximately 194 PFS events had occurred. The first interim OS analysis was planned at the time of the primary analysis of PFS. A final OS analysis was estimated to occur approximately 19 months later. With the assumptions specified in SAP Version 2, the cumulative power planned at the interim and final OS analysis was 25% and 59%, respectively.
L663: ## Efficacy Subgroup Analysis
L664: The generalizability of PFS after randomization results was investigated as a part of an exploratory analysis by estimating the treatment effect in subgroups based on baseline prognostic factors (including stratification factors) with unstratified analysis as defined in the SAP Version 2.
L665: ## Safety Analysis
L666: Safety was assessed through summaries of exposure to study treatment, AEs, changes in laboratory test results, and changes in vital signs and electrocardiograms (ECGs). All verbatim AE terms were mapped to Medical Dictionary for Regulatory Activities (MedDRA 26.1) thesaurus terms, and AE severity was graded according to NCI CTCAE v5.0. All AEs, serious adverse events (SAEs), AEs leading to death, selected AEs (including adverse events of special interest [AESIs]), and AEs leading to study treatment discontinuation or dose modification that occurred on or after the first dose of study treatment (i.e., treatment-emergent AEs) were summarized accordingly. Deaths and cause of death were summarized.
L667: ## PK Analysis
L668: Plasma concentrations of inavolisib, palbociclib, and fulvestrant vs. time data were tabulated and plotted. Inavolisib plasma concentration vs. time data, together with information on dosing
L669: and patient characteristics, were pooled and analyzed using a popPK analysis approach, as appropriate. The PK data were combined with the safety and efficacy data for exposure-response modeling.
L670: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L671: FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of the design and protocol aspects of the INAVO120 trial as stated above.
L672: FDA also notes that the statistical test for PFS was based on 320 patients randomized in a 1:1 ratio, where 194 PFS events would provide a power of approximately 85% to detect a hazard ratio (HR) of 0.65 with a 2-sided type I error of 5%. One interim analysis for futility was to be conducted after approximately 75 PFS events (33% of information). The futility boundary was non-binding. One interim analysis was to be performed for OS at the time of the primary endpoint analysis, when 105 OS events were expected to have occurred (68% information fraction).
L673: ## **Protocol Amendments**
L674: **The Applicant's Description:** The first version of the protocol was issued on 12 August 2019 and the protocol version in effect at the CCOD was Version 8. None of the implemented changes impacted the integrity of the trial or the interpretation of the results. The key changes relevant to the evaluation of efficacy and safety are summarized below:
L675: **Protocol Version 2** (16 October 2019) included new information about interstitial lung disease (ILD)/pneumonitis with palbociclib (IBRANCE®) based upon a label update (USPI). The pneumonitis management guidelines were updated to align with the updated label.
L676: **Protocol Version 3** (Voluntary harmonization procedure [VHP]) (4 August 2020) was amended to address feedback from VHP. Contraception requirements for women and men were amended in response to fulvestrant label change. Interim safety analysis of the first 25 enrolled patients was added.
L677: **Protocol Version 4** (Global) (3 November 2020) was primarily amended to merge the VHP and global versions and to add China-specific language. In addition,
L678: - The exclusion criterion prohibiting prior fulvestrant or other selective estrogen-receptor degrader was modified to allow prior fulvestrant or other selective estrogen-receptor degrader only in the neoadjuvant early breast cancer setting and only if given for less than 6 months
- China-specific language was added: new inclusion criterion specified that patients enrolled in the study in China were a current resident of mainland China and of Chinese ancestry. A China-specific pharmacokinetic objective was added
L679: **Protocol Version 5** (Canada) (21 May 2021) replaced Version 4 of the protocol only in Canada. Version 5 was primarily updated to allow for mobile nursing (MN) in Canada to improve access and convenience for patients participating in the study.
L680: **Protocol Version 6** (12 August 2022) included changes from both Version 4 (Global) and Version
L681: 5 (Canada). In addition, this protocol was amended to extend participant eligibility:
L682: - The eligible threshold for HbA1c was changed from &lt;5.7% [39 mmol/mol] to &lt;6.0% [42 mmol/mol] to more closely align with the eligible threshold for fasting glucose of &lt;126 mg/dL defining pre-diabetes per the American Diabetes Association (ADA)
- The eligibility criterion for measurable disease per RECIST v1.1 was extended to include bone-only disease with a measurable soft-tissue component
L683: **Protocol Version 7** (21 October 2022) was amended primarily to correct the creatinine clearance threshold in the inclusion criteria to \ge 50 mL/min (previously \ge 30 mL/min) to rectify an error in the previous amendment Version 6.
L684: **Protocol Version 8** (8 March 2023) was amended to redefine the study sample size due to the unanticipated slow enrollment experienced and was intended to minimize the risk of Last Patient In (LPI) occurring after the number of planned events for the primary PFS analysis was reached. The total planned enrollment of 400 participants was reduced to approximately 320. The number of PFS events to trigger primary study readout was reduced from 227 to 194, resulting in a change in the power of PFS from 90% to 85%, which was considered adequate for the primary endpoint. The impact on the power for the secondary endpoint OS was minimal.
L685: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L686: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment.
L687: ## **Trial Design**
L688: ## **8.1.1.2. Supportive Study: GO39374, Phase I**
L689: **The Applicant's Description:** Study GO39374 is an open-label, multicenter, Phase I study designed to evaluate the safety, tolerability, and PK of inavolisib administered orally as a single agent in patients with locally advanced or metastatic *PIK3CA*-mutated solid tumors, including breast cancer, and in combination with standard-of-care endocrine and/or targeted therapies in patients with *PIK3CA*-mutated LA/mBC.
L690: Patients were enrolled in two stages: a dose-escalation stage (Stage I, Arms A, B, and C) and an expansion stage (Stage II, Arms B, C, D, E, F, and G). Patients were assigned to one of seven regimens: inavolisib as a single agent (Arm A), inavolisib in combination with palbociclib and letrozole (Arm B), inavolisib in combination with letrozole (Arm C), inavolisib in combination with fulvestrant (Arm D), inavolisib in combination with palbociclib and fulvestrant (Arm E), inavolisib in combination with palbociclib, fulvestrant, and metformin (Arm F), and inavolisib in combination with trastuzumab and pertuzumab (and letrozole or fulvestrant, if applicable) (Arm G, not included in this submission). Cycle 1 in the dose-escalation cohorts of Arm A was 35 days in length; all other cycles were 28 days in length. The study design and treatment is shown in **Section 7.1, Table 5**.
L691: ## **Key Inclusion Criteria:**
L692: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219249}
L693: ## {Inavolisib}
L694: - Arm A enrolled patients with histologically documented *PIK3CA*-mutated, locally advanced, recurrent, or metastatic, incurable solid tumor malignancy, including breast cancer.
- Arms B, C, D, E, and F enrolled female patients with histologically documented *PIK3CA*-mutated, HR+/HER2- LA/mBC. Arms B and C enrolled postmenopausal female patients only.
- Arms A, B, C, D, and E: Fasting glucose \le 140 mg/dL and glycosylated hemoglobin (HbA_{1c}) &lt; 7\%
- Arm F only: Patients with BMI \ge 30 kg/m^2 or HbA_{1c} \ge 5.7\% and &lt; 7\% at baseline
- Arms A, C, D: Absolute neutrophil count \ge 1200/\mu L; Arms B, E, F: Absolute neutrophil count \ge 1500/\mu L
L695: ## Key Exclusion Criteria:
L696: - Type 1 or 2 diabetes requiring antihyperglycemic medication
- Known and untreated, or active CNS metastases (progressing or requiring anticonvulsants or corticosteroids for symptomatic control).
- Any prior treatment with PI3K inhibitor
- Stage II, Arm B, Arm C, Arm D, and Arm E only: Prior treatment with &gt; 1 cytotoxic chemotherapy regimen in the metastatic setting
- Stage II, Arm B and Arm E only: Any prior treatment with CDK4/6 inhibitor
L697: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L698: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment.
L699: The eligibility criteria for each arm of Study GO39374 varied and were different than that for INAVO120. Patients were not required to have endocrine resistant disease in GO39374.

Ethics Committees and Institutional Review Boards reviewed and approved all studies.

L704: The Roche Clinical Quality Assurance group or designee conducted 2 investigator audits, 2 study audits, 2 service provider audits, and 2 internal process audits for Study WO41554. One critical audit finding was identified during a service provider audit [REDACTED] (b) (4) The

L716: ## 8.1.2.1. Pivotal Study: WO41554 (INAVO120), Phase III
L717: ## Patient Disposition
L718: **Data:** Patients were recruited from 123 centers across 28 countries (Table 19).
L719: The FAS population was comprised of a total of 325 patients: 161 patients in the Inavo + Palbo + Fulv arm and 164 patients in the Pbo + Palbo + Fulv arm. One patient was randomized to the Inavo + Palbo + Fulv arm but withdrew consent to participate in the study prior to dosing with any study drug. At the CCOD (29 Sep 2023) for the primary analysis, the median duration of follow-up for all patients was 21.3 months and was similar in both arms. A lower proportion of patients in the Inavo + Palbo + Fulv arm (58.4%) in comparison to the Pbo + Palbo + Fulv arm (70.1%) had discontinued from all study treatment. Across individual study treatments, the most common reason for study drug discontinuation was progressive disease, which occurred at higher frequency in the Pbo + Palbo + Fulv arm.
L720: 102
L721: Version date: June 2022 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
L722: **Disclaimer:** In this document, the sections labeled as "Data" and "The Applicant's Position" are completed by the Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.
L723: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219249}
L724: ## {Inavolisib}
L725: A lower proportion of patients in the Inavo + Palbo + Fulv arm (35.4%) had discontinued from the study compared with the Pbo + Palbo + Fulv arm (40.9%). Death was the main reason for study discontinuation in both treatment arms, 26.1% of patients in the Inavo + Palbo + Fulv arm and 33.5% of patients in the Pbo + Palbo + Fulv arm.
L726: **The Applicant's Position:** The pivotal Study WO41554 enrolled a globally diverse population through the multi-center, multinational nature of the study.
L727: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L728: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment. This was a multinational trial with 16 patients enrolled from the United States as outlined in Table 19 below. Refer to FDA assessment below regarding the assessment of diversity in INAVO120 below, under demographic characteristics, and the PMC issued by FDA for diversity in Section 13.
L729: ## Protocol Violations/Deviations
L730: **Data:** As of the CCOD (29 Sep 2023), 28.3% of patients of the FAS had at least one major protocol deviation. Overall, major protocol deviations were comparable between arms (Inavo+Palbo+Fulv: 26.1% vs. Pbo+Palbo+Fulv: 30.5%). The most common protocol deviations were procedural violations (17.8%). Major protocol deviations due to COVID-19 were comparable across arms (3.7% vs. 3.0%). The majority of major protocol deviations related to COVID-19 were medication related (3.1% vs. 2.4%). The major protocol deviations related to the Ukraine-Russia conflict were comparable across arms (1.9% vs. 1.2%), and were medication related (1.9% vs. 1.2%) or procedural related (1.2% vs. 0.6%).
L731: **The Applicant's Position:** Overall, none of the major protocol deviations were considered to have impacted the monitoring of patient safety, the study outcome, or the interpretation of the study results.
L732: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L733: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment that the major protocol deviations likely did not impact the outcome or interpretation of the study results.
L734: The most common protocol deviations categorized under procedural violations were labs not done per protocol (5.0% vs 7.9%); tumor assessment not done per protocol (6.2% vs 4.3%) and ICF not signed prior to any study-specific procedure (1.9% vs 6.1%) in the inavolisib vs placebo treatment arm, respectively.
L735: The most common protocol deviations categorized under medication were Incorrect inavolisib/placebo dose modification/interruption or delay (4.3% vs 1.8%); Incorrect palbociclib and dose modification/interruption or delay (1.2% vs 3.0%) in the inavolisib vs placebo treatment arm, respectively.
L736: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219249}
## {Inavolisib}
L737: The most common protocol deviations categorized under inclusion criteria were hemoglobin A1C greater than 6% (1.2% vs 3%) in the inavolisib vs placebo treatment arm, respectively.
L738: The most common protocol deviations categorized under exclusion criteria were patient has received any prior systemic therapy for metastatic breast cancer (1.2% vs 0%) in the inavolisib vs placebo treatment arm, respectively.

L745: **The Applicant's Position:** In the FAS population, the two treatment arms were comparable with respect to demographic characteristics (Table 19).
L746: Of the patients enrolled in the U.S., the majority had their ethnicity reported as “not Hispanic or Latino” (87.5%), the remainder of patients had their ethnicity reported as “Hispanic” (12.5%). With respect to race, the majority of patients were White (81.2%), with the remainder of
L747: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219249}
L748: ## {Inavolisib}
L749: patients in race categories of African American/Black (12.5%) or Asian (6.2%). This aligns well with the demographic distribution of the general population of the U.S. provided by the most recent U.S. Census (1 July 2023): White (75.5%), Black/African American (13.6%), Asian (6.3%), American Indian/Alaska Native (1.3%), Native Hawaiian/Pacific Islander (0.3%) (U.S. Census Bureau 2023).
L750: U.S. sites participating in Study WO41554 were regionally distributed as follows: Northeast (4 sites), Southeast (18 sites), Central (1 site), and West Coast (4 sites), among which 9 sites had at least one patient enrolled. As elaborated in the Inavolisib Diversity Plan, expected incidences of HR+/HER- breast cancer of all stages, 8.9% are expected to be Hispanic (any race), 11.1% Black/African American, 0.4% to American Indian/Alaska Native, and 5.7% to be Asian/Pacific Islander. This compares favorably with the demographic distribution of US-enrolled patients described above, with the caveat that U.S. enrollment was challenging (n=16). Attempts were made to include more community-based clinical trial sites with broad patient demographics, however participation in Study WO41554 was declined.
L751: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L752: In general, the two arms were well balanced across baseline characteristics. This was a multinational study with just 16 patients (4.9%) enrolled from the US. Almost all patients were female (98%) and the median age of 54 years for the patient population is younger than the median age (62 years old) at the time of breast cancer diagnosis in the US (cancer. org).
L753: The majority of patients on study were White. Racial and ethnic minorities reflective of the U.S. population of patients with breast cancer were significantly underrepresented in INAVO120 (WO41554), with only two Black patients and 20 Hispanic/Latino patients enrolled on study. Due to the lack of diversity in the enrolled population, a Post Marketing Commitment (PMC) is being issued to further characterize the efficacy and safety of inavolisib for the treatment of breast cancer in study populations whose demographics are more representative of the population of U.S. patients for the proposed indication.
L754: ## Other Baseline Characteristics (e.g., disease characteristics, important concomitant drugs)
L755: ## Data:
L756: ## Baseline Characteristics
L757: The majority of patients (60%) were post-menopausal; the remainder (38.2%) were "not post-menopausal" (i.e., pre-/peri-menopausal); six males (1.8%) enrolled and all were recorded with menopausal status of "missing". Approximately half of patients (47.1%) had a body mass index (BMI) in the normal range (\ge 18.5 \text{ kg/m}^2 to &lt; 25.0 \text{ kg/m}^2). The majority of patients had a baseline HbA1c of &lt; 5.7\% (88.0%) and fasting glucose of &lt; 100 \text{ mg/dL} (71.1%).
L758: ## Baseline Disease Characteristics
L759: At study entry, the majority of patients had metastatic disease (99.1%) with the remainder locally advanced disease (0.9%). The majority of patients had an ER+/PR+ hormone receptor status (69.5%). Per eCRF data, most patients had secondary endocrine resistance (65.5%) vs. primary endocrine resistance (34.2%). The majority of patients had 2 (32.3%) or \ge 3 (51.4%) organs involved at baseline. Only 0.6% had known CNS metastases at baseline. Per eCRF data, the majority of patients had measurable disease (non-measurable/ evaluable disease, including bone-only, was excluded) (96.6%), visceral disease (80%; defined as lung, liver, brain, pleural, and peritoneal involvement) and liver metastases (51.7%). The majority of patients had received prior (neo)-adjuvant chemotherapy (82.8%) and/or prior (neo)-adjuvant ET (99.4%); very few patients had received prior adjuvant CDK4/6 inhibitor therapy (1.2%).
L760: **The Applicant's Position:** Study WO41554 enriched a patient population with poor prognosis clinicopathological characteristics. The two treatment arms were comparable with respect to baseline disease characteristics and breast cancer history and are representative of a patient population with endocrine-resistant *PIK3CA*-mutated, HR+, HER2- advanced breast cancer receiving treatment in a first-line setting.
L761: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L762: In general, FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment. The patient population enrolled on this trial had endocrine resistant/refractory tumors that are considered to have a poor prognosis. The majority of patients had "secondary" endocrine resistance. Primary endocrine resistance was defined as relapse while on the first 2 years of adjuvant endocrine therapy and secondary endocrine resistance was defined a relapse while on adjuvant endocrine therapy after at least 2 years or relapse within 12 months of completing adjuvant endocrine therapy. In addition, all patients had a *PIK3CA* mutation, which is also considered a poor prognostic factor.
L763: ## **Treatment Compliance, Concomitant Medications, and Rescue Medication Use**
L764: ## **Data and the Applicant's Position:**
L765: **Treatment Compliance:** The median relative dose intensity of inavolisib, palbociclib and fulvestrant was high (Inavo+Palbo+Fulv arm: 95.8% [range: 26.4%-100.0%] for inavolisib, 87.3% [range: 16.7%-104.5%] for palbociclib and 100.0% [range: 75.0%-125.0%] for fulvestrant vs. Pbo+Palbo+Fulv arm: 88.4% [range: 33.3%-112.3%] for palbociclib and 100.0% [range: 60.0%-122.2%] for fulvestrant) and comparable between the treatment arms, indicating that the triplet combination was well-tolerated and the addition of inavolisib did not compromise the patients' ability to receive palbociclib and fulvestrant. Further details on exposure to study treatment are presented in Table 26 in Section 8.2.4.
L766: **Concomitant medications:** Overall, 73.5% patients received at least one prior concomitant medication ongoing at baseline in the study and this was comparable between arms (Inavo+Palbo+Fulv: 75.2% vs. Pbo+Palbo+Fulv: 72.0%). The majority of patients (90.8%) received at least one concomitant medication initiated during study treatment and this was
L767: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219249}
## {Inavolisib}
L768: comparable between arms (Inavo+Palbo+Fulv: 93.8% vs. Pbo+Palbo+Fulv: 87.8%). The most frequently reported (&gt;20% of all patients) concomitant medications initiated during study treatment in the Inavo+Palbo+Fulv arm and Pbo+Palbo+Fulv arm, respectively, were: dexamethasone (33.5% and 20.1%), paracetamol (26.7% and 18.9%), and metformin (28.0% and 3.7%).
L769: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L770: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment of treatment compliance and concomitant medications. A higher percentage of patients in the inavolisib treatment arm were initiated on dexamethasone, paracetamol and metformin during the study, these treatments are reflective of the toxicities observed more commonly on the inavolisib treatment arm.
L771: ## Efficacy Results – Primary Endpoint (Including Sensitivity Analyses)
L772: **Data and the applicant's position:** The primary endpoint was met with a statistically significant and clinically meaningful improvement in investigator-assessed PFS (Table 20 and Figure 7). The results of the sensitivity analyses of BICR-assessed PFS (stratified HR = 0.50; 95% CI: 0.36, 0.68), investigator-assessed PFS in patients with centrally determined *PIK3CA* mutation-positive status (stratified HR: 0.44; 95% CI: 0.31, 0.62), and additional sensitivity analyses to assess the impact of stratification on PFS (unstratified HR = 0.50; 95% CI: 0.38, 0.67) and missing data (stratified HR = 0.44; 95% CI: 0.32, 0.60) were consistent with the primary analysis of investigator-assessed PFS, demonstrating the robustness of investigator-assessed PFS results. The PFS benefit by investigator-assessment per RECIST v1.1 favoring the Inavo + Palbo + Fulv arm was generally consistent across pre-defined patient subgroups.

L785: PFS results were mostly consistent within subgroups but varied somewhat by region, with the North America/Western Europe region performing the worst of the three regions. The difference in survival between arms is diminished in the North America/Western Europe region in OS (HR=1.04 [95% CI: 0.54, 2.00]) and BICR-assessed PFS (HR=0.99 [95% CI: 0.60, 1.66]) as well. However, the sample sizes in these subgroups are small, so these results should be interpreted with caution.
L786: FDA queried the Applicant about subgroup results for the North America/West Europe (NA/WE) patient population. In an Information Request Response dated May 20, 2024, the Applicant stated that they “observed a relatively large extent of imbalances in some baseline characteristics with known prognostic impact between the two arms in the NA/WE subgroup compared to the FAS population, which may negatively impact the performance of the inavolisib arm relative to the placebo arm in this subgroup and potentially explain the numerically larger hazard ratio for PFS.” These included a lower proportion of patients with 1 organ site (lower overall disease burden) in the inavolisib arm compared with the placebo arm (8.1% vs. 25.4%) in the NA/WE subgroup, which would be considered a better prognostic factor compared to &gt;1 organ site involvement. In addition, they noted a higher proportion of patients with liver metastases (56.5% vs 46%) and progesterone receptor (PR)-negative disease (30.6% vs 20.6%) in the inavolisib arm compared with the placebo arm in the NA/WE subgroup, respectively. Both the presence of live metastases and PR negative disease may be poor prognostic factors. The Applicant stated the difference in OS results for NA/WE subgroup when compared to other subgroups could also be attributed to these same imbalances noted for the PFS results and due to the limited number of OS events. FDA agrees that these imbalances may have impacted the subgroup results but again notes these subgroup analyses should be interpreted with caution.
L787: The concordance of PFS assessment between INV and BICR were examined and summarized in the table below (Table 22). The early discordance rate (EDR) and late discordance rate (LDR) were calculated for each arm. EDR is the frequency that the investigator declares recurrence earlier than BICR, and LDR is the frequency that investigator declares recurrence later than
L788: BICR. A negative differential discordance for the EDR and/or a positive differential discordance for the LDR between the experimental arm and the control arm beyond a threshold would suggest bias in the investigator assessment favoring the Inavo+Palbo+Fulv arm. Threshold values ranging from 0.075 to 0.100 were recommended based on simulation studies in Amit et al. 2011.
L789: **Table 22: FDA - Concordance of INV and BICR Assessment of PFS in All Patients**
L790: INV Assessment	BICR Assessment
Inavo+Palbo+Fulv (N = 161)	Pbo+Palbo+Fulv (N = 164)
Event	Censor	Event	Censor
Event	74	8	96	17
Censor	3	76	2	49
L791: The concordance was 93.2% (95% CI: 88.1%, 96.5%) for the Inavo+Palbo+Fulv arm and 88.4% (95% CI: 82.5%, 92.9%) for the Pbo+Palbo+Fulv arm. The EDR was 25.6% for the Inavo+Palbo+Fulv arm and 25.7% for the Pbo+Palbo+Fulv arm, a difference of -0.1%. The LDR was 62.5% for the Inavo+Palbo+Fulv arm and 58.0% for the Pbo+Palbo+Fulv arm, a difference of 4.5%. Neither of the differences for EDR or LDR was greater than the threshold recommended in Amit et al. to demonstrate investigator assessment bias in favor of Inavo+Palbo+Fulv.
L792: ## Data Quality and Integrity
L793: **Data and the Applicant's Position:** Information requested by the Office of Scientific Investigations (OSI) is provided in Module 5.3.5.4. No issues were identified with the data quality or integrity which could affect the efficacy results.
L794: A comprehensive assessment of the impact of the COVID-19 pandemic was conducted looking at planned study procedures, data collection, and data analyses. Overall, the impact of the COVID-19 pandemic for these studies was minor and there was no significant impact on the study results and the overall conclusions. Similarly, an assessment on the impact of the Russia-Ukraine conflict on Study WO41554 was also performed and assessed as minor. For Study GO39374, there were no patients enrolled at investigational sites in Russia or Ukraine; therefore, an assessment of the Russia-Ukraine conflict was not applicable for this study.
L795: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L796: Refer to Section 4.1 of this Assessment Aid regarding details for OSI inspections conducted by FDA. FDA OSI inspected 3 clinical sites for INAVO120 (site codes 323897, 323629, and 323146). INV-assessed PFS, OS, and BICR-assessed PFS were evaluated excluding these study sites to assess any possible effect on HR. Overall, the results were consistent with those of the ITT population. No action was indicated for the sites.

L807: **Overall Survival:** OS is the key secondary endpoint evaluated under Type I error control per the hierarchical testing procedure pre-specified in the SAP. At the planned interim analysis for OS, a total of 97 events had occurred (63% of planned OS events). The stratified HR was 0.64 (95% CI: 0.43, 0.97; p = 0.0338), with 42 deaths in the Inavo + Palbo + Fulv arm and 55 deaths in the Pbo + Palbo + Fulv arm (**Table 23**). Under the interim analysis stopping boundary (p ≤ 0.0098), statistical significance was not reached. However, the interim OS analysis is highly supportive of the primary endpoint, with the Kaplan-Meier OS curve further indicating treatment benefit. The Kaplan-Meier curve started to separate at the beginning of the study in favor of the Inavo + Palbo + Fulv arm, and remained separated throughout the follow-up period.
L808: **Objective Response Rate (Investigator-Assessed):** The confirmed ORR based on investigator assessment per RECIST v1.1 was more than doubled in the Inavo + Palbo + Fulv arm (58.4%) as compared with the Pbo + Palbo + Fulv arm (25.0%). An increase of Δ33.4% (95% CI: 23.3, 43.5) in the confirmed ORR favoring the Inavo + Palbo + Fulv arm was considered clinically meaningful.
L809: **Best Overall Response Rate (Investigator-Assessed):** The proportion of patients with a best
L810: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219249}
L811: {Inavolisib}
L812: overall response of CR or PR, based on investigator assessment per RECIST v1.1, was more than doubled in the Inavo + Palbo + Fulv arm (64.0%) as compared with the Pbo + Palbo + Fulv arm (29.9%). An increase of \Delta 34.1\% (95% CI: 23.9, 44.3) in favor of the Inavo + Palbo + Fulv arm was considered clinically meaningful.
L813: **Duration of Objective Response:** Among patients who achieved a confirmed objective response (CR or PR), the estimated median duration of response was approximately 8.8 months longer in the Inavo + Palbo + Fulv arm (18.4 months) as compared with the Pbo + Palbo + Fulv arm (9.6 months).
L814: **Clinical Benefit of Rate (Investigator-Assessed):** The proportion of patients with a CR, PR, and/or stable disease (SD) sustained for \ge 24 weeks, based on investigator assessment per RECIST v1.1, was higher in the Inavo + Palbo + Fulv arm (75.2%) as compared with the Pbo + Palbo + Fulv arm (47.0%), with an increase of \Delta 28.2\% (95% CI: 18.1, 38.3) in favor of the Inavo + Palbo + Fulv arm.
L815: **The Applicant's Position:** Secondary endpoint analyses of OS, ORR, BOR, CBR rate, and DOR consistently showed a clinically meaningful improvement in Inavo+Palbo+Fulv treated patients over the Pbo+Palbo+Fulv arm.
L816: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L817: FDA generally agrees with the efficacy results for the secondary endpoints of OS, ORR, BOR, DOR, and CBR as presented by the applicant above. However, FDA notes that CBR is not an endpoint evaluated for regulatory decision-making.
L818: FDA also notes that, since the interim analysis of OS at the time of PFS analysis was not statistically significant, none of the remaining secondary endpoints in the pre-specified testing hierarchy were formally tested at this time and results should be considered descriptive. The final analysis of OS is expected in the second quarter of 2025.
L819: ## Dose/Dose Response
L820: **Data and the Applicant's Position:** Please refer to Section 6.2.2.1 and Section 19.4.2.
L821: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L822: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. See Sections 6 and 19 of the Assessment Aid for further details on the FDA clinical pharmacology review for this NDA.
L823: ## Durability of Response
L824: **Data and the Applicant's Position:** Of the patients who had an objective response, the median duration of response in the Inavo+Palbo+Fulv arm was longer compared to the Pbo+Palbo+Fulv arm (18.4 months vs. 9.6 months) (Table 23).
L825: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L826: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment. Among patients achieving an objective response, longer median DOR was observed in the Inavo + Palbo + Fulv arm, 18.4 months, compared with 9.6 months in the Pbo + Palbo + Fulv arm.
L827: ## Persistence of Effect
L828: **Data:** The investigator-assessed PFS event-free rate at 6 months, 12 months, and 18 months demonstrated early and durable benefit as shown by consistently and substantially higher PFS rates in the Inavo+Palbo+Fulv arm compared with the Pbo+Palbo+Fulv arm starting from the first tumor assessment and throughout the follow-up. Further evidence of early onset and persistence of efficacy is supported by the interim OS analysis, which although not statistically significant, is highly supportive of the primary endpoint.
L829: **The Applicant's Position:** Overall, the consistently prolonged PFS, OS and DOR data support that inavolisib, combined with palbociclib and fulvestrant, is durable and persists after treatment.
L830: **The FDA's Assessment:**
FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment.
L831: ## Efficacy Results – Secondary and exploratory COA (PRO) endpoints
L832: **Data:** PRO analyses were conducted for the BPI-SF Worst Pain Severity item and the EORTC QLQ-C30 PF, RF, and GHS/QoL scales. High compliance rates (&gt;80%) for the BPI-SF Worst Pain Severity item and EORTC QLQ-C30 were seen in both treatment arms at baseline and through Cycle 15.
L833: **TTCD in Worst Pain Severity:** Median TTCD in worst pain severity, as assessed by the BPI-SF questionnaire worst pain severity item, was longer for the Inavo + Palbo + Fulv arm (30.9 months) compared to the Pbo + Palbo + Fulv arm (18.1 months), indicating patients receiving inavolisib experienced a longer duration of time without worsening pain severity.
L834: **TTCD and Mean Change in Physical Functioning, Role Functioning and HRQoL:** Median TTCD was 23.7 months and 22.7 months for physical functioning, 25.7 months and 24.2 months for role functioning, and 29.0 months and 27.4 months for HRQoL, as assessed by the EORTC QLQ-C30 function and GHS/QoL scales, in the Inavo + Palbo + Fulv arm and Pbo + Palbo + Fulv arm, respectively, indicating patients in both arms maintained their day-to-day functioning and HRQoL for a similar duration of time while on treatment. Mean change scores of less than 10 points observed in these scales during treatment demonstrate that patients' baseline levels of functioning and HRQoL were maintained while on treatment in both arms.
L835: **The Applicant's Position:** These findings demonstrate that adding Inavo to Palbo and Fulv prolongs time without clinically meaningful deterioration in pain severity and does not adversely impact patients' day-to-day functioning and HRQoL, further supporting a positive benefit/risk profile for inavolisib.
L836: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L837: FDA acknowledges that TTCD in worst pain severity, physical functioning, role functioning, and health-related quality of life were key secondary endpoints included in the testing hierarchy. Completion rates for all PROs were high at baseline, above 90%, and remained above 80% at subsequent assessment timepoints during which the majority of patients remained on treatment in each arm (Cycle 9 and Cycle 5 for the Inavo + Palbo + Fulv versus Pbo + Palbo + Fulv arms, respectively). In general, FDA has concerns about patient-reported time-to-deterioration endpoints, and this was communicated to the Applicant prior to submission. Further, as noted in the secondary endpoints section, these results are considered descriptive at this time because the first key secondary endpoint of OS was not statistically significant. The Applicant did collect PRO-CTCAE and patient-reported overall side effect impact data, which is described in Section 8.2 below.
L838: ## **Additional Analyses Conducted on the Individual Trial**
L839: **Data and the Applicant's Position:** No additional analyses were conducted.
L840: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L841: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment.
L842: ## **8.1.2.2. Supportive Study: GO39374, Phase I**
L843: The primary endpoint of Study GO39374 is safety. The study makes a preliminary assessment of the anti-tumor activity of inavolisib administered as a single agent in patients with locally advanced or metastatic *PIK3CA*-mutated solid tumors or in combination with other therapies in patients with *PIK3CA*-mutated HR+, HER2– LA/mBC. The data provided herein focuses on Arms B (Inavo+Palbo+Letro), E (Inavo+Palbo+Fulv), and F (Inavo+Palbo+ Fulv+ Metf).
L844: ## **Patient Disposition**
L845: **Data and the Applicant's Position:** All patients enrolled in Arm B (N =33), Arm E (N = 20), and Arm F (N =21) were treated except for 1 patient in Arm F. This patient withdrew consent and discontinued from the study prior to receiving any study drug. As of CCOD (27 March 2023), the majority of patients in Arm B (84.8%), Arm E (70.0%), and Arm F (95.2%) had discontinued from the study, mostly due to progressive disease (Arm B: 69.7%, Arm E: 50.0%, and Arm F: 61.9%). A total of 191 patients were enrolled across Arms A-F. At the CCOD, a total of 176 patients (92.1%) discontinued from the study. The majority of study discontinuations were due to progressive disease (141 patients [73.8%]) and symptomatic deterioration (clinical progression of study disease; 15 patients [7.9%]).
L846: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L847: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment.
L848: ## Demographic Characteristics
L849: **Data:** All patients enrolled in Arm B (N = 33), Arm E (N = 20), and Arm F (N = 21) were female (Table 24). The median age of patients was 57 years (range: 37-80 years) in Arm B, 55 years (range: 33-73 years) in Arm E, and 65 years (range: 33-77 years) in Arm F. Half of the patients in Arm E (50%) and the majority of patients in Arm B (78.8%) and Arm F (76.2%) had self-reported race recorded as White. The remaining patients in each arm self-reported as either Asian, Black or African American or were of the race category 'unknown'. All patients in Arm B, Arm E, and Arm F had a baseline Eastern Cooperative Oncology Group (ECOG) status of either 0 or 1. A high proportion of patients in Arm F had a baseline HbA_{1c} \ge 5.7\% (10 patients; 50.0%) and BMI \ge 30 \text{ kg/m}^2 (15 patients; 75.0%) reflecting the inclusion criteria for enrollment in Arm F (patients needed to meet at least one of these 2 eligibility criteria, which are considered risk factors for hyperglycemia).
L850: The overall population of GO39374 comprised of mostly female patients (99.5%) with breast cancer and 1 male patient (0.5%) with colorectal cancer (CRC) (in Stage I Arm A), was predominately self-reported as White (72.8%), and had a median age of 59 years (range: 31-85 years).

L860: ## **Other Baseline Characteristics (e.g., disease characteristics, important concomitant drugs)**
L861: **Data:** All patients enrolled in Arm B (N = 33), Arm E (N = 20), and Arm F (N = 21) had to have histologically documented, *PIK3CA*-mutated, HR+, HER2- LA/mBC.
L862: At baseline, the majority of patients in Arm B (93.9%), Arm E (95.0%), and all 21 patients (100%) in Arm F had at least one site of metastatic disease. The most common sites of metastatic disease were bone (Arm B: 66.7%; Arm E: 80.0%; Arm F: 76.2%), liver (Arm B: 54.5%; Arm E: 30.0%; Arm F: 61.9%), and lung (Arm B: 39.4%; Arm E: 30.0%, Arm F: 19.0%).
L863: The majority of patients in Arm B (97.0%), Arm E (60.0%), and all 21 patients in Arm F had received prior systemic anti-cancer therapy in the metastatic setting before study enrollment. Among these patients, the median line of prior therapy in the metastatic setting was 2 lines (range: 1-4 lines) for Arm B, 1 line (range: 1-4 lines) for Arm E, and 3 lines (range: 1-9 lines) for Arm F. In Arm B, 21.2% had received prior treatment with a CDK4/6 inhibitor. No patients in Arm E had received prior CDK4/6 inhibitor as per eligibility requirement, while 66.7% of patients in Arm F had received a prior CDK4/6 inhibitor.
L864: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219249}
L865: ## {Inavolisib}
L866: Most patients in Arm B (72.7%), Arm E (80%), and Arm F (95.2%) had received prior AI, with letrozole as the most common aromatase inhibitor therapy reported in all arms. Fourteen out of 33 patients (42.4%) in Arm B, 3/20 patients (15.0%) in Arm E and the majority of patients in Arm F (71.4%) had received a prior selective estrogen-receptor degrader (SERD). In the overall population, 97.4% of patients had at least one site of metastatic disease at baseline. A total of 94.8% of patients had received prior cancer therapy in the metastatic setting. Among the patients who received therapy in the metastatic setting, the median line of prior therapy in the metastatic setting was 2 (range: 1-10).
L867: **The Applicant's Position:** The population from Arms B, E, and F of Study GO39374 are representative of *PIK3CA*-mutated, HR+, HER2- LA/mBC patients receiving an endocrine-based combination therapy in a later line setting. There were no eligibility restrictions with respect to patients' endocrine sensitivity or resistance; however, the majority of patients enrolled in Arm B, Arm E, and Arm F were endocrine-resistant.
L868: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L869: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment. The majority of patients in Arms B, E and F had received prior systemic therapy in the advanced/metastatic setting. In addition, patients enrolled in GO39374 were not required to have endocrine resistant disease.
L870: ## Treatment Compliance, Concomitant Medications, and Rescue Medication Use
L871: **Data and The Applicant's Position:** At the CCOD, the median cumulative inavolisib dose intensity was high at 95.3%, 90.5% and 89.1% in Arms B, E, and F, respectively. The median cumulative palbociclib dose intensity was 80.6% in Arm E and 92.8% in Arm F, and the median cumulative fulvestrant dose intensity was 95.4% in Arm E and 95.7% in Arm F. In the overall population, the median cumulative inavolisib dose intensity across all arms was 96.0% (Table 28).
L872: Overall, 93.7% of patients had at least one medication ongoing at baseline. The most commonly used (\ge30% of patients overall) medication classes were: analgesics (59.7%); vitamins (44.5%); psycholeptics (44.5%); mineral supplements (40.8%); topical products for joint and muscular pain (36.1%); drugs for acid related disorders (35.1%); ophthalmologicals (31.9%); other dermatological preparations (30.4%).
L873: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L874: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment.

While activity was observed across the three treatment arms based on the confirmed ORR and DOR, given the small sample sizes and wide CI of ORR, the contribution of inavolisib to efficacy was unclear in these single arm cohorts. L885: ## 8.1.3. Integrated Review of Effectiveness
L886: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L887: FDA's conclusions regarding the efficacy of inavolisib plus palbociclib and fulvestrant for the treatment of adults with endocrine resistant, PIK3CA-mutated, HR-positive, HER2-negative,
L888: locally advanced, or metastatic breast cancer, following recurrence on or after completing adjuvant endocrine therapy, are based on results from 325 patients enrolled in INAVO120, an ongoing, randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, multicenter trial of inavolisib plus palbociclib and fulvestrant versus placebo plus palbociclib and fulvestrant. Patients enrolled on INAVO120 were required to have advanced breast cancer with a PIK3CA mutation, considered to be endocrine resistant, and could not have received prior systemic therapy in the advanced or metastatic setting for breast cancer. PIK3CA mutation was determined through testing of blood plasma-derived ctDNA with a next-generation sequencing (NGS) assay performed at the Sponsor-designated central laboratory, or through local testing of ctDNA or tumor tissue with an appropriately validated NGS or PCR assay. Primary endocrine resistance was defined as relapse while on the first 2 years of adjuvant endocrine therapy (ET) and secondary endocrine resistance was defined as relapse while on adjuvant ET after at least 2 years or relapse within 12 months of completing adjuvant ET.
L889: The primary efficacy endpoint for INAVO120 was PFS as assessed by investigator. A prospectively planned sensitivity analysis of PFS by BICR assessment was also performed on all patients. Key secondary endpoints included OS, ORR, and TTCD in worst pain severity, physical functioning, role functioning, and health-related quality of life. DOR was a secondary endpoint.
L890: INAVO120 met its primary endpoint with a median PFS of 15 months (95% CI 11.3, 20.5) vs 7.3 months (95% CI: 5.6, 9.3) favoring inavolisib (HR 0.43, 95% CI: 0.32, 0.59; p-value &lt;0.0001). PFS by BICR results were supportive of the primary endpoint. OS is the key secondary endpoint per the hierarchical testing procedure pre-specified in the SAP. The interim OS analysis at the time of PFS analysis with 63% information fraction was not statistically significant. Interim OS results showed a HR of 0.64 (95% CI: 0.43, 0.97) with median OS not estimable (95% CI: 27.3, NE) on the inavolisib with palbociclib plus fulvestrant arm and 31.1 months (95% CI: 22.3, NE) on the placebo + palbociclib + fulvestrant arm. Thus, there appears to be no detriment in OS at this time. The final analysis of OS is expected in the second quarter of 2025. Results for the secondary endpoints of ORR and DOR appear to support the primary endpoint results. However, since the interim analysis of OS at the time of PFS analysis was not statistically significant, none of the remaining secondary endpoints in the pre-specified testing hierarchy were formally tested at this time.
L891: Time to confirmed deterioration (TTCD) in worst pain severity, physical functioning, role functioning, and health-related quality of life were key secondary endpoints included in the testing hierarchy. In general, FDA has concerns about patient-reported time-to-deterioration endpoints, and this was communicated to the Applicant prior to submission. Further, these results are considered descriptive at this time because the first key secondary endpoint of OS was not statistically significant as noted above.
L892: As noted in the Integrated Assessment of Effectiveness (Section 8.1.4), the results of the GO39374 trial were not considered supportive of expanded indication beyond the population enrolled in INAVO120.
L893: The addition of inavolisib to palbociclib and fulvestrant resulted in a statistically significant and clinically meaningful improvement in PFS based on results from INAVO120. Results for secondary endpoints were supportive of the primary endpoint. However, racial and ethnic minorities reflective of the U.S. population of patients with breast cancer were significantly underrepresented in INAVO120. INAVO120 was a multinational trial with just 16 patients (4.9%) enrolled from the US. Almost all patients were female (98%) and the median age of enrolled patients was 54 years. The majority of patients on study were White, with only two Black patients and 20 Hispanic/Latino patients enrolled on study. Due to the lack of diversity in the enrolled population, a Post Marketing Commitment (PMC) is being issued to further characterize the efficacy and safety of inavolisib with palbociclib and fulvestrant for the treatment of breast cancer in study populations whose demographics are more representative of the population of U.S. patients for the proposed indication.
L894: ## 8.1.4. Assessment of Efficacy Across Trials
L895: **Data and the Applicant's Position:** Because the study designs, patient populations, and the combination regimens differed between Studies WO41554 and GO39374, the efficacy data are presented for each study separately, no pooling of efficacy data between the two studies was conducted.
L896: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L897: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment.

L900: Not applicable.
L901: ## 8.1.5. Integrated Assessment of Effectiveness
L902: **Data:** See Section 8.1.1 Pivotal Study: WO41554 (INAVO120), Phase III and Supportive Study: GO39374, Phase I for efficacy data.
L903: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L904: **Pivotal Study WO41554:** Study WO41554 met its primary endpoint. The addition of inavolisib to the standard of care regimen of palbociclib and fulvestrant reduced the risk of disease progression or death by 57% and more than doubled the median PFS (15.0 months in the Inavo+Palbo+Fulv arm vs. 7.3 months Pbo+Palbo+Fulv arm). The results of the sensitivity analysis of BICR-assessed PFS, the sensitivity analysis of investigator-assessed PFS in patients with centrally determined *PIK3CA* mutation-positive status, and additional sensitivity analyses to assess the impact of stratification and missing data on PFS, were consistent with the primary analysis, and thus overall support the robustness of the observed PFS benefit of Inavo+Palbo+Fulv over Pbo+Palbo+Fulv. The PFS benefit by investigator-assessment favoring the Inavo+Palbo+Fulv arm was generally consistent across pre-defined subgroups.
L905: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219249}
## {Inavolisib}
L906: The investigator-assessed PFS event-free rate at 6 months, 12 months, and 18 months demonstrated early and durable benefit as shown by consistently and substantially higher PFS rates in the Inavo+Palbo+Fulv arm compared with the Pbo+Palbo+Fulv arm starting from the first tumor assessment and throughout the follow-up. Further evidence of consistent efficacy is supported by the interim OS analysis, which although not statistically significant, is highly supportive of the primary endpoint. In addition, the confirmed objective responses were also durable. Of the patients who had a confirmed objective response, the median duration of response was approximately 8.8 months longer in the Inavo+Palbo+Fulv (18.4 months) arm than in the Pbo+Palbo+Fulv arm (9.6 months).
L907: Patient-reported outcomes showed that adding inavolisib to palbociclib and fulvestrant extends time without clinically meaningful worsening of pain severity without contributing additional burden to patients' day-to-day function and overall quality of life.
L908: ***Supportive Study GO39374:*** In Study GO39374, the triplet combination of inavolisib plus palbociclib and fulvestrant or inavolisib plus palbociclib and letrozole resulted in consistent and promising clinical activity in terms of investigator-assessed endpoints of ORR, DOR, CBR, and PFS, regardless of endocrine backbone (letrozole or fulvestrant) and across multiple treatment lines in patients with *PIK3CA*-mutated HR+, HER2– LA/mBC.
L909: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L910: FDA agrees that INAVO120 (Study WO41554) met its primary endpoint of PFS by investigator. Furthermore, the sensitivity analysis for PFS by BICR was also supportive of the primary endpoint. The interim analysis of OS at the time of PFS analysis was not statistically significant but does appear to be supportive of the primary endpoint results. Results for the additional secondary endpoints of ORR and DOR also support the primary endpoint results; however, since the interim analysis of OS at the time of PFS analysis was not statistically significant, none of the remaining secondary endpoints in the pre-specified testing hierarchy were formally tested.
L911: (b) (4)
L912: Data from a phase 1 trial, GO39374, was submitted by the Applicant
L913: GO39374 is an ongoing, open-label, multicenter, Phase I study designed to evaluate the safety, tolerability, and PK of inavolisib administered orally as a single agent in patients with locally advanced or metastatic PIK3CA-mutated solid tumors, including breast cancer, and in combination with standard-of-care endocrine and/or targeted therapies in patients with PIK3CA-mutated locally advanced and metastatic breast cancer. Not all patients enrolled on GO39374 had endocrine resistant breast cancer. Arms B (inavolisib in combination with palbociclib and letrozole), E (inavolisib in combination with palbociclib and fulvestrant), and F (inavolisib in combination with palbociclib, fulvestrant, and metformin) were identified by the Applicant to be the most pertinent to the current NDA submission.
L914: 121
L915: Version date: June 2022 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
L916: **Disclaimer:** In this document, the sections labeled as "Data" and "The Applicant's Position" are completed by the Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.
L917: While, activity was observed across the three treatment arms based on the confirmed ORR and DOR, given the small sample sizes and wide CI of ORR, the contribution of inavolisib to efficacy was unclear in these single arm cohorts. (b) (4)
L918: The addition of inavolisib to palbociclib and fulvestrant resulted in a statistically significant and clinically meaningful improvement in PFS for the patient population enrolled on INAVO120. Results for secondary endpoints were supportive of the primary endpoint. The safety profile for inavolisib is reviewed in Section 8.2 of this Assessment Aid. Overall, the benefit-risk assessment is favorable for the triplet combination treatment with inavolisib + palbociclib + fulvestrant in adults with endocrine resistant, PIK3CA mutated, HR-positive, HER2-negative, locally advanced, or metastatic breast cancer, following recurrence on or within 12 months of adjuvant endocrine therapy. There is not adequate data to support a benefit for use of the triplet combination in patients that do not have PIK3CA mutated advanced breast cancer that is not considered endocrine resistant or for use of a different endocrine therapy other than fulvestrant.
L919: Currently, there are no FDA approved treatments specifically for the first line treatment of patients with PIK3CA mutated locally advanced or metastatic HR-positive, HER2-negative breast cancer considered to have endocrine resistant disease. Alpelisib and capivasertib are two drugs approved by the FDA for use in patients with PIK3CA-mutated advanced breast cancer. However, both are for second line and later treatment and are not specifically for patients with endocrine resistant disease. Addition of CDK4/6 inhibitor to ET has improved the outcomes of patients with advanced HR-positive breast cancer. The addition of inavolisib to this standard of care doublet therapy further improves the outcome specifically for patients with endocrine resistant, PIK3CA-mutated advanced breast cancer.
L920: Patients should be selected for treatment based on the presence of PIK3CA mutation using the FoundationOne Liquid CDx (F1LDx). The sPMA P190032-S023 will be contemporaneously approved with this NDA for inavolisib. A PMC has been issued for tissue testing, as the PMA is planned to be submitted at the end of 2024.
L921: ## 8.2. Review of Safety
L922: **The Applicant's Position:** The clinical safety data is primarily derived from the pivotal Phase III Study WO41554, with supportive data derived from the Phase I Study GO39374 and specifically from Arms B, E and F for the triplet combination (Inavo+Palbo+ET). In Study WO41554, safety data are available from the safety analysis set (SAS) (defined as patients exposed to any study treatment) which included 162 patients in the Inavo+Palbo+fulv arm and 162 patients in the Pbo+Palbo+fulv arm. Supportive safety data from Study GO39374, Arms B (N=33) (Inavo+Palbo+Letro), E (N=20) (Inavo+Palbo+fulv) and F (N=20) (Inavo+Palbo+fulv+Metf), who received the triplet combination of Inavo+Palbo plus an ET, have been included to allow for a comprehensive assessment of the safety profile of triplet combination in a broader patient population with underlying high risk factors for hyperglycemia and multiple prior lines of
L923: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219249}
L924: ## {Inavolisib}
L925: therapies. With the addition of safety data from Arms B, E and F, as well as aggregate data from the overall safety population (Arms A-F) of GO39374 (N = 190), the Sponsor considers the totality of safety data from approximately 350 patients treated with inavolisib as a single agent and in combination therapy across multiple lines of therapy as adequate to characterize the safety and tolerability profile of inavolisib.
L926: Pooling across the studies or presenting data from both studies side-by-side was not considered appropriate due to differences in the patient population, treatment regimens, the range of inavolisib dose levels, and the differences in AE grading criteria for these two studies.
L927: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L928: The safety analyses were based primarily on FDA's independent review of data from the phase 3 INAVO120 (WO41554) trial. The FDA's safety analysis focused on deaths and treatment emergent adverse events (TEAEs) including serious adverse events (SAEs), TEAEs leading to trial drug discontinuation, dose reduction, or dosage interruption, grade 3-4 TEAEs, and all-grade TEAEs. The FDA also reviewed laboratory data. AEs of special interest included hyperglycemia, stomatitis, and diarrhea.
L929: Topline safety results were reviewed for GO39374 by FDA. In general, no new safety signals were noted from the GO39374 data compared to the safety data from INAVO120. In depth safety analyses were not conducted by FDA for the GO39374 trial, as this trial was not considered to be supportive of expanded indication.
L930: ## 8.2.1. Safety Review Approach
L931: The Applicant's Position: The safety and tolerability assessments consisted of the monitoring and recording of AEs, including SAEs and protocol-specified selected AEs, performing protocol-specified safety laboratory assessments, and assessing protocol-specified vital signs, ECG, ECOG performance status and PRO -CTCAE assessments.
L932: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L933: FDA agrees that the safety review approach was appropriate to evaluate the safety profile for inavolisib.
L934: ## 8.2.2. Review of the Safety Database
L935: ## Overall Exposure
L936: Data and the Applicant's Position: See Table 26, Table 27, and Table 28 for an overview of study treatment exposure. The median relative dose intensity of inavolisib in the Inavo+Palbo+Fulv arm (95.8%) of Study WO41554 was high and comparable with the median dose intensity of inavolisib in Study GO39374 Arm B (95.5%), Arm E (93.8%), Arm F (92.0%) and the overall safety population (96.6%), demonstrating good tolerability of a triplet combination with palbociclib,
L937: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219249}
L938: {Inavolisib}
L939: plus fulvestrant, or letrozole, across a broad patient population. Furthermore, the median relative dose intensities of combination therapy components were also high and comparable within Study GO39374 and between studies, showing the addition of inavolisib did not compromise the dose intensity of palbociclib, fulvestrant and letrozole.

L967: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L968: The FDA's review of safety population for this application is primarily from the INAVO120 Trial (WO41554). FDA agrees that the exposure is adequate to characterize safety of inavolisib. The median relative dose intensity of inavolisib in the Inavo+Palbo+Fulv arm (95.8%) of the INAVO120 (WO41554) trial was high. In addition, the median relative dose intensity of palbociclib and fulvestrant was similar between the two treatment arms of the INAVO120 (WO41554) trial; therefore, the triplet combination therapy including inavolisib did not have a significant impact on the ability to administer full dose of palbociclib+fulvestrant.
L969: In depth exposure analyses were not conducted by FDA for the GO39374 trial.
L970: ## Relevant characteristics of the safety population:
L971: **Data and the Applicant's Position:** The safety population in WO41554 was similar to FAS. Refer to Section 8.1.1 for the summary of demographics and baseline characteristics in the FAS population for WO41554 and SAS population for GO39374, and Section 8.1 for an overview of the eligibility criteria. Study GO39374 enrolled patients with broader HbA1c and fasting blood glucose (FBG) criteria to allow for the characterization of the risk of hyperglycemia in patients with underlying risk factors.
L972: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L973: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment that the safety population for the INAVO120 trial (WO41554) was similar to the FAS. FDA agrees the safety population for GO39374 is the same as the SAS population summarized in Section 8.1.1.
L974: ## Adequacy of the safety database:
L975: **The Applicant's Position:** The evaluation of safety is primarily derived from the pivotal Study WO41554, with supportive data derived from Study GO39374 to support the adequate characterization of efficacy, safety, and tolerability of inavolisib in combination with palbociclib and an ET in patients with *PIK3CA*-mutated HR+, HER2- LA/mBC.
L976: Data from Study GO39374 will supplement the safety database for inavolisib based combinations (aggregate data from Arms A-F), including additional data for patients, who were enrolled with higher baseline FBG (\le 140 mg/dL) and HbA1c levels (HbA1c &lt;7%) compared to the eligibility criteria for Study WO41554 trial. This will further support the applicability of the inavolisib triplet combination in patients with underlying risk factors for hyperglycemia.
L977: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L978: The FDA conducted in depth safety analyses based on data from the phase 3 INAVO120 trial. Topline safety results were reviewed by FDA for GO39374.
L979: ## 8.2.3. Adequacy of Applicant's Clinical Safety Assessments
L980: ## Issues Regarding Data Integrity and Submission Quality
L981: **The Applicant's Position:** No concerns impacting data integrity, quality, and reporting were identified for the studies included in this submission.
L982: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L983: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment.
L984: ## Categorization of Adverse Event
L985: **The Applicant's Position:** Verbatim descriptions for AEs and diseases entered by the investigators were assigned to Preferred Terms (PTs) by using MedDRA v26.1 (in Study WO41554) and v26.0 (in Study GO39374).
L986: The AE severity grading scale for the NCI CTCAE v5.0 (in Study WO41554) and v4.0 (in Study GO39374) was used for assessing AE severity and routine laboratory parameters. For events of varying severity, the highest grade was used in the summaries, unless specified otherwise. In the summary tables, patients who experienced the same event on more than one occasion were counted once and at the highest grade in the calculation of the event frequency and severity.
L987: Selected AEs for inavolisib were defined based on known and evolving safety profile of inavolisib, known risks for the combination agents and protocol-defined AESI. These selected events are grouped by medical concepts as described in Table 29.

L994: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L995: In general, FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment for categorization of adverse events.
L996: FDA's grouped term (GT) for rash differed slightly from the one used by the Applicant. This difference resulted in identifying a few additional patients. The adverse events for the additional patients included eczema, erythema multiforme, palmar-plantar erythrodysesthesia, and skin exfoliation. The incidence of rash was updated by FDA in the USPI reflecting this grouping of PTs.
L997: ## Routine Clinical Tests
L998: **The Applicant's Position:** Patients were closely monitored for safety and tolerability throughout the conduct of studies. The assessments performed were considered adequate for the expected toxicities associated with inavolisib and the combination agents. Patients were assessed for toxicity at each clinic visit and prior to each in-clinic dose of study treatment; dosing occurred only if the clinical assessments and local laboratory test values were acceptable. The assessments included, complete physical exam, ECOG performance status, vital signs, tumor and response evaluations, laboratory tests (includes but not limited to hematology, chemistry, fasting insulin, fasting lipid profile, amylase and lipase, HbA1c, serum fibrinogen and coagulation, urinalysis, pregnancy test, PK analysis, and biomarker assays), ophthalmic examinations, electrocardiograms, and clinical outcome assessments.
L999: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1000: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment.
