L1010: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1011: **Pivotal Study WO41554:** At the time of CCOD (29 Sep 2023), a total of 97 patients (29.9%) had died due to any cause. The proportion of patients who died in the Inavo+Palbo+Fulv arm (26.5%) was lower in comparison with the Pbo+Palbo+Fulv arm (33.3%). The most common cause of death in both treatment arms was progressive disease (Table 30). Grade 5 AEs were reported in 6 patients (3.7%) in the Inavo+Palbo+Fulv arm and 2 patients (1.2%) of patients in the Pbo+Palbo+Fulv arm (Table 31). None of the Grade 5 AEs were reported as related to any study drug by the investigator and no Grade 5 AE by PT was observed in &gt;1 patient in either arm.
L1012: **Supportive Study GO39374:** As of the CCOD (27 March 2023), 7 deaths have been reported in
L1013: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219249}
L1014: ## {Inavolisib}
L1015: the study. All deaths occurred within 30 days following the last study treatment administration. Among these events, 4 deaths (one in Arm A, two in Arm C, and one in Arm F) were due to disease progression. The remaining 3 deaths were due to Grade 5 AEs and were all reported from Arm D (Inavo+Fulv). These Grade 5 AEs (by PT) were: hypertrophic cardiomyopathy, peritonitis, and pleural effusion; none of the events were considered related to inavolisib or fulvestrant by the investigator.
L1016: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1017: In general, FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment of deaths in the INAVO120 trial (WO41554). FDA agrees that the most common cause for death on either treatment arm was progressive disease.
L1018: The death narratives were not reviewed for GO39374.
L1019: Following are a summary of the 8 deaths (6 on the inavolisib treatment arm and 2 on the placebo arm) due to an adverse event in the INAVO120 Trial (WO41554). The FDA included these deaths in the USPI as contribution of inavolisib cannot be ruled out for the 6 deaths (3.7%) that occurred on the inavolisib + palbociclib + fulvestrant arm.
L1020: ## Patient ID# (b) (6) --Applicant Attribution: Cerebral hemorrhage
L1021: 60-year-old White female randomized to the *inavolisib* treatment arm was diagnosed with Grade 3 acute coronary syndrome (ACS) on Study Day 493. On Study Day 499, the event of ACS was considered resolved with sequelae (unspecified). There was no change in study treatment due to ACS. On Study Day 500, the patient was diagnosed with Grade 4 cerebral hemorrhage. No treatment was given for the event and the patient died on Study Day 506 due to cerebral hemorrhage. The patient also had six episodes of Grade 2 hyperglycemia during study treatment, all of which resolved. It was unknown if an autopsy was performed.
L1022: The Investigator considered acute coronary syndrome and cerebral hemorrhage to be unrelated to inavolisib, fulvestrant, and palbociclib and related to other cause (unspecified).
L1023: Concurrent condition at the time of study entry included blood cholesterol increased. FDA cannot rule whether inavolisib contributed to the ACS event and subsequent cerebral hemorrhage. It is not clear if the patient was on an anticoagulant during or after the diagnosis of ACS. The patient did have reported episodes of Grade 1 platelet count decreased; however, it is unlikely this contributed to the event of cerebral hemorrhage.
L1024: ## Patient ID# (b) (6) --Applicant Attribution: Acute Coronary Syndrome
L1025: 57-year-old Asian female randomized to the *inavolisib* treatment arm was diagnosed with life threatening cardiac arrest on Study Day 4. The patient's condition deteriorated in the ambulance on the way to the hospital and died in the ambulance. An autopsy was not performed.
L1026: Version date: June 2022 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
L1027: **Disclaimer:** In this document, the sections labeled as "Data" and "The Applicant's Position" are completed by the Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.
L1028: *The Investigator considered acute coronary syndrome to be unrelated to placebo, fulvestrant, and palbociclib and related to disease under study.*
L1029: *Pertinent concurrent conditions at the time of study entry included myocardial fibrosis, essential hypertension, and pericarditis. It is possible these conditions contributed to the ACS event. It is difficult to assess the cause of cardiac arrest and FDA cannot rule whether inavolisib contributed to the ACS event.*
L1030: ## **Patient ID#** (b) (6) --Applicant Attribution: Gastrointestinal hemorrhage
L1031: *42-year-old Asian female randomized to the inavolisib treatment arm was diagnosed with gastrointestinal hemorrhage on Study Day 491 (symptoms not reported). Cardiopulmonary resuscitation was performed however the patient died. The patient had progression of disease in non-target lesions on Study Day 443 and treatment was permanently discontinued on Study Day 460 with the last dose of palbociclib administered on Study Day 453, fulvestrant on Study Day 454, and inavolisib on Study Day 460. An autopsy was not performed.*
L1032: *The Investigator considered acute coronary syndrome to be unrelated to placebo, fulvestrant, and palbociclib and related to disease under study.*
L1033: *FDA agrees that the event of gastrointestinal hemorrhage was unlikely related to inavolisib as study treatment had been discontinued more than 30 days prior. The patient had no reported adverse event of thrombocytopenia that may have contributed to bleeding.*
L1034: ## **Patient ID#** (b) (6) --Applicant Attribution: Death
L1035: *74-year-old White female randomized to the inavolisib treatment arm was reported to have poor condition and not get up on Study Day 276. On Study Day 277, the participant died, and the reason of death was unknown as per death certificate. An autopsy was not performed.*
L1036: *The Investigator considered death to be unrelated to inavolisib, fulvestrant, palbociclib and related to other cause (disease progression).*
L1037: *Given the limited information, FDA considers it difficult to assess the cause of death. Although the death may be due to progression of disease, no clear evidence has been presented, even after FDA review of information requests to the Applicant that were unable to provide additional clinical information for attribution.*
L1038: ## **Patient ID#** (b) (6) --Applicant Attribution: COVID-19

It was unknown if an autopsy was performed.*

L1046: Patient ID# [REDACTED] (b) (6) --Applicant Attribution: COVID-19 pneumonia

No known treatment was administered for the event as it was reported that medical documents were not available as participant was hospitalized in another hospital. On Study Day 67, the participant died due to COVID-19 pneumonia. An autopsy was not performed.*
L1048: *The Investigator considered COVID-19 pneumonia to be unrelated to placebo, fulvestrant and palbociclib and unrelated to other cause (unspecified).*
L1049: *FDA agrees with the diagnosis of COVID-19 and agrees the death due to pneumonia was likely due to COVID-19.*
L1050: Patient ID# [REDACTED] (b) (6) --Applicant Attribution: Cardiac Arrest
L1051: *57-year-old Asian female randomized to the placebo treatment arm was diagnosed with Grade 4 cardiac arrest on Study Day 4. The patient died later the same day due to cardiac arrest. An autopsy was not performed.*
L1052: *The Investigator considered cardiac arrest to be unrelated to placebo, fulvestrant and palbociclib and related to disease under study.*
L1053: *Pertinent concurrent conditions at the time of study entry include hypertension and hyperlipidemia. Given the limited information, it is difficult to assess the cause of cardiac arrest.*
L1054: ## Serious Adverse Events
L1055: ## Data and the Applicant's Position:
L1056: **Pivotal Study WO41554:** A numerically higher proportion of patients experienced SAEs in the Inavo+Palbo+Fulv arm compared with the Pbo+Palbo+Fulv arm (24.1% vs. 10.5%, respectively). The SAEs were distributed across multiple PTs and no SAE was reported with a \ge 2\% difference between treatment arms. The majority of SAEs were Grade 3 in severity in both the Inavo+Palbo+Fulv arm (16.7%) and the Pbo+Palbo+Fulv arm (7.4%). The incidence of SAEs of any grade in the Infections and Infestations System Organ Class (SOC) was higher in the Inavo+Palbo+Fulv arm (11 [6.8%]) compared with the Pbo+Palbo+Fulv arm (3 [1.9%]) and was primarily driven by PTs of COVID-19 (4 [2.5%] vs. 1 [0.6%]), pneumonia (3 [1.9%] vs. 0) and urinary tract infection (2 [1.2%] vs. 0), which were all assessed by investigator as not related to any study drug.
L1057: The SAEs occurring in \ge 2 patients in either arm were: COVID-19 (4 [2.5%] Inavo+Palbo+Fulv vs. 1 [0.6%] Pbo+Palbo+Fulv), pneumonia, anemia, febrile neutropenia (3 [1.9%] vs. 0 each), urinary tract infection, diarrhea, pyrexia, and acute coronary syndrome (2 [1.2%] vs. 0 each), vomiting (1 [0.6%] vs. 2 [1.2%]), and pulmonary embolism (0 vs. 2 [1.2%]).
L1058: **Supportive Study GO39374:** In the overall safety population, 85 SAEs were reported in 27.4% of patients. SAEs occurring in \ge 2 patients (1.1% overall) included: hyperglycemia (4 [2.1%]), cellulitis, hypercalcemia, and urinary tract infection (3 each [1.6%]), and back pain, headache, osteomyelitis, pneumonia, rash, small intestinal obstructions, superior vena cava syndrome, and vomiting (2 each [1.1%]); all other events were reported in one patient each. SAEs assessed by the investigator as related to any study treatment (except metformin) were reported in 14 patients (7.4%).
L1059: Amongst Arms B, E, and F,
L1060: - In Arm B (Inavo+Palbo+Letro; n=33 patients), 25 SAEs were reported in 42.4% of patients (14 of 33 patients). Cellulitis occurred in 2 patients (7.4%) in Arm B; the remainder of SAEs were single occurrences observed in one patient each (3.7%).
- In Arm E (Inavo+Palbo+Fulv; n=20 patients), 9 SAEs were reported in 30.0% of patients (6 of 20 patients). No SAE occurred in more than one patient.
- In Arm F (Inavo+Palbo+Fulv+Met; n=20 patients), 15 SAEs were reported in 40.0% of patients (8 of 20 patients). No SAE occurred in more than one patient.
L1061: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1062: FDA agrees that the incidence of SAEs were higher on the inavolisib treatment arm in the INAVO120 trial (WO41554). The highest incidence of SAEs on the inavolisib treatment arm occurred in the System Organ Class (SOC) of Infections and Infestations (6.8%), followed by Gastrointestinal disorders (4.3%) and Blood and Lymphatic disorders (4.3%). Most of the

Patients on the inavolisib arm were on treatment for a longer duration (compared to the placebo arm) with potential concomitant neutropenia, which may have led a higher rate of infections. However, it is not known if inavolisib may have exacerbated the risk of infection.

L1080: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1081: **Pivotal Study WO41554:** The proportion of patients who experienced AEs leading to withdrawal of any study drug was overall low in both arms (Inavo+Palbo+Fulv: 6.8% vs. Pbo+Palbo+Fulv: 0.6%), indicating manageability of AEs and good tolerability of inavolisib in combination with palbociclib and fulvestrant (Table and Table ).
L1082: **Supportive Study GO39374:** In the overall safety population, AEs leading to withdrawal of any study treatment were reported in 7 patients (3.7%) and all AEs were single occurrences observed in one patient each. AEs leading to withdrawal of any study drug in Arm B, Arm E, or Arm F were infrequent. Overall, 2 patients each in Arm B, Arm E, and Arm F withdrew from treatment due to an AE; AEs that led to treatment withdrawals were: cerebrovascular disorder and hyperglycemia (one patient each in Arm B), asthenia and diarrhea (one patient each in Arm E), panniculitis (one patient in Arm F), esophageal candidiasis and hypocalcemia (both events in one patient in Arm F).
L1083: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1084: More patients on the inavolisib treatment arm in the INAVO120 trial (WO41554) discontinued treatment due to an AE compared to the placebo arm. The only adverse event that occurred in more than one patient (2 patients) leading to treatment discontinuation on the inavolisib treatment arm was hyperglycemia. All other adverse events occurred in one patient.
L1085: The most common reason for treatment discontinuation was progression of disease, which occurred in a high proportion of patients on the placebo arm (62%) compared to the inavolisib treatment arm (42%).
L1086: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment of discontinuations for GO39374.

L1098: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1099: **Pivotal Study WO41554:** A numerically higher proportion of patients in the Inavo+Palbo+Fulv arm (69.1%) compared with the Pbo+Palbo+Fulv arm (34.6%) experienced AEs leading to the dose interruption of inavolisib/placebo. A numerically higher proportion of patients in the Inavo+Palbo+Fulv arm (23 patients [14.2%]) compared with the Pbo+Palbo+Fulv arm (5 patients [3.1%]) experienced AEs leading to the dose reduction of inavolisib/placebo (Table 34). The most common PT leading to dose reductions (2.5%) or interruptions (24.7%) in the Inavo+Palbo+Fulv arm was hyperglycemia.
L1100: **Supportive Study GO39374:** In the overall safety population, AEs leading to dose modification or interruption of inavolisib were reported in 64.7% of patients, with hyperglycemia (36.8%) being the most frequently reported AE (Table 35). Hyperglycemia was the most commonly reported AE leading to dose modification or interruption of inavolisib in Arm B (27.3%), Arm E (40.0%) and Arm F (55.0%).
L1101: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1102: FDA agrees that a higher proportion of patients on the inavolisib treatment arm in the INAVO120 Trial (WO41554) required a dose interruption or dose reduction compared to the placebo arm. The most common AE leading to an interruption of inavolisib treatment was hyperglycemia, followed by COVID-19, neutropenia, diarrhea, and mucosal inflammation. The most common cause leading to dose reduction in the inavolisib treatment arm was hyperglycemia (in 2.5% of patients). Hyperglycemia, diarrhea, and stomatitis/mucosal inflammation are known side effects of PI3K inhibitors, and these adverse events occurred at a higher incidence in the inavolisib treatment arm compared to the placebo arm. These toxicities could be managed with dose interruption or dose reduction of inavolisib without permanent discontinuation of treatment.
L1103: COVID-19 occurred in a higher proportion of patient on the inavolisib treatment arm compared
L1104: to the placebo arm and led to a higher rate of dose modifications in the inavolisib treatment arm. The INAVO120 trial was during the COVID-19 pandemic and the higher incidence on the inavolisib treatment arm is likely due to the longer duration of treatment (and potentially with neutropenia) compared to the placebo arm. However, it is not clear if inavolisib may have exacerbated the COVID-19 infection.
L1105: Hematologic toxicities, including neutropenia, anemia, and thrombocytopenia, are known side effects of palbociclib. These toxicities occurred in a similar proportion of patients in both treatment arms; however, dose modifications occurred more on the inavolisib treatment arm, likely due to longer duration of treatment compared to the placebo arm.
L1106: ## Significant Adverse Events
L1107: ## Data and the Applicant's Position:
L1108: ***Pivotal Study WO41554:*** The incidence of Grade 3-4 AEs by highest grade was comparable between the Inavo+Palbo+Fulv (85.8%) and Pbo+Palbo+Fulv (82.1%) arms (Table 36). The incidence rates were driven by Grade 3-4 AEs (by PTs) of neutropenia (47.5% vs. 48.1%) and neutrophil count decreased (34.6% vs. 32.7%). The majority of Grade 3-4 AEs in both treatment arms were associated with myelosuppression. Other common Grade 3-4 AEs (by PTs) were hyperglycemia (Inavo+Palbo+Fulv: 5.6% and Pbo+Palbo+Fulv: 0%), diarrhea (3.7% vs. 0%) and stomatitis/oral mucositis (3.1% vs. 0%). The proportion of patients with Grade 3-4 AEs related to any treatment were comparable between the arms (85.2% vs. 80.2%). See Section 8.2.4 for further information on Grade 5 AEs.
L1109: ***Supportive Study GO39374:*** In the overall GO39374 safety population the most frequent Grade 3-4 AEs (\ge 10\%) were hyperglycemia (24.2%) and neutropenia (23.7%). All Grade 3-4 AEs for neutropenia were reported from palbociclib-containing arms (Arm B, Arm E, and Arm F). The most frequently reported Grade 3-4 AE (\ge 50\% in each arm) among patients in Arm B and Arm E was neutropenia (66.7% and 70.0%, respectively). In Arm F, neutropenia (45.0%) and hyperglycemia (45.0%) were the most frequently reported Grade 3-4 AEs.
L1110: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1111: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment. Hyperglycemia, diarrhea, and stomatitis/mucositis are discussed further in Section 8.2.5 (Submission Specific Safety Issues).
L1112: Hematologic toxicities were seen in both treatment arms and are known toxicities with palbociclib. Neutropenia and anemia were seen in a similar proportion of patients regarding all grade toxicity. However, grade 3-4 toxicity was slightly higher in the inavolisib treatment arm as noted in Tables 23 and 24. Thrombocytopenia (including platelet count decreased) was higher in the inavolisib treatment arm compared to the placebo treatment arm. There did not appear to be a significantly increased rate of bleeding events associated with the thrombocytopenia. All grade hemorrhage (grouped term) events occurred in 10% of patients on
L1113: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219249}
L1114: {Inavolisib}
L1115: the inavolisib treatment arm and 6% on the placebo arm.
L1116: The most common significant adverse events in GO39374 were neutropenia and hyperglycemia.

L1140: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1141: **Pivotal Study WO41554:** Inavolisib in combination with palbociclib and fulvestrant in patients with PIK3CA-mutated, HR+, HER2- LA/mBC demonstrated manageable safety and tolerability profile as shown by the high median relative dose intensity of inavolisib, the comparable median relative dose intensities of palbociclib and fulvestrant in both arms, and low treatment discontinuation rate of the Inavo+Palbo+Fulv combination due to AEs. The safety profile of the Inavo+Palbo+Fulv combination regimen was consistent with the known safety profile of each individual study drug component and the underlying disease. No new safety findings were identified. The addition of inavolisib did not contribute to increased treatment burden in patients, as indicated by the majority of patients in both arms reporting levels of selected symptoms from the PRO-CTCAE and overall bother from treatment side effects at moderate levels or less. A summary of key AE-related safety results are presented in Table 36.
L1142: Supportive Study GO39374: Inavolisib as monotherapy and in combination therapies with palbociclib, letrozole, fulvestrant, and/or metformin was generally well tolerated, toxicities were manageable, and AEs were in line with the anticipated safety profile of inavolisib, the known safety profiles of the individual components of combination therapies, and the underlying disease. At the time of CCOD, there were no new or unexpected safety signals identified for inavolisib in this study. A summary of key AE-related safety results are presented in Table 37.
L1143: For the purpose of labeling the Applicant is proposing Table 38 for the USPI to reflect the safety profile of inavolisib.
L1144: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1145: All grade adverse events occurred in almost all patients in both arms of INAVO120. A higher proportion of patients experienced SAEs in the inavolisib treatment arm compared to the placebo arm and more patients in the inavolisib treatment arm had treatment discontinuations and dose modifications (both interruptions and dose reductions). Grade 5 adverse events, SAEs and dose modifications are discussed in more detail in the respective sections above. Modifications have been made to the Applicant Table 38 by FDA for inclusion in the USPI, as noted in Table 39, as FDA grouped additional PT terms for certain adverse reactions which the Applicant agreed to. In addition, (b) (4) was removed from the adverse reaction table (b) (4)

L1169: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1170: **Pivotal Study WO41554:** Overall, clinically relevant shifts (defined as shifts from Grade 0, 1, or 2 at baseline to Grade 3 or 4 post-baseline) in blood chemistry, coagulation, and hematology during study treatment were infrequent and comparable between the Inavo+Palbo+Fulv and Pbo+Palbo+Fulv arms. The most commonly occurring clinically relevant hematology shift from baseline was low total absolute neutrophils (81.7% Inavo+Palbo+Fulv vs. 78.5% Pbo+Palbo+Fulv). Regardless of baseline fasting blood glucose, the majority of patients (64.8%) in the Inavo+Palbo+Fulv arm maintained fasting blood glucose &lt;160 mg/dL. There was 1 patient in the Inavo+Palbo+Fulv arm and 3 patients in the Pbo+Palbo+Fulv arm who fulfilled the laboratory criteria for potential Hy's law. However, all of these cases were confounded by concurrent worsening of liver metastasis and did not meet the criteria for drug-induced liver injury.
L1171: **Supportive Study GO39374:** For the overall safety population, the most commonly occurring clinically relevant hematology shift from baseline was low total absolute neutrophils (48 patients [25.5%]), while the most commonly occurring clinically relevant biochemistry shifts from baseline were high fasting glucose (34 patients [18.3%]) and high glucose (15 patients [12.8%]).
L1172: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1173: The most common laboratory abnormalities in the INAVO120 trial were observed in the hematologic parameters. The hematologic abnormalities were noted in both treatment arms and are known to occur with palbociclib treatment. Platelets decreased (all grade and grade 3-4) were higher in the inavolisib treatment arm compared to the placebo arm.
L1174: Increased fasting glucose occurred in a larger proportion of patients on the inavolisib treatment arm compared to placebo. Increased fasting glucose corresponds with a known toxicity of hyperglycemia for PI3K inhibitors, including inavolisib. Patients were required to have a HbA1c &lt;6% and fasting blood glucose &lt;126 to be eligible for the INAVO 120 trial.
L1175: Laboratory abnormalities were not reviewed in the GO39374 Trial.
L1176: ## Vital Signs
L1177: ## Data and the Applicant's Position:
L1178: ***Pivotal Study WO41554:*** There were no clinically relevant differences between the treatment arms with respect to vital sign parameters during the study.
L1179: ***Supportive Study GO39374:*** Although there were vital sign parameters outside normal limits noted among patients without an abnormality at baseline, these were not considered to be clinically relevant.
L1180: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1181: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment for Study WO41554.
L1182: Vital signs were not reviewed for Study GO39374.
L1183: ## Electrocardiograms (ECGs)
L1184: ## Data and the Applicant's Position:
L1185: ***Pivotal Study WO41554:*** The baseline ECG data were comparable between the arms. No clinically meaningful trends post-baseline vs. baseline in mean ECG parameters were identified in either of the arms.
L1186: ***Supportive Study GO39374:*** Overall, the ECG data showed no clinically meaningful trends post-baseline vs. baseline in mean ECG parameters.
L1187: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1188: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment. No clinically meaningful change (\ge 20 msec mean increase in QTc interval) was noted. Refer to FDA QT-IRT review.
L1189: ## QT
L1190: ## Data:
L1191: ***Pivotal Study WO41554:*** PTs in the Investigations SOC included ECG QT prolonged (6 patients [3.7%] Inavo+Palbo+fulv vs. 1 patient [0.6%] Pbo+Palbo+fulv) and ECG T wave abnormal (1 patient [0.6%] vs. no patient [0%]). In the Inavo+Palbo+fulv arm, one patient (0.6%) experienced a Grade 3 event of ECG QT prolonged, 2 patients (1.2%) a Grade 2 event and 3 patients (1.9%) a Grade 1 event. The one event of ECG T wave abnormal in the Inavo+Palbo+fulv arm was reported as Grade 1 in the single patient (0.6%). The Grade 3 event of ECG QT prolonged was considered related to inavolisib, required treatment, led to dose reduction of inavolisib and was considered recovering/resolving at the CCOD (29 Sep 2023). There were no SAEs reported.
L1192: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219249}
## {Inavolisib}
L1193: *Supportive Study GO39374:* Six patients experienced AEs within the Investigations SOC related to ECG abnormalities, which included ECG QT prolonged (2.6%) and ECG ST segment depression (0.5%).
L1194: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1195: Although in Study GO39374, there is limited ECG data with the triplet combination (inavolisib+ palbociclib+ fulvestrant in Arms E and F) that was selected for the Phase III study, the data from single agent inavolisib arms (Arm A), doublet arms (Arm C: inavolisib+ letrozole and Arm D: inavolisib+ fulvestrant), and the other triplet arm (Arm B: inavolisib+ palbociclib+ letrozole), can be extrapolated to the inavolisib+ palbociclib+ fulvestrant triplet combination due to following reasons:
L1196: - Inavolisib exposure is comparable between all the arms, hence no additional potential for QT prolongation is expected with the triplet combination.
- No risk of QT prolongation has been reported for the combination agents, palbociclib and fulvestrant.
L1197: Based on the concentration-QTc analysis from study GO39374, it would not be expected that inavolisib would have a clinically relevant QTc prolongation effect.
L1198: No clinically significant trends in post-baseline vs. baseline mean ECG parameters were identified in both pivotal and supportive studies.
L1199: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1200: The Interdisciplinary Review Team for Cardiac Safety Studies at the FDA completed a QT review and concluded that inavolisib at the proposed dosage of 9mg daily is unlikely to cause \ge 20 msec mean increase in QTc interval based on the alternative QT assessment pathway. See Section 6.3.1 for additional details.
L1201: *See the full review by the IRT for Cardiac Safety Studies (QT-IRT) for further details.*
L1202: ## Immunogenicity
L1203: **Data and the Applicant's Position:** Not applicable; immunogenicity assessments were not performed in both studies.
L1204: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1205: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment.
L1206: ## 8.2.5. Analysis of Submission-Specific Safety Issues
L1207: ## **Data and the Applicant's Position:**
L1208: *Pivotal Study WO41554:* A comparable proportion of patients reported selected AEs (based on the known and evolving safety profile of inavolisib and protocol-defined AESI and analyzed using grouped terms) in the Inavo+Palbo+Fulv arm (96.9%) and the Pbo+Palbo+Fulv arm (96.3%). The majority of selected AEs were of Grade 1 or 2 in severity with the exception of
L1209: neutropenia in which Grade 3-4 neutropenia was reported in 80.2% of patients in the Inavo+Palbo+Fulv arm and 78.4% of patients in the Pbo+Palbo+Fulv arm. There were no Grade 5 selected AEs in either arm.
L1210: The majority of patients who experienced a selected AE of hyperglycemia (known on-target, reversible toxicity associated with PI3K pathway inhibition) in the Inavo+Palbo+Fulv arm had Grade 1 (16.0%) or Grade 2 (37.0%) events; Grade 3 events were reported in 5.6% of patients in the Inavo+Palbo+Fulv arm and no Grade 4-5 events were reported; all events were non-serious, and manageable with oral anti-hyperglycemic agents and dose reductions (2.5%) or interruptions (27.8%) in all patients including pre-diabetic patients enrolled in the Inavo+Palbo+Fulv arm. The majority of events of hyperglycemia in the Inavo+Palbo+Fulv arm were reversible with only 2 patients (1.2%) discontinuing from the study due to AEs of hyperglycemia and Type 2 diabetes mellitus. Among the inavolisib-treated patients who had a key selected event of hyperglycemia, the median time to first onset was 7 days (range: 2.0 to 955.0 days). Frequency of new onset of key selected AE of hyperglycemia was highest during the first 2 months of treatment.
L1211: The majority of patients who experienced a selected AE of diarrhea had Grade 1 (27.8%) or Grade 2 (16.7%) events in the Inavo+Palbo+Fulv arm, and more patients experienced all-grade diarrhea events in the Inavo+Palbo+Fulv arm (48.1%) compared with the Pbo+Palbo+Fulv arm (16.0%). Grade 3 events were reported in 3.7% of patients. Two patients (1.2%) in the Inavo+Palbo+Fulv arm experienced diarrhea SAEs, both of which were Grade 3 in severity and recovered/resolved at the CCOD. No patient experienced a Grade 4 or 5 diarrhea event in either arm and no event led to withdrawal of any study drug in either arm. In the Inavo+Palbo+Fulv arm, diarrhea events led to dose reduction of any study drug in 2 patients (1.2%) and dose interruption of any study drug in 12 patients (7.4%). Among the inavolisib-treated patients who had a key selected event of diarrhea, the median time to first onset was 15 days (range: 2.0 to 602.0 days). Frequency of new onset of key selected AE of diarrhea was highest during the first month.
L1212: All patients who experienced a selected AE of rash had Grade 1 (Inavo+Palbo+Fulv: 21.0%) or Grade 2 events (4.3%). There were no Grade 3 or 4 events, no rash events that led to withdrawal of any study drug and no patient experienced an SAE of a rash event during the study in either arm. In the Inavo+Palbo+Fulv arm, rash events led to dose reduction of any study drug in 1 patient (0.6%) and dose interruption of any study drug in 3 patients (1.9%). Among the inavolisib-treated patients who had key selected event of rash, the median time to first onset was 29 days (range: 1.0 to 952.0 days). There was no clear trend in terms of the frequency of new onset of key selected AE of rash.
L1213: The majority of patients who experienced a selected AE of stomatitis and mucosal inflammation had Grade 1 (32.1%) or Grade 2 (13.6%) events in the Inavo+Palbo+Fulv arm. Grade 3 stomatitis/mucosal inflammation events were only reported in patients in the Inavo+Palbo+Fulv
L1214: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219249}
L1215: ## {Inavolisib}
L1216: arm (5.6% of patients) and no Grade 4, Grade 5 or SAEs were reported in either arm. In the Inavo+Palbo+Fulv arm, stomatitis and mucosal inflammation events led to withdrawal, dose reduction, and dose interruption of any study drug in 1 patient (0.6%), 6 patients (3.7%), and 19 patients (11.7%) respectively. Among the inavolisib-treated patients who had key selected event of stomatitis and mucosal inflammation, the median time to first onset was 13 days (range: 1.0 to 610.0 days). Frequency of new onset of a key selected AE of stomatitis and mucosal inflammation was highest during the first 3 months.
L1217: Neutropenia events were well-balanced between the two arms. The majority of patients who experienced a selected AE of neutropenia had Grade 3 (64.2%) or Grade 4 (16.0%) events in the Inavo+Palbo+Fulv arm. No patient experienced a Grade 5 neutropenia event in either arm. Neutropenia events led to withdrawal of any study drug in 1 patient (0.6%) in the Inavo+Palbo+Fulv arm. In the Inavo+Palbo+Fulv arm, neutropenia events led to dose reduction of any study drug in 30.9% of patients and dose interruption of any study drug in 56.8% of patients.
L1218: **Supportive Study GO39374:** For the overall safety population, the most commonly reported (\ge 50\%) selected AEs (by grouped terms) were hyperglycemia (66.8%), diarrhea (64.7%), and nausea (56.8%), which were manageable with standard of care measures and dose modifications or interruptions. In Arm B, Arm E, and Arm F, the most commonly reported selected AEs (\ge 50\% in each arm) were, respectively: hyperglycemia (63.6% in Arm B, 70.0% in Arm E and Arm F), diarrhea (75.8%, 85.0% and 60.0%), nausea (51.5%, 70.0% and 60.0%), stomatitis and mucosal inflammation (66.7, 90.0% and 55.0%) and neutropenia (81.8%, 85.0%, 55.0%), which were manageable with standard of care measures and dose modifications or interruptions. Only one patient in Arm E had a selected AE of diarrhea leading to withdrawal of all study drugs including inavolisib.
L1219: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1220: Hyperglycemia is a known toxicity with PI3K inhibitors. Patients were required to have FBG&lt;126 mg/dL and HbA1C&lt;6% at study entry for INAVO 120. Increased fasting glucose (based on laboratory abnormality) occurred in 85% of patients treated with inavolisib, including Grade 3 events in 12% of patients. There were no grade 4 or 5 events or cases of diabetic ketoacidosis (DKA) or hyperosmotic hyperglycemic nonketotic (HHNK) states. Sixty-six patients received treatment for hyperglycemia, this included 62 patients receiving metformin and 11 patients receiving insulin. Hyperglycemia was also managed with dose modifications, including dose reduction in 3% of patients and dose interruption in 28% of patients. Permanent treatment discontinuation was required in 1% of patients (2 patient) due to hyperglycemia.
L1221: Stomatitis occurred in 51% of patients treated with inavolisib, Grade 3 events in 6% of patients. There was no grade 4 or 5 events or SAE associated with stomatitis. Stomatitis led to interruption inavolisib in 10%, to dose reduction in 3.7%, and to discontinuation of inavolisib in 0.6% of patients. For patients treated with inavolisib in combination with palbociclib and
L1222: fulvestrant, 38% of patients used a mouthwash containing corticosteroid for management or prophylaxis of stomatitis.
L1223: Diarrhea occurred in 48% of patients treated with inavolisib, including Grade 3 events in 3.7% of patients. There were no grade 4 or 5 events or cases of colitis on the INAVO120 Trial. There were two SAEs associated with diarrhea, both of which resolved. In addition, there was a case of acute kidney injury associated with diarrhea and cases of dehydration associated with diarrhea. Dose interruptions were required in 7% of patients, and dose reductions occurred in 1.2%. Anti-diarrheal medicines were used in 28% of patients treated with inavolisib to manage symptoms.
L1224: Ocular toxicity occurred in 22% of patients treated with inavolisib compared to 13% on the placebo arm. The most common toxicity was dry eye (9%). There were no grade 3-5 events, no SAEs and ocular toxicity did not lead to dose modifications (discontinuation, reduction or interruption).
L1225: Neutropenia is a known toxicity with treatment of palbociclib and occurred at similar rates in both treatment arms.
L1226: Pneumonitis occurred in 3 patients on inavolisib treatment arm (compared to 0 on the placebo arm). There were no grade 3-4 events or SAEs. One patient had a dose interruption, there were no dose reductions or drug discontinuations.
L1227: The Applicant included hyperglycemia in the Warnings and Precautions section of the proposed USPI. In addition, FDA required inclusion of stomatitis and diarrhea in the Warnings and Precautions section based on the incidence of these events and the occurrence of severe cases for both stomatitis and diarrhea in the INAVO120 trial.

L1243: *The methodology of data analyses has evolved between Studies GO39374 and WO41554 for selected AE categories of “Lymphopenia” and “Anemia”. For Study GO39374, the analyses of these two categories are presented by individual MedDRA Preferred Terms contained within each category, and therefore not presented in the current table as grouped term analyses.
L1244: Source: Adapted from Interim CSR, Study GO39374, Report 1125233, Table 18; t_ae_grp_oview_CSR_SE_27MAR2023_39374; t_ae_pt_CSR_SE_27MAR2023_39374; Dataset: ADAE
L1245: ## 8.2.6. Clinical Outcome Assessment (COA) Analyses Informing Safety/Tolerability
L1246: ## Data and the Applicant's Position:
L1247: Pivotal Study WO41554: Seven AEs deemed most applicable to inavolisib, palbociclib, and fulvestrant based on preliminary safety or published studies (i.e. diarrhea, nausea, vomiting, fatigue, mouth sores, decreased appetite, and rash), were selected from the PRO-CTCAE item library for assessment, along with an additional single item assessing bother due to side-effects of treatment.
L1248: In both arms, worst post-baseline levels of “moderate” / “occasionally” or less were reported by &gt;70% of patients for decreased appetite, nausea, and vomiting, and by &gt;60% of patients for mouth sores, diarrhea and fatigue. Results of bother from treatment side effects showed a similar pattern with &gt;55% patients in both arms reporting no more than moderate levels of bother, suggesting that inavolisib did not contribute increased treatment burden when added to fulvestrant and palbociclib.
L1249: Higher, i.e. worse, ratings of post-baseline severity/frequency (“severe” / “frequently” or “very severe” / “almost constantly”), were reported by a greater proportion of patients in the Inavo+Palbo+Fulv arm than the Pbo+Palbo+Fulv arm, particularly for diarrhea (Inavo+Palbo+Fulv: 32.9% vs. Pbo+Palbo+Fulv: 10.1%), mouth/throat sores (30.2% vs. 8.9%), and decreased appetite (27.0% vs. 12.7%). The proportion of patients reporting post-baseline rash (rated in terms of presence as “Yes” / “No”) was 53.9% vs. 40.5% in the Inavo+Palbo+Fulv and the Pbo+Palbo+Fulv arm, respectively. These higher rates reported by patients in the inavolisib arm mirror the safety data showing higher prevalence of stomatitis, diarrhea, and rash associated with inavolisib. Despite greater proportions of patients in the inavolisib arm reporting higher/worse levels of treatment-related symptoms post-baseline, no additional detriment to their baseline levels of day-to-day functioning or HRQoL was observed while on treatment, further demonstrating the tolerability of the inavolisib regimen.
L1250: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1251: The Applicant collected PRO-CTCAE items as described above. In general, these patient-reported symptoms were observed to be worse in the inavolisib arm, with the greatest difference observed for diarrhea, mouth sores, decreased appetite, and rash. These symptoms are supportive of the clinician reported safety information and were considered informative for labeling.
L1252: The Applicant also collected overall side effect impact using the MBI which prompted patients “I am bothered by side effects of treatment”. At baseline, the proportion of patients with MBI responses of “not at all” were similar between arms. Throughout the trial, patients in the inavolisib with palbociclib and fulvestrant arm reported more side effect bother compared to the placebo with palbociclib and fulvestrant arm. For example, at Cycle 2 Day 15, the
L1253: proportion of patients with MBI responses of “not at all” were 25% in the ITOVEBI with palbociclib and fulvestrant arm and 53% in the placebo with palbociclib and fulvestrant arm. This disparate overall side effect bother was similar across cycles and also supports the safety findings above.
L1254: These PRO data were assessed appropriately with acceptable data quality. The patient-reported data provide additional insight into the safety and tolerability of the approved regimen and were included in section 6 of the USPI to further inform patients and providers.
L1255: ## 8.2.7. Safety Analyses by Demographic Subgroups
L1256: ## Data and the Applicant's Position:
L1257: Pivotal Study WO41554: Given the relatively small number of patients included in the analyses by age, gender, race, and ethnicity, these subgroup comparisons should be interpreted with caution.
L1258: Overall, the incidences of SAEs and AEs leading to dose modification/interruption of any study treatment was numerically higher in elderly patients (65-74 years and \ge 75 years vs. &lt; 65 years age groups) which could be due to higher prevalence of comorbidities in elderly patients and/or reduction in the reserve capacity of the organs in general.
L1259: Consistent with the disease demographics for breast cancer, the vast majority of patients treated with Inavo+Palbo+Fulv were female (n=157) compared with male (n=5). In the male patients, no AEs leading to death or AEs leading to withdrawal of treatment were reported.
L1260: The majority of the 162 patients treated with Inavo+Palbo+Fulv were White (n=94, 58.0%) followed by Asian (n=62, 38.3%), Unknown (n=5, 3.1%), and Black or African-American (n=1, 0.6%). AEs leading to withdrawal from any study treatment or dose modification/interruption of inavolisib were comparable between the White and Asian racial subgroups, and across regions, supporting the manageability and tolerability of inavolisib in combination with palbociclib and fulvestrant in both White and Asian racial subgroups and across geographical regions.
L1261: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1262: incidences of SAEs and AEs leading to dose modification/interruption of any study treatment was numerically higher in elderly patients; however, there were only 11 patients &gt;75 years and 47 patients between 65-74 years of age enrolled on study. In addition, there is limited information regarding different racial/ethnic groups from the INAVO120 Trial (WO41554) as the majority of patients enrolled were White. Overall, only two Black patients and 20 Hispanic/Latino patients were enrolled on INAVO120 trial (WO41554). Due to the lack of diversity in the enrolled population, a Post Marketing Commitment (PMC) is being issued to further characterize the efficacy and safety of inavolisib for the treatment of breast cancer in study populations whose demographics are more representative of the population of U.S.
L1263: patients for the proposed indication.
L1264: ## 8.2.8. Specific Safety Studies/Clinical Trials
L1265: **Data and the Applicant's Position:** The clinical safety data supporting evaluation of the intended indication is primarily derived from the pivotal Phase III Study WO41554, with supportive data derived from the Phase I Study GO39374. No studies were conducted to evaluate a specific safety concern.
L1266: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1267: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment that the safety data supporting the intended indication is primarily from the INAVO120 trial (WO41554). GO39374 was not reviewed in depth by the FDA, as it was not considered to support the intended indication.
L1268: ## 8.2.9. Additional Safety Explorations
L1269: ## Human Carcinogenicity or Tumor Development
L1270: **The Applicant's Position:** No carcinogenicity studies with inavolisib have been conducted. No carcinogenicity studies are currently planned because such studies are not required for drugs intended for advanced cancer patients per ICH-S9 (DHHS 2010).
L1271: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1272: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment.
L1273: ## Human Reproduction and Pregnancy
L1274: **The Applicant's Position:** There were no pregnancies reported in patients treated with inavolisib in either of the two studies. No fetus should be exposed to inavolisib. Because inavolisib will likely cross the placental membrane, inavolisib should not be administered to pregnant women.
L1275: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1276: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment.
L1277: **Pediatrics and Assessment of Effects on Growth**
**Data and the Applicant's Position:** The Applicant received the Agreed Initial Pediatric Study Plan (iPSP) Agreement for Study WO41554 from the Agency on 12 September 2022. Further details are in Module 1 of the dossier.
L1278: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1279: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment.
L1280: ## Overdose, Drug Abuse Potential, Withdrawal, and Rebound
L1281: **Data:** There is limited experience with overdose with inavolisib in clinical trials. In clinical studies, inavolisib was administered at doses up to 12 mg once daily. Accidental overdosage of
L1282: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219249}
L1283: ## {Inavolisib}
L1284: Inavolisib have been reported in clinical studies as an AE. The highest dose administered in Study WO41554 was 18 mg in one patient. The patient erroneously took two 9 mg tablets of inavolisib on one occasion. The event of accidental overdosage was resolved in one day and did not require treatment or lead to dose modification of any study drugs. There were no additional AEs reported due to the accidental overdosage.
L1285: In Study GO39374, 3 patients deviated from assigned study drug dose which resulted in an overdose of inavolisib administration which were reported as major protocol deviations. The highest dose administered in Study GO39374 due to deviation was 20 mg in two patients. All overdoses of inavolisib were for one occasion and associated with no AEs.
L1286: **The Applicant's Position:** Patients who experience overdose should be closely supervised and supportive care instituted. There are no known antidotes for inavolisib.
L1287: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1288: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment.
L1289: ## 8.2.10. Safety in the Post-market Setting
L1290: ## Safety Concerns Identified Through Post-market Experience
L1291: **The Applicant's Position:** Inavolisib has not been approved for use anywhere in the world; therefore, no post-marketing data regarding its use are currently available.
L1292: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1293: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment.
L1294: ## Expectations on Safety in the Post-market Setting
L1295: **The Applicant's Position:** Toxicities have been adequately represented in 351 patients in both Study WO41554 and Study GO39374 for this application and are expected to be similar in the post-market setting. It is expected that safety issues can be adequately managed through labeling, additional risk minimization measures and routine post-marketing surveillance.
L1296: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1297: The safety assessment for inavolisib for this NDA review is primarily based on the 162 patients treated on the inavolisib+palbociclib+fulvestrant treatment arm of the INAVO120 trial. The FDA will continue to monitor post-marketing reports and safety reports submitted after approval.
L1298: ## 8.2.11. Integrated Assessment of Safety
L1299: **The Applicant's Position:** In line with the efficacy benefit described in Section 8.1.4, the safety and tolerability profile of inavolisib in combination with palbociclib and ET as observed in the pivotal Study WO41554 and supportive Study GO39374 appeared to be manageable and acceptable.
L1300: The overall safety profile of inavolisib in combination with palbociclib and fulvestrant in Study WO41554 was comparable with the overall safety population (Arms A-F) and Arms B, E and F of Study GO39374, strengthening the characterization of the inavolisib safety profile across inavolisib-containing treatment regimens including triplet regimens with palbociclib and fulvestrant or with palbociclib and letrozole and supporting the tolerability and AE management of inavolisib. Data from both studies were consistent with the anticipated safety profile of inavolisib and the respective safety profiles of palbociclib, fulvestrant, and letrozole. No new or unexpected safety findings were identified in either of the studies.
L1301: In both Studies WO41554 and GO39374, the proportion of patients who experienced AEs leading to withdrawal of any study drug was overall low indicating manageability of AEs and good tolerability of inavolisib in combination with palbociclib and an ET.
L1302: Hyperglycemia is a reversible on-target toxicity with early onset associated with drugs that target the PI3K-AKT-mTOR pathway. In Study WO41554, the majority of patients who experienced a selected AE of hyperglycemia in the Inavo+Palbo+Fulv arm had Grade 1 or Grade 2 events; all events were non-serious, and manageable with oral anti-hyperglycemic agents and dose reductions (2.5%) or interruptions (27.8%) in all patients including pre-diabetic patients enrolled in the Inavo+Palbo+Fulv arm. Grade 3 hyperglycemia events were reported in a low proportion of patients in the Inavo+Palbo+Fulv arm and no Grade 4 or Grade 5 hyperglycemia events were reported in either arm. Median time to first onset of hyperglycemia was 7 days. The majority of events of hyperglycemia in the Inavo+Palbo+Fulv arm were reversible with only 2 patients (1.2%) discontinuing from the study due to AEs of hyperglycemia and Type 2 diabetes mellitus. The overall incidences of hyperglycemia AEs in the Inavo+Palbo+Fulv arm of Study WO41554 (58.6%) was lower than that seen in the overall safety population (66.8%) or in Arm E or Arm F individually (70.0% each) of Study GO39374 and could be due to differences in NCI-CTCAE grading criteria, more restrictive hyperglycemia inclusion criteria and an improved manageability of hyperglycemia in Study WO41554. No cases of diabetic ketoacidosis or hyperosmotic hyperglycemic nonketotic states were reported across both studies thereby indicating that hyperglycemia was well-managed per the protocol-defined guidance irrespective of underlying risk factors.
L1303: The majority of diarrhea AEs across both studies were Grade 1-2 with no Grade 5 events and a total of 3 SAEs (2 in Study WO41554 and 1 in Study GO39374). Dose reduction, interruption and withdrawal of any study drug due to diarrhea were not frequent and supported the manageability of diarrhea AEs.
L1304: Across both studies, most rash events were Grade 1-2 with one Grade 3 event, no Grade 4 or 5 events and a total of 2 SAEs (both in Study GO39374). No patient discontinued any study drug due to rash across both studies. A low proportion of patients required dose interruptions or dose reductions due to rash AEs.
L1305: The incidence of neutropenia AEs in the Inavo+Palbo+fulv arm of Study WO41554 was consistent with the palbociclib-containing Arms B, E, and F of Study GO39374. Across both studies, the majority of neutropenia events were Grade 3-4 with no Grade 5 events reported. Two SAEs, 1 event in the placebo arm of Study WO41554 and 1 event in Arm B of Study GO39374, were reported. Among common selected AEs in Study WO41554, the incidence and severity of neutropenia were well balanced between the two treatment arms and consistent with the incidence of this AE reported with palbociclib (IBRANCE® USPI); thereby, indicating that inavolisib has minimal impact on the known risk of neutropenia associated with palbociclib.
L1306: Across both studies, most of the stomatitis and mucosal inflammation events were Grade 1-2 with no Grade 5 events. One SAE was reported in Study GO39374. The AEs of stomatitis and mucosal inflammation were manageable with standard clinical measures and dose modifications/interruptions.
L1307: The median dose intensity of triplet combination of Inavo+Palbo+fulv was high across both studies thereby showing that the addition of inavolisib did not compromise the dose intensity of palbociclib and fulvestrant (current standard of care). At the time of CCOD for Study WO41554, the majority of patients in the Inavo+Palbo+fulv arm had been on treatment for longer than 6 months in comparison to the majority of the patients in the Pbo+Palbo+fulv arm being on treatment for less than 6 months.
L1308: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1309: The safety profile for inavolisib in combination with palbociclib and fulvestrant is primarily based on data from the safety population from the INAVO120 trial (WO41554). This includes 162 patients treated with inavolisib+palbociclib+fulvestrant. Data from Study GO39374 was not considered supportive of the intended indication and was not reviewed in depth. The patient population in Study GO39374 was different than the patient population for the granted indication.
L1310: The overall safety profile for the triplet combination was consistent with the anticipated safety profile for inavolisib and the known safety profile for palbociclib and fulvestrant.
L1311: Adverse events known to occur with PI3K inhibitors include hyperglycemia, stomatitis, diarrhea, and rash. Hyperglycemia (increased laboratory glucose) occurred at much higher rates on the inavolisib treatment arm (85%) compared to the placebo arm (43%). Hyperglycemia was managed with dose modifications and implementation of anti-hyperglycemic therapy as needed. Only two patients required permanent discontinuation of inavolisib due to hyperglycemia. Grade 3 increased laboratory blood glucose (FPG &gt; 250 to 500 mg/dL) occurred in 12% of patients, and 0.6% with Grade 4 (FPG &gt; 500 mg/dL) events. There was no grade 5 cases of hyperglycemia or cases of DKA and HHNK. However, patients that enrolled on the trial were required to have fasting blood glucose (FBG) &lt;126 and HBA1c&lt;6%. Therefore, the safety
L1312: of inavolisib in patients with a history of diabetes or higher baseline risk factors (FBG&gt;126 and Hb1c&gt;6%) is not known. Patients treated with inavolisib will need to be closely monitored for hyperglycemia and start treatment as indicated, including anti-glycemic therapy, dietary modifications, adequate hydration, and evaluation by a specialist. Hyperglycemia is labeled in the warnings and precautions section of the USPI.
L1313: Stomatitis occurred at higher rates in the inavolisib treatment arm (51%) compared to the placebo arm (27%), including 6% of patients on the inavolisib arm having grade 3 events. There were no grade 4-5 events or SAEs. Stomatitis was managed with dose modifications and required permanent discontinuation in one patient. For patients treated with inavolisib, 38% used a mouthwash containing corticosteroid for management or prophylaxis of stomatitis. Patients should be monitored for stomatitis and have treatment instituted as indicated. Stomatitis is labeled in the warnings and precautions section of the USPI.
L1314: Diarrhea occurred at higher rates in the inavolisib treatment arm (48%) compared to the placebo arm (16%), including 4% of patients on the inavolisib arm having grade 3 events. There were two SAEs and no grade 4-5 events or colitis. Diarrhea was managed with dose modifications and no patients required treatment discontinuation due to diarrhea. Anti-diarrheal medicines were used in 28% of patients treated with inavolisib to manage symptoms.
L1315: Rash occurred in 26% of patients treated with inavolisib compared to 19% of patients on the placebo arm. There were no grade 3-5 events or SAEs. Few patients needed dose modifications due to rash (3 patients had dose interruption and one patient had dose reduction) and no patients required treatment discontinuation.
L1316: Ocular toxicity occurred in 22% of patients treated with inavolisib compared to 13% on the placebo arm. The most common toxicity was dry eye (9%). There were no grade 3-5 events, no SAEs and ocular toxicity did not lead to dose modifications (discontinuation, reduction, or interruption).
L1317: Hematologic toxicities were seen in both treatment arms and are known toxicities with palbociclib. Neutropenia and anemia were seen in a similar proportion of patients regarding all grade toxicity. However, grade 3-4 toxicity was slightly higher in the inavolisib treatment arm. Thrombocytopenia (including platelet count decreased) was also higher in the inavolisib treatment arm compared to the placebo treatment arm.
L1318: The addition of inavolisib to palbociclib and fulvestrant resulted in increased toxicity. However, the median relative dose intensity of palbociclib and fulvestrant was similar between the two treatment arms of the INAVO120 (WO41554) Trial; therefore, the triplet combination therapy including inavolisib did not have a significant impact on the ability to administer full dose palbociclib+fulvestrant. The most notable increase in adverse events were observed in hyperglycemia, stomatitis, and diarrhea, including severe cases. Therefore, hyperglycemia,
L1319: Stomatitis and diarrhea are labeled under Warnings and Precautions in the USPI to better inform providing physicians and to provide guidance for dose modifications. These adverse events could be managed with dose interruptions and dose reductions in most cases. Other adverse events (including laboratory abnormalities) noted in \ge 20\% of patients treated with inavolisib+palbociclib+fulvestrant include decreased neutrophils, decreased hemoglobin, increased fasting glucose, decreased platelets, decreased lymphocytes, stomatitis, diarrhea, decreased calcium, fatigue, decreased potassium, increased creatinine, increased ALT, nausea, decreased sodium, decreased magnesium, rash, decreased appetite, COVID-19 infection, and headache.
L1320: In addition, the Applicant further explored safety and tolerability through assessment of patient-generated data. A list of expected symptomatic adverse events from the combination of inavolisib, palbociclib, and fulvestrant (diarrhea, nausea, vomiting, fatigue, mouth sores, decreased appetite, and rash), was selected from the patient reported outcome (PRO)-CTCAE item library, along with an additional single item assessing bother due to side-effects of treatment. These PRO data were assessed appropriately with acceptable data quality. Analysis of patient-reported symptoms that worsened from baseline were generally observed to be worse in the inavolisib arm, with the greatest difference observed for diarrhea, mouth sores, decreased appetite, and rash. The patient-reported data provide additional insight into the safety and tolerability of the approved regimen and were included in section 6 of the USPI to further inform patients and providers.
L1321: ## SUMMARY AND CONCLUSIONS
L1322: ## 8.3. Statistical Issues
L1323: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1324: There were no major statistical issues with this application. The INAVO120 study met its primary endpoint of INV-assessed PFS, showing a statistically significant improvement in PFS with the treatment of inavolisib + palbociclib + fulvestrant. The results were generally consistent across sensitivity analyses and subgroup analyses, except for Region, where the difference in survival between arms is diminished in the North America/Western Europe region. However, subgroup analyses should be interpreted with caution due to small sample sizes in each subgroup. The interim OS analysis at the time of PFS analysis with 63% information fraction was not statistically significant. Interim OS results showed a HR of 0.64 (95% CI: 0.43, 0.97) with median OS not estimable (95% CI: 27.3, NE) on the inavolisib + palbociclib + fulvestrant arm and 31.1 months (95% CI: 22.3, NE) on the placebo + palbociclib + fulvestrant arm. Thus, there appears to be no detriment in OS at this time. The final analysis of OS is expected to occur in the second quarter of 2025.
L1325: ## 8.3.1. Conclusions and Recommendations
L1326: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1327: INAVO120, a randomized, placebo-controlled, double-blind, multicenter trial in 325 patients with endocrine resistant, PIK3CA mutated, HR-positive, HER2-negative (defined as IHC 0 or 1+, or IHC 2+/ISH-), advanced or metastatic breast cancer, following recurrence on or after completing adjuvant endocrine therapy and who have not received prior systemic therapy for locally advanced or metastatic disease, demonstrated statistically significant and clinically meaningful improvement in PFS by investigator which was supported by an interim OS that showed no detriment. Inavolisib is associated with increased hyperglycemia, stomatitis, and diarrhea compared to placebo and are included as Warnings and Precautions, as these were considered severe, and occurred in a significant proportion of patients as noted in labeling.
L1328: The review team recommends granting regular approval to inavolisib + palbociclib + fulvestrant for the following indication:
L1329: *Inavolisib (ITOVEBI), in combination with palbociclib and fulvestrant, is indicated for the treatment of adults with endocrine-resistant, PIK3CA-mutated, hormone receptor (HR)-positive, human epidermal growth factor receptor 2 (HER2)-negative, locally advanced or metastatic breast cancer, as detected by an FDA-approved test, following recurrence on or after completing adjuvant endocrine therapy.*

L1342: ## 10 Pediatrics
L1343: **The Applicant's Position:** Not applicable as the applicant has not proposed any pediatric sections in the proposed labeling (Agreed iPSP; Reference ID 5044201).
L1344: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1345: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment. No pediatric patients were included in INAVO120 (WO41554), which was the primary study used to evaluate efficacy in this NDA. FDA issued an Agreed Initial Pediatric Study Plan (iPSP) Agreement for a full waiver for the indication to IND 130909 on September 12, 2022.
L1346: FDA waived the pediatric study requirement for this application because necessary studies are impossible or highly impracticable. This is due to the rarity of patients with breast cancer and pediatric cancers with PI3KCA mutations. Across pediatric cancers, PIK3CA mutations are far less common in pediatric tumors and are primarily limited to central nervous system (CNS) tumors. Inavolisib is not expected to be effective in CNS tumors, because inavolisib is a substrate of P-glycoprotein and breast cancer resistance protein which limits accumulation and activity within the CNS.

L1368: ## 12 Risk Evaluation and Mitigation Strategies (REMS)
L1369: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**

L1371: ## 13 Postmarketing Requirements and Commitment
L1372: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1373: The following postmarketing requirements (PMR) and postmarketing commitments (PMC) were issued by FDA and agreed to by the Applicant:
L1374: ## PMRs:
L1375: - **PMR 4718-1:** Conduct a multicenter, randomized clinical trial to further characterize known serious risks with inavolisib including severe hyperglycemia, stomatitis/mucosal inflammation, and diarrhea, and compare the safety and activity of inavolisib 9 mg daily versus a lower daily dose in patients with *PIK3CA*-mutant, hormone receptor (HR)-positive, HER2-negative locally advanced or metastatic breast cancer.
L1376: Draft Protocol Submission:	04/2025
Final Protocol Submission:	10/2025
Trial Completion:	10/2028
Final Report Submission:	04/2029
L1377: **FDA Rationale:** Conducting additional inavolisib dosage optimization is needed to identify whether alternative dosing would reduce the rate of the known serious risk of increased severe adverse events including increased blood glucose, stomatitis/mucosal inflammation, and diarrhea in the target patient population. The Applicant has not provided sufficient justification and supportive data that inavolisib 9 mg PO QD is the optimized dosage for the target patient population in combination with palbociclib and fulvestrant. There are extremely limited clinical data at lower inavolisib dosages with only 20 patients treated at dosages lower than the proposed 9 mg QD dosage across the entire development program of inavolisib. For the proposed inavolisib combination therapy with palbociclib and fulvestrant, all patients were treated at the proposed 9 mg QD inavolisib dosage. At the proposed 9 mg QD inavolisib dosage in combination with palbociclib and fulvestrant, multiple serious ARs in the pivotal study (i.e., severe cases of increased blood glucose, stomatitis/mucosal inflammation, and diarrhea) occurred at high rates. Toxicities led to dosage modification in 69% of patients treated with the proposed recommended dose regimen. Finally, while there were no apparent exposure-response (E-R) relationships for efficacy endpoints, there were multiple positive E-R relationships for safety endpoints including Grade \ge 2 anemia, Grade \ge 2 hyperglycemia, Grade \ge 3 adverse reactions, and inavolisib dosage modifications due to adverse reactions. Given the limited dose finding, high rates of serious ARs, poor tolerability, and positive E-R relationships for multiple safety endpoints, the proposed inavolisib dosage of 9 mg PO QD is not adequately supported as the optimized dosage. Therefore, the Applicant should conduct additional dosage optimization to identify whether an alternative inavolisib dosage would reduce the risk of serious toxicities.
L1378: - **PMR 4718-2:** Complete the ongoing renal impairment clinical trial and evaluate the pharmacokinetics and safety of inavolisib in participants with normal renal function and patients with severe renal impairment, to evaluate the serious potential risk of increased drug exposure.
L1379: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219249}
L1380: ## {Inavolisib}
L1381: Design and conduct the trial in accordance with the FDA Guidance for Industry titled "Pharmacokinetics in Patients with Impaired Renal Function: Study Design, Data Analysis, and Impact on Dosing and Labeling."
L1382: Final Protocol Submission:	08/2023 (completed)
Trial Completion:	12/2024
Final Report Submission:	06/2025
L1383: Submit the datasets with the final report submission.
L1384: **FDA Rationale:** The final report and datasets from the ongoing renal impairment study are needed to evaluate the pharmacokinetics and safety of inavolisib in participants with severe renal impairment and to support dosage recommendations in this population based on the known serious risk of increased inavolisib exposure in patients with renal impairment. Inavolisib is cleared in part via renal excretion. The Applicant provided data to support dosage recommendations for patients with mild and moderate renal impairment but did not provide sufficient data to support dosage recommendations for patients with severe renal impairment. Patients with moderate renal impairment had ~60% higher inavolisib exposure compared to patients with normal renal function. Given the increased exposure and multiple positive exposure-response relationships for safety, a reduced starting dosage is needed in patients with moderate renal impairment to mitigate the risk of increased serious toxicities including increase blood glucose, stomatitis/mucosal inflammation, diarrhea, and Grade \ge 3 adverse events. Increased exposure of inavolisib and the need for a reduced starting dosage are expected in patients with severe renal impairment given the known renal excretion and findings in patients with moderate renal impairment. A dedicated renal impairment study including subjects with severe renal impairment is currently ongoing, but results are not expected until 2025 per discussion with the Applicant.
L1385: 3. **PMR 4718-3:** Conduct a hepatic impairment clinical trial to evaluate the serious potential risk of increased drug exposure and determine a safe and appropriate dose of inavolisib in patients with moderate and severe hepatic impairment. Design and conduct the trial in accordance with the FDA Guidance for Industry entitled "Pharmacokinetics in Patients with Impaired Hepatic Function: Study Design, Data Analysis, and Impact on Dosing and Labeling."
L1386: Draft Protocol Submission:	03/2025
Final Protocol Submission:	07/2025
Trial Completion:	07/2028
Final Report Submission:	01/2029
L1387: Submit the datasets with the final report submission.
L1388: **FDA Rationale:** A clinical hepatic impairment study is needed to evaluate the pharmacokinetics and safety of inavolisib in participants with moderate and severe hepatic impairment and to support dosage recommendations in this population. This study is intended to assess the serious potential risk of increased inavolisib exposure as described below. Per the Mass Balance and Absolute Bioavailability Study GP42652, the absolute oral bioavailability of inavolisib is 76%.
L1389: *Circulating metabolites were minimal with the majority (99.5% of drug-related material in plasma) being parent inavolisib in the human mass balance study. A mean of 48% of the dose (40% as the parent drug and 8% as metabolites) was recovered in urine, and 48% (10% as the parent drug and 38% as metabolites) was recovered in feces. The amount of drug recovered as metabolites in feces and urine accounts for approximately 46% of the administered dose. Therefore, a &gt;20% contribution of hepatic elimination cannot be ruled out and patients with hepatic impairment may have increased exposure to inavolisib. The Applicant provided data to support dosage recommendations for patients with mild hepatic impairment but did not provide sufficient data to support dosage recommendations for patients with moderate or severe renal impairment. If patients with moderate or severe hepatic impairment have increased inavolisib exposure, a reduced starting dosage may be needed to mitigate the serious risk of increased severe toxicity including hyperglycemia, stomatitis/mucosal inflammation, diarrhea, and other severe adverse reactions.*
L1390: ## PMCs
L1391: 1. **PMC 4718-4:** Conduct a clinical pharmacokinetic trial to evaluate the effect of repeat doses of inavolisib on the single dose pharmacokinetics of a sensitive CYP3A substrate to inform appropriate management strategies for clinically relevant drug interactions. Design and conduct the trial in accordance with the FDA Guidance for Industry entitled “Clinical Drug Interaction Studies — Cytochrome P450 Enzyme- and Transporter-Mediated Drug Interactions.”
L1392: Draft Protocol Submission:	04/2025
Final Protocol Submission:	08/2025
Trial Completion:	02/2027
Final Report Submission:	08/2027
L1393: *FDA Rationale: In an in vitro study (16-0131), inavolisib increased CYP3A mRNA up to 40-fold. Based on FDA assessment, available data cannot rule out potential induction effect of inavolisib on sensitive CYP3A substrates. Induction of CYP3A by inavolisib may result in reduced exposure of sensitive CYP3A substrates which could reduce effectiveness of concomitantly administered CYP3A substrates.*
L1394: *The Applicant did not evaluate the induction potential of inavolisib on CYP2C isoforms. If the induction potential of inavolisib on CYP3A is confirmed, an additional study to evaluate the induction potential of inavolisib on CYP2Cs in an in vitro setting and/or in a clinical trial may be needed.*
L1395: *In addition, if inavolisib is at least a weak inducer of CYP3A in vivo, there is the potential for drug-drug interaction between inavolisib and combined oral contraceptives (COC), which are known substrates of CYP3A, which could reduce the effectiveness of the COC and increase the risk of unintended pregnancy. If the induction potential of inavolisib on CYP3A is confirmed, an additional study to evaluate the DDI potential between inavolisib and COC in a clinical trial may be needed.*

L1397: 2. **PMC 4718-5:** Conduct a clinical pharmacokinetic trial to evaluate the effect of repeat doses of inavolisib on the single dose pharmacokinetics of a CYP2B6 substrate to inform appropriate management strategies for clinically relevant drug interactions. Design and conduct the trial in accordance with the FDA Guidance for Industry entitled “Clinical Drug Interaction Studies — Cytochrome P450 Enzyme- and Transporter-Mediated Drug Interactions.”

L1400: 3. **PMC 4718-6:** Complete the ongoing clinical trial, INAVO120 (Study WO41554), titled “A Phase III, Randomized, Double-Blind, Placebo-Controlled Study Evaluating the Efficacy and Safety of Inavolisib plus Palbociclib and Fulvestrant versus Placebo plus Palbociclib and Fulvestrant in Patients with PIK3CA-Mutant, Hormone Receptor-Positive, HER2- Negative Locally Advanced or Metastatic Breast Cancer”, to provide the final overall survival (OS) analysis.”

It is important to review the final OS results for this study to confirm there is continued clinical benefit with use of inavolisib for the treatment of patients with PIK3CA-mutant, hormone receptor-positive, HER2- negative locally advanced or metastatic breast cancer.*
L1404: 4. **PMC 4718-7:** Conduct an integrated analysis containing data from clinical trials and post-marketing reports, observational studies (e.g., real-world evidence), and other sources to further characterize the pharmacokinetics (PK), pharmacodynamics (PD), safety and efficacy of inavolisib in racial and ethnic minority patients and older patients age &gt;65 years with PIK3CA-mutant, hormone receptor (HR)-positive, HER2-negative locally advanced or metastatic breast cancer. The analyses should support comparative safety and efficacy outcome analyses and characterization of potential differences in PK and PD between racial and ethnic minority patients and White patients, and older and younger patients.

L1407: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 219249}
L1408: {Inavolisib}
L1409: this application was enrolled in the U.S. In addition, racial and ethnic minorities reflective of the U.S. population of patients with breast cancer, particularly Black/African American and Hispanic/Latino patients, were significantly underrepresented in the efficacy and safety population that supported this application, with only 2 Black/African American patients and 20 Latino/Hispanic patient enrolled. Therefore, it is important to evaluate data further characterizing the efficacy and safety of inavolisib for the treatment of breast cancer in study populations whose demographics are more representative of the population of U.S. patients for the proposed indication.
L1410: 5. **PMC 4718-8:** Conduct an appropriate analytical and clinical validation study using the INAVO120 clinical trial or other data adequate to support the development of an in vitro diagnostic device for tumor tissue that is essential to the safe and effective use of inavolisib for treatment of patients with PIK3CA-mutant, hormone receptor (HR)-positive, HER2-negative locally advanced or metastatic breast cancer.
L1411: Final Report Submission:
L1412: 12/2024
L1413: **FDA Rationale:** The clinical validity of F1LCDx was demonstrated by assessing clinical efficacy in the F1LCDx PIK3CA mutation-positive population from the INAVO120 clinical trial based on PFS as the primary efficacy endpoint. However, F1LCDx is a plasma-based test and it is known that identifying the PIK3CA mutation status with a plasma based test may potentially result in a high rate of false-negative results given the low shedding of ctDNA into circulation. A false-negative result could result in delayed treatment or deprive a patient from a potentially beneficial treatment with inavolisib. A companion diagnostic device that can detect PIK3CA mutation in tumor tissue from the intent-to-treat population is needed for the safe and effective use of inavolisib for treatment of patients with PIK3CA-mutant, hormone receptor-positive, HER2-negative locally advanced or metastatic breast cancer.

L1503: ## 19.4.1. Bioanalytical Methods
L1504: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1505: FDA finds the Applicant's bioanalytical method validation and performance acceptable to determine the concentration of inavolisib, fulvestrant, palbociclib, and letrozole.
L1506: The Applicant submitted the following method validation and cross-validation reports:
L1507: - Validation of a Method for the Determination of GDC-0011 in Human Plasma by HPLC with MS/MS Detection; report No. 21-0686
- Validation of a Method for the Determination of Fulvestrant in Human Plasma by HPLC with MS/MS Detection; report No. 11-0261
